`from`/`to` are parent-relative, so measuring the top-level elements alone reads one half of a two-half
piece as the whole of it — which is why `RolledStockStagesTests` could only ever compare stages to each
other. It measures composed extents against the form's declared `BaseLength` now, and all 27 shipped stage
shapes land on the model exactly.

Mutation-checked: moving a crop row to 1.75 and dropping a blade tier each fail their guard; so do a wrong
spread exponent (`castslab` 0.3219 → 0.4) and a wrong declared length (`ShingledBar` 18 → 16), the second
of which the old relative check could not have seen.

Touched: `CastStockForms.cs` (new, siex), `SteelIndustryExpandedModSystem.cs`,
`CastStockItemDefinitions.cs`, `StockItemDefinitions.cs`, `RollSetItemDefinitions.cs`,
`HeatingHearthLayout.cs`, `ShapeExtents.cs` (new), `CastStockFormsTests.cs` +
`ShippedCastCropTableTests.cs` (new, siex), `RolledStockStagesTests.cs`, `FurnacePartsTests.cs`, three
siex ladders, `assets/siex/config/processjobs/shear.json`, two goldens, and the six docs that own any of
it.

## 2026-08-14 - Stock stage art: one shape fixed, a generator bug found, 27 stages regenerated

**The cast forms looked art-blocked and were not.** A first pass measured all three cast shapes as wrong
and reported them as lane groups needing a redraw. That was my error: **a child's `from`/`to` are relative
to its parent's `from`**, and read as absolute the billet's two-halves-end-to-end drawing reads as three
lanes side by side. Composed properly, `castbloom` was already **4 × 4 × 25** and `castslab` already
**12 × 4 × 25** — only `castbillet` was short, **3 × 3 × 24** against the settled 27. Its parent half went
12 → 15, which is also the bloom/slab idiom. Gate is 9 targets / **4,111**.

⛔⛔ **`generate-rolled-stock.py` had the same bug, and it had been latent for as long as every base was a
single childless cuboid.** `scale_element` scaled a child's parent-relative offsets about an **absolute**
centre, so a regenerated slab came out **10.89 wide where 9.65 was intended**. Now each element is lifted
into absolute space, scaled, and re-relativised against the parent's scaled `from`; `centre_x` had the same
fault. ★★ **Proved against the shipped art**: all five `shingledslab` stages reproduce exactly. Compare
**geometry, not bytes** — texture keys, element names and UVs all move innocently when a base is redrawn.

⛔ **Its `FORMS` paths were stale and failed silently.** Both shingled bases had moved into `items/smithed/`
and become **family files** (nine stages of two routes in one file), so the script had been unrunnable
while looking runnable. `FORMS` now names the element that IS the piece, a missing base or element is a
loud `SystemExit`, and it takes form names plus `--out` so a change can be checked without clobbering
shipped art.

★ **A form is now drawn at its own base thickness plus every gap below it.** The cast bloom and slab leave
the long cell at **4**, above the shared `STAGES` top of 3, so without this their base stage had no shape.

★★ **The shipped shingled stages were 2 voxels short, and every guard passed.**
`A_stage_shape_is_as_long_as_conserving_its_volume_demands` measures each stage against **the base stage's
own drawn length**, not against `StockForm.BaseLength` — a relative check cannot see a whole family sitting
short. Regenerated from the remade bases (the owner pointed at them); `ShingledBar1` is 3 × 3 × 18 now,
matching the 18 the form always declared.

★ **17 cast stage shapes ship**, and every generated width and length lands exactly on rolled-parts.md's
crop table — billet 4.5 × 2 × 27 and 9 × 1 × 27, bloom 8 × 2 × 25 and 8 × 1 × 50, slab 15 × 2 × 40 and
15 × 1 × 80. That is a strong check on the exponents (1.0 / 1.0 / 0.3219) and max widths (9 / 8 / 15).

★ **One stale gotcha struck**: stock.md said the billet's 2.25 half-step *"`RollSetSpec` cannot even
declare as an output"*. True when outputs lived on the roll set; the crop table is `ProcessJob` now and
2.25 is an ordinary `stage`.

⛔ **Not done**: registering the three `StockForm`s, their ladders, roll-set `accepts` and the five crop
rows. That is now ordinary code with no art in the way.

Touched: `item-sandcast-castbillet.json`, `generate-rolled-stock.py`, 17 new + 10 regenerated stage shapes,
`assets/iiex/shapes/item/castbillet.json`, `stock.md`, `rolled-parts.md`, `NEXT.md`.

## 2026-08-14 - B3c closed: the forming line makes a product

**A shingled bar now becomes 4 rods, 2 beams or 2 plates; a shingled slab becomes 2 boiler plates.**
`RolledItemDefinitions` ships all six items rolled-parts.md owns — `rod` 25 u, `nailplate` 100, `beam` 200,
`blank` 200, `skelp` 200, `boilerplate` 600 — each mass the density rule on its drawn section, declared as
a `const` with the section in its comment. Three drawn shapes exported (`rivetrod`, `nailplate`, `beam`);
the other three take the placeholder the roll sets already use. The crop table ships at
`assets/iiex/config/processjobs/shear.json`. Gate is 9 targets / **4,043**.

⛔⛔ **What is declared is wider than what is reachable, and the middle blocker was a surprise.** Three
declared routes do not work, each for its own reason, and all three are now written down rather than left
to be rediscovered:

- **The rod fork** needs `iiex:stock-rod` — a `Rod` `StockForm` plus the mill admitting `game:rod-iron` at
  its deck.
- ⛔ **Cast stock cannot enter the mill at all.** `StockForm` registers exactly **two** forms,
  `shingledbar` and `shingledslab`, and `config/stageladders/` holds exactly two ladders. So every
  `castbillet` / `castbloom` / `castslab` crop row in the design — five of the thirteen — has no route to
  its input, whatever the shear declares. This was not recorded anywhere before.
- **`heavyplate` waits on M.8**, which merges it with `castplate-heavy` onto one metal axis. Shipping a
  rolled-only one now is exactly the duplication that stage exists to remove — and it would leave M.8
  migrating two items instead of one.

⛔⛔ **Deferring `heavyplate` has a consequence worth stating plainly: the cast-slab route is not walkable.**
Its 2.0 crop is recoverability's **mandatory** one, so with no product to claim there a cast slab rolled on
to 1.0 lands at 80 long against the 48 limit. It needs cast forms *and* M.8 before it closes.

★ **Two vanilla items stay vanilla's**, which is the settled rod-fork ruling and worth not re-litigating:
the re-rollable rod is `game:rod-{metal}` and the plate is `game:metalplate-{metal}`. Both are exactly the
section the design asks for, and 30-odd call sites already name them — minting ours would split every
consumer in two.

★ **`ShippedCropTableTests` is the check the dangling roll-set output codes never had.** Four tests: the
table parses and has the rows it claims; every crop names a **rung its roller family actually walks**
(mutation-checked — moving one row to 1.75 names it exactly); every output resolves to a real item; and no
crop asks more drive than the mill does, since a line that can roll the stock must be able to cut it.

★ The beam's shape file is a **family file** — it carries the mill's flattened stages and another product
beside the beam — so the item renders `selectiveElements: ["Beam"]` rather than the whole file.

Touched: `RolledItemDefinitions.cs`, `ShippedCropTableTests.cs`, `assets/iiex/config/processjobs/shear.json`,
three exported shapes, three lang files, seven goldens, `rolled-parts.md`, `STATE.md` (B3c struck), `NEXT.md`.

## 2026-08-14 - The shear is built: the forming line's last station stands

**The owner supplied the filler layouts and it unblocked the same day.**
`docs/internal/workbench/machines.txt` covers all ten mpenergy megablocks and the three pipe machines —
the thing shear.md recorded as *"blocked on the owner, not to be invented, a wrong legend fails silently"*.
Its *Mp cutter* entry confirms the 3 × 1 × 2 measured off the art. Gate is 9 targets / **4,019**.

Shipped: `BlockShear`, `BlockEntityShear`, the runtime shape, `shearblade-{iron,steel}`, two grid recipes,
two cost-catalogue keys, three locales, 11 station tests. `ShearFeed`/`ShearDecision` and `WorkPiece.Cropped`
already existed, so the decision and the tally needed nothing.

★★ **Two framework pieces had to land first, and both serve the nine machines still to come.**

- **Slab footprint cells.** `StructureFillers` already read a per-cell `collisionBox` at runtime, but
  nothing could *author* one — `FillerCellSpec` had no box and the layout builder had no verb. Now
  `FillerSlab.Half(face)` + `FillerLayoutBuilder.Slab('_', BlockFacing.DOWN)`, with the box serialized as
  the two corners in vanilla's lowercase spelling. ⛔ Serializing the `Cuboidf` itself writes its twenty-odd
  computed properties (`Width`, `MidX`, `Center`, …) — 25 lines of derived junk per cell, all of it landing
  in a golden. Six of the ten machines in machines.txt use slabs.
- **`MachineTool` in exlib** — the tiered-consumable contract, `{ schema, tier }` under a `machinetool`
  attribute. ⛔ It is deliberately **not** an `ItemDie`: a die names the job, so a bench without one has no
  work; a tool names only hardness and the jobs come from the declared table. The machining line's
  universal cutter is the next consumer.

★★ **The drive-torque question closed without any of its three proposals.** shear.md offered a new
`DriveTorque` state field, a `StoredEnergy` proxy, or attempting the stroke and letting a stall answer.
None was needed: `MpEnergyNetworkState` already writes `SupplyPower = driveTorque * Speed` every tick, so
torque comes back exactly as **`SupplyPower / Speed`**. No new field, and `NotEnoughDrive` stays a verdict
that can actually fire. The division is always defined because a stopped run is refused as `NotTurning`
first. Pinned by halving the supply at the same speed and watching the same stroke be refused — which a
direct power comparison could not tell apart.

⛔⛔ **Shipping the shape tripped two animation guards it had never met.** The editable had been drawn for
months but never converted, and `LoopingAnimationTests` runs over *shipped* shapes only: `CutterMass` was
keyframed at frame 0 but not 59, so the guillotine drifted back across the wrap instead of returning
through the drawn motion; and `Shaft` (720°) and `Shaft2` (−360°) unwound a whole turn backwards for want
of `rotShortestDistanceZ`. Both fixed in `convert-shape.py` rather than by hand, for the reason it already
rewrites `onAnimationEnd`: Blockbench cannot express either, so every editable lacks them and every future
conversion needs the same two rules.

★ **The footprint test reads the definition, not a placed block.** `TestBlocks.Configure` builds a bare
`Block` with no attributes, so asserting against a placed one reports an empty footprint and passes by
having nothing to check — the stub-hides-production trap, caught by writing the premise as an assertion.

⛔ **Deliberately not done**: the handbook page. The mill has none either, and the forming shop wants one
page covering both stations rather than half a page each. And the crop table stays empty — that is B3c,
now the *whole* of what stands between the forming line and a finished product.

★ **Also ruled (owner, this session): `flatwide` gets manual gap control.** Its top roller is movable and
the player sets the gap by holding RMB, so one wide set covers every wide gap instead of one item per gap.
`flat` and `grooved` stay locked and friction-walked, and friction still bounds the wide set. **Not built**
— it needs the two `i1` cells of the mill's new layout, which the built mill lacks. Recorded in
rolling-mill.md and NEXT.md so the amendment is not lost.

⛔ **Three of machines.txt's layouts describe blocks that already exist and differ from them** — the
rolling mill (decks become slabs, two new interaction cells), the twin-tub blower (its far end becomes a
real pipe port rather than a cell the blast main abuts) and the fluid pump. None is in `ReleasedCodes`, so

## 2026-08-14 - M.6: codes are now resolved, and the Bessemer vessel can be raised again

**A code a definition points at was checked in no direction, in any suite.** M.6's remaining half closes
that. `ReferencedCodes` (exlib.Testing) collects every code the three mods *reference* rather than
register — recipe outputs and ingredients across all three recipe shapes, RCC `requireStacks`, and every
stack a blocktype or itemtype names in its own body — and `CrossModReferenceTests` (siex suite, the only
one that sees all three mods) resolves each against their union catalogue. **928 references, 530 of them
into a mod domain.** Gate is 9 targets / **3,994**.

⛔⛔ **It printed B23 on its first run, before the guard had any content written for it.** Stage 3 of the
Bessemer vessel required `iiex:pipe-straight-ns-{metal}` — a `{metal}` group that never existed, on a code
missing the `tier` segment M.4 introduced. `ExConstruction` resolves every ingredient *before* the
creative-instant shortcut and hard-fails a non-wildcard miss, so the vessel could not be raised in
survival **or** creative. It is now `iiex:pipe-cast-straight*`, the trailing-star wildcard its own three
gas-intake recipes already use, with `siex:rcc-ingredient-pipe` for the missing-stack hint (a wildcard
require has no resolved stack to take a name from) in all three locales.

★ **Not one orientation.** An exact `-ns` would refuse a player holding the same segment placed the other
way round, and a wildcard also downgrades any future miss from a hard failure to a soft one.

⛔ **B19 is untouched, so the vessel is creative-only.** Enumerating every recipe output across the three
mods sharpened it: the cast tier's *fittings* — passthrough, passthroughbend, valve, pressure valve — are
all craftable, and it is the four **plain segments** that have none. The tier reads half-built rather than
absent. The rolled tier (B5) has no recipe at all, re-confirmed the same way.

★ **The second find generalises further than the first.** Five slag blocks dropped `slag-path-free`,
`slag-brickslab-down-free` and kin with **no domain**. An unqualified code parses as `game:`, so each named
a vanilla block that does not exist. Whether the loader would have qualified them is not worth depending
on — a mod definition should say which registry it means. All five now carry `iiex:`.

★ **Stacks are found by shape, not by key**: any object with a `code` string beside a `type` of
`item`/`block`, which is what makes drops, `smeltedStack`, `groundStack`, `shatteredStack` and mold outputs
fall out for free — and what keeps a *newly added* attribute covered. Keying on attribute names would stop
covering one the moment it appeared, which is the exact failure the check exists to catch. It cost nothing
to be general: `variantgroups` and filler `behaviors` also carry a `code` and neither has that `type`.

⛔ **Two exclusions, both decisions rather than gaps, and both stated in the tests.** *Vanilla codes* — 398
of the 928 — need a per-version manifest off `.game/<slug>/assets`, because we ship 1.20/1.21/1.22 and a
code added in 1.21 is a real defect for 1.20; that is its own unit of work. And a bare *wildcard* is not
reported, because the metal families emit `metalplate-*`, `rod-*` and `metalnailsandstrips-*` into the
mods' own domains alongside vanilla's, so an unqualified one is a net over both registries.

★ **Three guards on the guard**, because a collector that goes quiet is indistinguishable from a clean
run: every `Origin` must yield at least one in-domain reference (mutation-checked — dropping
`InDefinitions` names both starved origins), the in-domain count must stay above 450 of ~530, and it must
*not* be all of them. The count floor started at 150 and was ratcheted after measuring: 150 would have
passed while three quarters of the corpus went missing.

★ **`test/Integration.Tests` declined.** Both premises had expired — the directory was deleted as an
orphan earlier the same day, and the siex suite is already the only one seeing all three mods. A fourth
project would add a build target and a suite-list row to hold tests that are correctly placed today.

Touched: `test/ExpandedLib.Testing/ReferencedCodes.cs` (new),
`test/SteelIndustryExpanded.Tests/Definitions/CrossModReferenceTests.cs` (new),
`BlockConverterBessemer.cs`, `SlagBrickDefinitions.cs`, `SlagPathDefinitions.cs`, six goldens, three lang
