## 2026-08-14 - M.5 built: smex + hpex are `siex` 0.9.9, and the mod set is final

**The second and last merge.** `{smex + hpex}` → **Steel Industry Expanded (`siex`)** - one assembly
(`siex.dll`), one domain, one ModSystem, one config section, one test suite, `assets/siex/`,
`docs/siex/`, handbook renumbered 00-05 with the high-pressure page last. `src/HighPressureExpanded`,
`assets/hpex`, `docs/hpex` and `test/HighPressureExpanded.Tests` are gone. The gate is now
**9 targets / 3,990 tests** (was 12 / 4,000), green across 1.20/1.21/1.22. M1 is built; the mod set is
`exlib`, `iiex`, `siex` and closed.

**Coverage was proved by diffing distinct test-method names, not counts: 219 → 219, none removed.**
Seven `Hpex*Tests` classes in `Definitions/` were the same check parameterised by domain and assembly,
so with one domain they collapse onto their `Siex*` twin - verified method-name-identical pairwise
before deleting either. The two `MegablockDropTier` suites pin different blocks and both survive.

⛔⛔ **The plan's migration table was incomplete, and the guard is the only reason we know.** M.5e listed
the four existing migrations to retarget and never mentioned the 38 released `smex:` codes whose blocks
*stayed* in this mod - the converter family, the cowper heat sink, the air blower, both hoppers, the
smoke stack. They looked covered because `ReleasedCodeCoverageTests.Domains` still carried a stale
`("smex", …)` row, and `DefinitionCodes.ForDomain` **injects** the domain rather than filtering by it,
so smex was being synthesised as a live domain and every `smex:` code trivially reached "itself". This
is trap 3 from the M.4 record, caught in the act. Deleting the row named all 38;
`SmexToSiexMigration` (nine shared bases, variants read off the live registry) answers them, and
carries the saved block-entity tree so a migrated converter keeps its heat and charge.

⛔ **"M.5 owes exactly one block-entity alias" was wrong - it owes six.** The plan reasoned that only the
smoke stack backed migrating blocks, the rest being recorded debt. `ReleasedEntityClassTests` named the
five it missed (converter bessemer/control/transmission, air blower, heat sink). Both guards were
mutation-checked in the failing direction afterwards.

⛔⛔ **A config section is keyed by mod id, so the rename silently discarded every player's tuning.**
Nothing had listed this: `LegacyFileNames` folds a legacy *file* into a section, which is a different
move, and a missing section is indistinguishable from a fresh install - no error, no log line, every
value quietly back on its coded default. smex shipped 0.9.8, so this was live data. Fixed in exlib with
`LegacySectionIds` / `ExConfigDocument.FoldLegacySections`, plumbed through the attribute, the register
and the generator; four tests, mutation-checked. A later legacy section fills only the gaps, so
absorbing two mods keeps both halves with the survivor authoritative.

★ **That fix made one of the plan's own instructions obsolete.** M.5d said to drop the pre-merge config
migration rows as unreachable, siex starting at 0.9.9. But a carried section brings its own
`ConfigVersion`, so a player arriving from smex 0.9.3 still crosses the 0.9.5 row. They were kept and
the reasoning written into the doc comment.

★ **Two typed overloads replaced six hardcoded strings.** The definitions named their block entities as
`.EntityClass("smex.BlockEntityX")` literals - which after the domain flip would have declared a class
nothing registers, reproducing the data-loss bug the previous entry fixed. They now use
`.EntityClass<T>()` / `.EntityBehavior<T>()`, which compose the key from the assembly's own domain and
cannot drift again.

**Also fixed, found in passing:**

- ⛔ **The coverage gate has not gated iiex since M.4.** `FLOORS` is keyed by *assembly* name, and M.4's
  blanket rename left `"IronIndustryExpanded"` there while the csproj set `<AssemblyName>iiex</...>`.
  A key matching no package is silently ungated **and** dropped from TOTAL - the file's own note warns
  about exactly this. Keys are now `exlib`/`iiex`/`siex` (verified against a real cobertura report, not
  assumed), and an unmatched key now fails the run instead of passing quietly - mutation-checked by
  putting the folder name back. With the gate actually gating, the floors were re-ratcheted off a
  measured full-solution run: exlib 33 → **68** (73.6 actual), iiex → **55** (60.3), siex → **60**
  (66.8), TOTAL 35 → **60** (65.4). They had drifted roughly 30 points below the truth.
- M.4 rename artefacts: a duplicate `IronIndustryExpanded` key in the same `FLOORS` dict (the first was
  dead), `IronIndustryExpanded.Tests` listed twice in the legacy CI matrix, and duplicate `iiex=`
  staging pairs in three `tasks.json` tasks.
- Four orphaned `command-steel-*` lang keys across all three locales, naming a command replaced by
  exlib's generic `/exmod recipes <mod>`. The same sweep M.4 ran for `command-steam-*`.
- `game:tabname-*` collided as it did in M.4; the merged tab is named for the merged mod
  ("Steel Industry" / "Стальная промышленность" / "Сталева промисловість"), not either half.
- A stale `docs/design/hpex.md` citation, a path that has never existed.

**Lang merged by JSON key union**, never a directory move: 174 + 25 → **194** keys per locale, the
arithmetic accounted for exactly (4 shared keys with identical values, 1 tab collision), then 4 orphans
removed → 190. Staged-output check passed: exactly one ModSystem-bearing dll per published mod folder.

**Docs.** The unambiguous half was swept across 45 files - paths and type names that no longer exist
(`src/SteelmakingExpanded`, `assets/smex`, `HpexExtractionMigration`, the `Smex*`/`Hpex*` type
prefixes), with `SmexToIiexMigration` protected as a live type and the worklog and the two merge
records left alone as historical. ⛔ **Deferred on purpose: ~107 `smex:`/`hpex:` code literals in
`docs/design/**`.** Many are deliberate migration-source citations that must never be rewritten
(`naming.md` says so explicitly), so telling those from live-code references needs judgment per site.
That belongs with the plan triage, which is now the next task.

## 2026-08-14 - Scouting M.5 found live data loss in SHIPPED content

**Every block entity a smex 0.9.8 world holds was migrating empty.** Found while reading
`smex_0.9.8.zip` for M.5's block-entity alias table - the same table M.4 built for ppex.

The mechanism, and why nothing caught it. `BlockMigrationModSystem` reads the old state from a **live**
block entity (`ba.GetBlockEntity(pos)`), then hands it to `MigrateBlockEntity`, which
`SmexToIiexMigration` implements as a verbatim `FromTreeAttributes` copy. But a block entity's class
string is stored per-BE in the **save**, appears in no definition, and nothing migrates it: the shipped
worlds hold `smex.BlockEntityMoltenCanal` and friends, which no mod has registered since the ironmaking
content moved to iwex. So the game never constructs the old BE, `GetBlockEntity` returns null,
`oldState` arrives null, and the copy copies nothing. The block code migrates correctly and the block
arrives - empty. A canal loses its metal, a barrel its contents, a frozen pool its heat. No exception,
no log line, and every code-level guard green, because the codes were never the problem.

⛔ **11 classes affected**, all with real player worlds behind them: the four molten-canal classes, the
barrel, slag, solidified iron, the MP blower, the tuyere, the tap, and `smex.BlockEntityBlastFurnace` -
which despite its name backs the charge **door** (`blastfurnace/door.json`); the furnace core shipped
with no entityClass at all. Registered in iiex against their current types, three of them renamed as
well as relocated (`SolidifiedIron` → `HearthMetal`, `MpBlower` → `TwinTubMPBlower`,
`BlastFurnace` → `ChargeDoor`).

★ **The eight remaining shipped classes are deliberately NOT aliased.** Converter, cowper stove, air
blower, heat sink and the tiered tap/tuyere spellings are recorded in `ReleasedCodeDebt`, so their
blocks reach no live block either - an alias cannot save state for a block that never arrives. The
guard encodes that exemption rather than leaving it to be re-derived.

**Guarded, because the fix is worthless unguarded.** `gen-released-codes.py` now also derives
`ReleasedCodes.EntityClasses` (33 rows) from the release trees, and `ReleasedEntityClassTests` holds
every shipped class to resolving - via an alias, or via `{domain}.{TypeName}` still being live - unless
its blocktype is wholly debt. A second test refuses aliases for strings no release ever wrote, so the
list can only shrink as content retires. Mutation-checked: deleting the 11 rows fails with all 11 named
and their source blocktypes listed.

★★ **The general shape, for M.5 and any future rename.** `ReleasedCodeCoverageTests` proves a shipped
**code** reaches a live block; this proves a shipped **class** reaches a live type. They fail
differently and neither implies the other - the code half was green throughout.

Gate: 12/12, 1.20/1.21/1.22 (hpex suite 56 → 58).

## 2026-08-14 - M.4: iwex + lpex are one mod, `iiex`

**The merge, executed.** `src/IronworkingExpanded` + `src/LowPressureExpanded` -> `src/IronIndustryExpanded`,
one assembly (`iiex.dll`), one domain, one ModSystem, one config section, one test suite. lpex was the
smaller half (49 `.cs` to iwex's 155) and the dependency already ran lpex -> iwex, so absorbing upward
deleted an edge rather than inverting one. Version 0.7.0, continuing ppex's line; `modinfo` declares only
exlib. Assets merged to `assets/iiex/`, docs to `docs/iiex/`, handbook renumbered 00-09 (iron 00-04, steam
05-09). 12 gate targets now, not 15.

**Four things the merge could have broken silently, and what stopped each.**

`PipeMigration`'s three branches discriminated on **domain**, which is exactly what the merge collapses.
Both tiers carry the same `SegmentTypes`, so a mechanical `dom == "iiex"` rewrite emits every released
`ppex:` segment code twice with different targets - a free tier upgrade for a player's iron pipes, i.e. M3
inverted. They gate on the block's own `tier` variant now, with the domain kept only to scope the walk to
our own blocks. Mutation-checked: dropping the tier gate reproduces the double-claim and
`No_released_code_is_claimed_by_two_different_migrations` catches it, naming
`ppex:pipe-straight-ns-iron -> iiex:pipe-cast-straight-ns vs iiex:pipe-plated-straight-ns`.

**Two `ExRecipeProfiles.Register` calls would have silently replaced each other.** Both registries key on
the mod id and *replace* on a repeat, so keeping `IwexConfig` and `LpexConfig` as two sections under one
assembly would have left whichever registered first unreachable, with nothing to show for it. Merged into
one `IiexConfig` (110 + 45 properties, only `ConfigVersion` and `RecipeLevel` colliding) and one
`IiexRecipeConfig`. ⛔ That catalogue merge had its own trap: both mods spelled their pipe rows
`pipe-straight-grid`, and the collection initialiser assigns through the indexer - one tier's four rows
would have overwritten the other's with no duplicate-key error and no failing test. Keys are now
`pipe-plated-*` / `pipe-cast-*`.

**A stale domain row makes the whole migration contract pass over a world that will never exist.**
`ReleasedCodeCoverageTests.Domains` had to lose both the `iwex` and `lpex` rows and gain one `iiex` row
together, because `DefinitionCodes.ForDomain` *injects* the domain rather than filtering by it. Verified in
the dangerous direction: putting a stale `("lpex", mergedAsm)` row back leaves all six tests **green** while
synthesising phantom `lpex:` live blocks. No guard can catch that, so the reason is written on the field.

**Block-entity class strings live in the save, not in any definition.** `CodeRelocation` never touches
them and no golden sees them, so an unresolved key drops the block entity - a boiler keeps its blocks and
loses its water. The shipped `ppex_0.6.8.zip` names fifteen `entityClass` values; thirteen are now aliased
in `iiex` and the two extracted machines in `hpex`, which had none at all. `ppex.BlockEntityMpFluidPump`
stays unaliased until that pump is ported back from `0.9-support`.

**Two guards were wrong rather than the code.** `CostSelectorOverlap` compared selectors across
`RecipeCostEntry.Type`, so the Cornish boiler's legitimate `-rcc` and `-grid` rows read as an overlap the
moment they shared a catalogue; `ExRecipeCosts.Apply` dispatches on the type, so they can never contend.
And `RolledJointTests` was parameterised by **domain** as a proxy for tier - every plated-vs-cast row
collapsed into a literal duplicate asserting nothing about the pairing it was named for. Reparameterised on
`BlockPipe.*Tier`. That is the second half of deferred defect 1's "tautological `RolledJointTests`".

**The suite merge lost no coverage:** 1139 distinct test-method names before, 1139 after, the only two
removed being ones deliberately replaced. Eight duplicate `Lpex*Tests` classes were byte-equivalent to
their `Iwex*` counterparts once both resolved to the same assembly, so the -26 raw test count is duplicate
elimination, not lost checks.

**Verified where it only shows in game:** each mod staged with `dotnet publish` carries exactly one
ModSystem-bearing dll (`iiex.dll`, `HighPressureExpanded.dll`, `SteelmakingExpanded.dll`, `exlib.dll`).
A second one makes Vintage Story refuse to load the mod entirely, and no test run can see it.

**Content fallout worth knowing.** The two creative tabs collapsed onto one key and the *last* value won,
so the merged mod's tab read "Low Pressure"; it is now Iron Industry / Железная промышленность / Залізна
промисловість. Four `command-steam-*` lang keys were orphaned - `/exmod steam` was replaced by the generic
`/exmod recipes` long ago and nothing references them - so they are purged rather than renamed. The
lang trees were unioned by JSON key rather than moved as a directory, since all three locale filenames
collide and a directory move leaves them mutually consistent while losing a whole mod's strings.

Full gate green: **12/12 targets, 4,000 tests, 1.20/1.21/1.22**. Clean Release build has six warnings, all
pre-existing.

## 2026-08-14 - M.0: the merge would have deleted a pipe tier, and the plan said the problem was closed

**Scouting M.4 turned up a live defect in the plan itself.** The `iiex` merge's passthrough row read
**"✅ CLOSED (owner ruling + built 2026-08-14)"**. It was closed on the **block code** and wide open on the
**asset Location**, which is the level `ExDefinitions` actually keys on.

`ExBlockDef.Location` is `{domain}:blocktypes/{assetName}.json` - the asset path and no variants - and
`BlockPipe.Common` passed the same `assetName` (`pipe/straight`) for every tier, adding `tier` afterwards
as a *variant group*. M.2 moved the code and fixed the handbook `groupBy`; nothing moved the Location. So
under one domain the plated and cast tiers collided on **six blocktype Locations and one recipe Location**,
and a second def landing on an existing key **replaces it - last-writer-wins, ordered by load, unlogged**.

⛔⛔ **Merging as planned would have deleted a whole pipe tier on the first green build.** The codes still
differ (`pipe-plated-*` vs `pipe-cast-*`), so every code-level guard, every `groupBy` guard and every
migration guard stays green; the surviving tier places, renders and groups correctly and the other simply
does not exist. That is M3 - *a merge collapses no tier* - executed silently, by the one row that claimed
it was handled. The plan is corrected in place and the fix is recorded as stage **M.0**.

★★ **Written as a failing assertion first.** `PipeTierLocationTests` lives in exlib and drives the
factories rather than the shipping providers, so it holds for any tier pair including one a consumer adds.
Three claims - two tiers in one domain share no definition Location, no segment shape, and exlib pins no
shape to a content mod's domain - and all three failed before anything changed, naming the six Locations
and six shape paths. A fourth asserts every tier declares the same count, so the others cannot pass by a
tier collapsing to nothing.

**The fix.** `BlockPipe.Asset(tier, leaf)` yields `pipe/{tier}/{leaf}` and is used by the four segment
factories and by `BlockPipePassthrough`; the segment art moved to
`assets/{iwex,lpex,hpex}/shapes/pipe/{plated,cast,rolled}/`; the two colliding recipe files split to
`pipes-plated` / `pipes-cast`. Goldens and the generated block-code tables were regenerated through
`EXLIB_WRITE_GOLDENS` / `EXLIB_WRITE_BLOCKCODES`, never hand-edited, and only the tiered goldens were
deleted - lpex's four untiered fittings keep their location and would have been lost by a blanket sweep.

⛔ **Two of the six shape collisions were the design, not the bug.** The passthroughs deliberately share
one brick mesh across every tier and differ only by the sheet texture, so per-tier folders would have been
wrong for them. What *was* wrong: the shared mesh was written `iwex:pipe/passthrough` - **a content mod's
domain hardcoded inside exlib**, which resolves to nothing the moment iwex is renamed, and a blocktype
whose shape resolves to nothing loads with no shape rather than failing. That art now lives in
`assets/exlib/shapes/pipe/` and the literal is gone.

⛔ **Left deliberately, and a rename sweep will miss it.** `BlockPipePassthrough.Sheet` still returns
`iwex:block/metal/castiron`, and `assets/hpex/shapes/pipe/rolled/*.json` name the same texture. It is
referenced by ~40 files across three mods, so it belongs to the merge's rename rather than here - but the
four **hpex** references sit outside both merging asset trees, which is exactly where a per-file sweep over
"the two mods being merged" stops looking.

★ **A premise that did not survive checking.** The scouting reported that nothing resolves a `shapeByType`
target to a file on disk, and called the art move a silent risk. `DefinitionAssets.MissingShapes` has done
precisely that all along and is wired into all five suites - and it resolves the **shape's own** domain, so
the cross-domain move into `exlib:` was verified rather than assumed. Mutation-checked by hiding one moved
file: red, naming the expected path, then green on restore.

Verified on the full gate: 15 targets, **4,023 tests**, green across 1.20/1.21/1.22.

**M.4 itself is not started**, and the scouting produced an ordered five-stage ruling for it. The findings
that change the shape of that work: `CodeRelocation.Remap` walks the **live** registry, so there is no
`ppex → lpex → iiex` chain to preserve - it is one hop and the edit is to retarget `newDomain` in place;
only **exlib, ppex and smex** have ever shipped, so `iwex:`/`lpex:` codes need no migration at all;
`PipeMigration`'s three branches discriminate on **domain**, which is the thing the merge collapses, and
must become tier gates; and both smex and hpex list lpex *and* iwex explicitly, so the second
`ProjectReference` must be deleted rather than left to the transitive one - a failure that is green
everywhere and absent in game.

## 2026-08-14 - F3 closed: the two pages the guard could not reach, and the drift's actual source

**F3.1's second half, F3.3, and a stage the plan did not have.** `WikiParity` closed the two pages it
could see. These are the other two, and every defect in them had to be found by reading the library,
because the guard resolves symbols and none of these were symbol errors.

**`Getting-Started.md`.** The plan predicted three defects and all three were real: the `partial` advice
routing to a generator deleted in `f4ddcea`, the claim that exlib "is compiled into `lpex.dll`", and the
missing `$(GamePath)` definition. Three more came out of the same read and all three are worse.

⛔⛔ **The `ProjectReference` snippet was missing `<Private>false</Private>`** - the single
highest-consequence line on the page. Without it the SDK copies `exlib.dll` into the reader's mod output
and Vintage Story refuses to load the mod **at all**, naming no cause the reader can act on; hpex's own
csproj comment records the message verbatim. Every mod csproj in this repo sets it. The page shipped the
one variant that silently produces a mod that never appears.

⛔ **The registration snippet does not compile** - `CommandRegistry` is in `.Registries.Commands`, not
`.Registries.Entities`, and only the one `using` was given. ⛔ **And the dependency floor was `0.7.0`.**
A modinfo dependency is a *minimum, not a pin* - the recurring lesson from the 0.9.7 release - which is
exactly why a stale floor is dangerous rather than harmless: it lets a player satisfy it with an exlib
predating two documented breaking changes, and the failure lands at world load as a missing member.

**`Config-System.md`.** The fabricated `Edit` on `ExConfigRegister<T>` was the predicted defect and the
smallest one. The page had never mentioned the **shared, mod-sectioned document** at all - it taught
per-mod config files, and taught as the *current* file name (`lpex_values.json`) a string that is now a
**legacy** name folded into the `lpex` section of `ex_values.json`. Three further behaviours were stated
backwards: the write-back is **server-only** (both sides load one store in one process in singleplayer
and would race), an out-of-range value on load is **reset to the coded default, not clamped to the
bound**, and `LegacyFileNames` **folds a legacy file into a section** rather than renaming it.

**F3.3, and the settlement is a deletion.** ★★ The preference-wiring order was stated in three places,
and the constraint all three argue about **does not exist in either direction**: `ExPreferences.LoadConfig`
never reads the registered set, `PreferenceRegistry.RegisterAll` never reads the config. Picking a winner
between the two documented orders would have shipped a third wrong statement. exlib itself does the
opposite of what the wiki instructed, which is what the "contradiction" actually was.

★ The real constraint was a different one nobody had written down: register preferences **before the
mod's own `CommandRegistry.RegisterAll`**, because a preference sub-command resolves its definition
**once, at registration time**, into a local it then holds. exlib survives only through an explicit
`?? new MeasurePreference()` fallback; a consumer writing `Find(key)!` gets an NRE at world load.

⛔⛔ **Only one of the three statements was in the wiki - the other two were source XML docs.** That is
the finding worth carrying. `PreferenceRegistry`'s own summary carried both the wrong order and a claim
that the `.exmod` command "builds a sub-command per registered preference", which nothing implements;
`ExpandedLibModSystem` asserted the shipped order with a **false reason**. The same pattern held on the
config side: the wiki's two worst errors are the *verbatim text* of `ExConfigRegister`'s `LegacyFileNames`
and `Load` doc comments. **The page and its source said the same wrong thing and corroborated each
other.** Fixing the page alone would have regenerated it on the next read, so F3.5 was added to the plan
mid-stage and fixes the comments too - including `IExConfigAccess.Range`, documented as `"0 to 1"` where
the formatter emits `"0..1"`, a case where the wiki was right and the source was wrong.

**On the guard.** ⛔ `WikiParity` caught **none** of this, and a green run is not evidence a page is
correct - only that its symbols exist. Two structural gaps surfaced: `ClassWithBase` has no room for a
type parameter, so a fence declaring `class ExConfigRegister<TConfig> : IExConfigAccess` skips the
declaration rules entirely (which is why the fabricated `Edit` survived), and a member written on a
lowercase local is invisible. Mutation-checked that the rewritten pages *are* under the guard: a probe
naming a missing member and a fake `Ex` type failed both rules, on the new content, and was reverted.

Verified on the full gate: 15 targets, **4,019 tests**, green across 1.20/1.21/1.22.

Also swept in the same pass, because they carry the same claims one click away: `Registries.md` (the
`partial` note, the ordering block, the cheat-sheet's `CommandRegistry` in `Start`),
`Source-Generators.md` and `Block-Networks.md` (the stale config file names), `Commands.md` (the
`.exmod measure` sub-command was missing from the built-in table while `Registries.md` pointed at it),
and lpex's README, which listed a `Preferences/` directory that moved to exlib.

## 2026-08-14 - the wiki gets the guard the rest of the repo already had

**F3.4, then F3.1 and F3.2 as its output.** This repository tests its goldens, its lang keys, its code
literals and its released block codes. The public API documentation - the one artifact a third party is
actually asked to build against - had no guard at all, and had drifted accordingly. `WikiParity` closes
that, and the order matters: the guard was written first and then pointed at the wiki, so the fixes below
are its report rather than a hunt.

It checks three claims, each picked because it is unambiguous and a reader acts on it. A member written
against a type exlib owns must exist on it. An `Ex`-prefixed identifier written as code must name a type.
And a declaration's shape must match the base it names: `public override` against a `protected` member is
CS0507, and `abstract` against a member the base implements tells a consumer to write something they do
not have to. **129 symbols across 15 pages.**

★ **It found every defect the plan had predicted, on the first run, plus one it had not.** The deleted
`ExAttributeGenerator` (gone in `f4ddcea`, still documented with a section and two examples). The
`public override GetFallbackOrientation` that cannot compile. The `AllowedOrientations` declared abstract
where it is virtual with a definitions-derived default - and `GetFallbackOrientation` was declared
abstract in the same list, which the plan had not recorded.

⛔ **Two things the design of the guard turned on.** A bare signature list is where a page drifts furthest
from the code, and it has no `class X : Base` line to resolve against - so the base is taken from the
section's own heading (*"Key `BlockNetworkNode` members to know"*), which is what made that list
checkable at all. And the generators **cannot be reflected**: the project sets `IncludeBuildOutput=false`
and ships no runtime assembly, so a naive check reports every generator as missing. Their names are read
out of `src/ExpandedLib.Generators/*.cs` rather than allowed by hand - which is the difference between a
guard that would have let `ExAttributeGenerator` through forever and one that catches the next deletion.

Mutation-checked in both directions: reinstating the deleted generator name fails it, and widening an
override back to `public` fails it.

**F3.2 with it.** Both `Block-Networks.md` and `Production-Machines.md` taught spending the base-class
slot on being a graph node or a machine - the exact workaround the membership and process behaviours were
built to remove. Both are now fronted with the three axes, each naming the escape its own reader needs.

⛔ **What the guard does not see, recorded so it is not mistaken for coverage.** `Config-System.md`'s
documented `Edit` method is written on a lowercase local (`_store.Edit`), and the guard only resolves
members written against a type name; `Getting-Started.md`'s wrong claims are prose. Both remain open
under F3.1. A guard that narrows a surface is not one that closes it, and the plan says so now.

Also: `DefinitionGoldens.RepoRoot()` is public, so a source-tree-reading helper no longer needs its own
copy of the probe. Fifteen test files still carry one, each hardcoding this repository's solution name -
unchanged here, but there is now something for them to collapse onto.

Verified on the full gate: 15 targets, **4,019 tests**, green across 1.20/1.21/1.22.

## 2026-08-14 - the passthroughs were one code shared between two tiers

**Follow-on to M.2, on an owner ruling.** Scouting M.3 turned up something M.2 had left: iwex and lpex
**both** call `BlockPipePassthrough.Passthroughs(domain)`, so both ship
`pipe-{passthrough|passthroughbend}-{brick}-{orient}` off the same factory, at the same asset path, with
the same shape. The only difference is the sheet texture - corroded iron for iwex, cast iron for lpex.
Two domains, so two codes, so nobody noticed. Under one domain they are **one** `ExDefinitions` key and
the later registration replaces the earlier: last-writer-wins, ordered by load, no error (F8.7). M.2 had
deliberately left the passthroughs untiered because they bear no pressure.

Owner ruling: it is a tier, so it gets the axis. Both pairs now carry `tier` declared first -
`iwex:pipe-plated-passthrough-{brick}-*`, `lpex:pipe-cast-passthrough-{brick}-*` - with the sheet texture
keyed on the **tier** rather than the domain, which is what makes the pair legible after the merge. They
get distinct names too: Plated / Cast Pipe Passthrough. ru and uk had drifted apart on this block
independently (Проходка трубы vs Проходная труба), so both are unified on one noun phrase per locale -
draft, pending the owner.

⛔⛔ **The migration surgery M.2 sidestepped was real, and one half of it was load-bearing.**
`PipeMigration`'s brick branch built its historical smex code as `"gas" + path` from the **whole live
path**, so a tier segment would have gone into the old code and matched nothing; it now rebuilds from the
variants. Worse, `LpexRenameMigration` listed `pipe-passthrough` as a flat `ppex → lpex` rename, which
finds a live block by prefix - and `pipe-cast-passthrough-…` does not start with `pipe-passthrough-`.
Mutation-checked by dropping the fix: **120 released `ppex:` codes lose their migration**, silently but
for `ReleasedCodeCoverageTests`. `CodeRelocation` already had the rename overload this needed.

★ **Three consumers only tests found.** `LpexRecipeOutputTests` caught 16 recipe outputs still naming the
old codes - it is the guard that says "this block cannot be crafted, with no error anywhere", and it was
right. `CowperOrientationTests` restated the expected code as a literal. And `PipeFittings` built its
passthrough with the old code, which after the change would have satisfied no layout.

⛔ **And a pre-existing defect found on the way, not fixed here.**
`assets/smex/patches/compat/lpex/refractory.json` adds the three refractory brick grades back to the
passthrough/outlet families when smex is installed. It targets `lpex:blocktypes/**pipes**/passthrough.json`
- plural - while the code-first defs emit `blocktypes/**pipe**/passthrough.json`. **All 24 of its
blocktype patches have been dead since the code-first move**, so smex players have been quietly missing
the refractory passthroughs; only its three recipe patches still apply, and they output variants that no
longer exist. Its brick-index paths were `/variantgroups/1/`, which the new `tier` group shifts to `/2/`;
that is corrected so the file is right if revived. Reviving it is a content decision - the refractory
grades were deliberately removed from these families - so it is recorded rather than switched on.

## 2026-08-14 - the pipe tier is a variant, and it is the first segment of the code

**M.2 of the framework-hardening plan.** The pipe tier was the block's domain: `iwex:pipe-straight-ns`
was plated because it was iwex, and `BlockPipe` resolved the burst rating, the throughput and the joint
family out of `Code.Domain`. The merge (M1) puts iwex and lpex in one domain, and M3 forbids losing
either tier, so the tier had to move onto the block. It is now a variant group and the code is
`pipe-{tier}-{type}-{orient}` - `iwex:pipe-plated-straight-ns`, `lpex:pipe-cast-straight-ns`,
`hpex:pipe-rolled-straight-ns`. `BlockPipe.Tier` reads it, and the three registries are keyed on it.

Scope is the four segments of all three tiers plus both valves. The bricks - passthrough, outlet,
passthrough-bend, fluid intake - name no tier: they bear no pressure, share one shape and bolt to any
flanged run, and a block with no tier falls back to exactly the defaults they resolved to before
(5 atm, 120 L/s, flanged). So no fitting's behaviour moved, and `PipeMigration`'s brick branch, which
reads the whole live path to rebuild `"gas" + path`, keeps working untouched.

★★ **Tier-first was already guarded, which is a better answer than the scouting's.** The plan reasoned
from `WildcardUtil.fastMatch` that a leading `*` absorbs the new segment, so the ~30
`ShapeByType("*-straight-ns", …)` selectors keep matching. True - but `EmittedBlocktypeShapeTests` has
asserted since before this stage that every emitted variant resolves a shape, and its doc comment names
this exact hazard. Mutation-checked properly: declaring `tier` last **and re-blessing the golden** - the
realistic version of the mistake, since a golden that disagrees is the first thing anyone fixes - still
fails it.

⛔ **Two things the scouting had wrong, both found by doing it.**

The handbook selectors do **not** widen to `pipe-*-straight-*`. A `groupBy` selector carrying no domain
is qualified with the grouping block's own before matching
(`SlideshowItemstackTextComponent.cs:108-115`), so the three tiers group separately today *only because
they are three domains*. `pipe-*-straight-*` reproduces that now and silently merges all three into one
handbook entry the moment two tiers share a domain - which is the merge, which is the point. They are
`pipe-{tier}-straight-*` instead, which is correct on both sides of it.

And `PipeMigration` did need an edit. Its live-block rows follow the tier for free, as scouted - the old
code is rebuilt from `Variant["type"]` and `["orientation"]` read individually while the target is
`Code.Clone()`. But three legacy rows at the bottom (`gaspipe-blower`, `gaspipe-heated`, `gaspipe-intake`)
name `iwex:pipe-straight-{axis}` as a **literal** target, and a literal follows nothing.

**A new guard, for the one surface this stage moved that had none.** `shapeByType` was covered; the
handbook's `groupBy` is the same failure class on a sibling key - start-anchored, and an unmatched
selector is not an error - and nothing checked it. `Every_handbook_group_selector_matches_a_shipped_code`
resolves each selector the way the game does and requires it to match at least one shipped code, across
the whole domain rather than one def (a segment lists all four of its tier's shapes, so a per-def check
would be wrong). It earned itself immediately: a `git checkout` meant to undo a mutation reverted a
golden past its legitimate M.2 regeneration, and this is the test that said so.

★ **A fixture was asserting through a coincidence.** The three pressure-valve fixtures wore
`lpex:pressurevalve-{material}-ns` with a `material` variant production dropped long ago. Under the new
resolution they answer the *untiered default* of 5 atm - and the cast tier's rating is also 5, so every
assertion would have kept passing while testing the fallback. They now carry the shipped code and the
`tier` variant, and `The_ceiling_is_the_cast_tier_s_rating_and_it_comes_from_the_variant` pins the
difference by registering a throwaway tier at 9.5 atm and checking a valve wearing it reads 9.5.
cast-pipes.md's Gotcha 2 had already named this hazard in the abstract; this is it in a test.

**Display names, at last.** All three tiers rendered the identical "Piping (Straight)" - the tooltip
could not tell them apart, which is half of why the tier being invisible on the block was tolerable.
Now Plated / Cast / Rolled Piping, with ru and uk written to the glossary's conventions
(листовая/чугунная/катаная, листова/чавунна/катана) and **flagged as drafts for the owner's read**. The
two valve names are deliberately unchanged: there is only one of each, so nothing needs disambiguating,
and calling a valve "Cast" would assert a material its own recipe contradicts - it is built from a
*plated* segment, because B19 leaves the cast tier uncraftable. The `tier` variant carries the rating;
the name does not have to carry the material.

⛔ **Left on the doorstep, deliberately.** `BlockConverterBessemer.cs:108` still requires
`lpex:pipe-straight-ns-{metal}`, which resolved to nothing before this stage and resolves to nothing
after it (B23). The correct target is now `lpex:pipe-cast-straight-ns` - which has no recipe (B19), so
repointing it trades one wall for another and reads as a fix. M.3 owns that layout; both halves belong
in one change. Noted in NEXT.md.

Docs: `rolled-pipe.md` and `cast-pipes.md` both carried the retired *"the tier is the mod, not a variant
axis"* ruling and are rewritten with what supersedes it and why; `pipe-network.md` § 5's two tables are
re-keyed and its Gotcha 11 sharpened. Every citation touched was re-read against the file first.

Verified on the full gate: 15 targets, **4,017 tests**, green across 1.20/1.21/1.22.

## 2026-08-13 - the shear IS drawn, and its decision is built

⛔⛔ **Owner correction: the shear has art, and I reported twice that it had none.** It is filed under the
shop name - `machines/mpenergy/machine-mp-megablock-**cutter**.json` - so a sweep for "shear" across
`assets/editable/shapes/` found nothing, and shear.md had recorded that absence as fact ("Nothing is
drawn"). Search art by what a machinist would call the machine. The tooling item is
`items/smithed/item-forged-machineshears.json`, and `machine-mp-megablock-nailcutter.json` beside it is the
nail machine, not this.

⛔⛔ **The footprint in the design was wrong too.** Measured off the shape: x −16…31, y 0…26, z 0…16 =
**3 × 1 × 2 cells**. shear.md said 1 × 1 × 1 and "needs neither filler nor multiblock". The blade alone is
14 voxels wide. ⛔ The filler *layout* within those cells is the owner's and is pending, for this machine
and the nine other mpenergy megablocks - not invented here, because a wrong legend fails silently.

★★ **Two open items close on the art.** The blades carry 14 voxels of edge, which takes `flatwide`'s
`MaxWidth` 15 - the widest plate the forming line makes - so **the wide shear is this block**, not a second
station and not a hammer die. And the blade set is settled by machining-line.md's tooling table: a forged
and tempered consumable with **vanilla's temper ladder as the tier ladder**, so no hardness system is owed.

What the drawn `cycle` clip already decides: `Shaft` turns 720° against `Shaft2`'s 360°, so the gearing is
**2:1** and one slow revolution is **one stroke**; `CutterMass` swings from its −15° rest to closed at
**frame 44 of 60**, so the cut lands three-quarters through the clip rather than on frame 0. It is a lever
guillotine, and the drive runs along z, across the blade.

**`ShearFeed` / `ShearDecision` built** (13 tests) - the pure decision, which needs no footprint and so
could land ahead of the layout. Seven verdicts in the order a player can fix them: `NoBladeSet` → `NoJob` →
`Spent` → `BladeTooSoft` → `NotTurning` → `NotEnoughDrive`.

★★ **The gate reads `ProcessJob.MinTorque` and `.MinTier`, not `RollSetSpec`'s.** The job already declares
both, so the blade set needs no spec format of its own - which is the cheapest possible answer to "blade
sets are named and unspecified". `RollingPass.CanCarry` gains its first production caller in the same
change. Cold cutting stays a *power* achievement: below rolling heat the stroke costs
`MinTorque × ShearColdTorqueMultiplier` and is never refused for being cold, because shearing needs force
rather than friction. Mutation-checked: dropping the `Max(1f, …)` clamp and dropping the turning check each
break a test.

## 2026-08-13 - the crop count, and the rule it forced

`WorkPiece.Cropped` plus `Crop(count)` / `CropsLeft(count)` / `IsSpent(count)`, round-tripped on the stack
under `stockCropped`. All five suites pass: **3903**. The shear is now the only thing left before the
forming line can make a finished product.

★★ **Built as crops taken, not crops remaining**, which is the one place it departs from the ruling's
wording. Counting up keeps zero meaning *untouched*, so a piece that has never met a shear and one worked
out to nothing cannot read alike - with "remaining" they collide on the same stored 0 and the state is
ambiguous. It also means no piece already in a world needs migrating, and the **declared count stays the
authority**: retuning a crop row from 4 to 6 gives every existing piece the two extra crops rather than
stranding it on the number it was cut against. Written as an absence when zero, so whole pieces still stack.

⛔⛔ **And it forced a rule no design page had stated: a part-cropped piece cannot be rolled.** New
`FeedVerdict.PartCropped`, checked before the gap is judged (a part piece offered a bad gap must not send
the player along the barrel looking for a good one). Without it: crop three products out of a bar, roll what
is left to the next stage, and it is worth that stage's whole count again - metal from nothing.

★★ That refusal is what *keeps `WorkPiece.Mass` deferred*. The tally is against one stage's count, so
carrying a part piece between stages needs a proportion - two ints, or the mass the ruling took off the
table. Refusing the carry makes one `int` per stage sufficient. It costs the player only the order they
work in, and [rolling](../../design/processes/rolling.md) already describes the mandatory crop that way:
*"each of the five 600 u pieces is either claimed … or rolled on through 1.5 to 1.0"* - the five products,
not the remainder mid-cut.

Mutation-checked: removing the mill's refusal breaks three `MillFeedTests`; removing `Crop`'s spent clamp
breaks the worked-out test. Three design pages carried the superseded `mass / count` wording (shear.md's
interaction table, its settled-ruling bullet and its blocker row) and are corrected, along with the modder
wiki, which gained the staged-crops/whole-item-converts table and both consequences.

## 2026-08-13 - the torque state, built

The first of yesterday's two rulings, implemented. All five suites pass: **3893** (one net test fewer -
two geometry-of-the-bite torque tests retired, one "every hot pass asks the same" gained).

`BlockEntityRollingMill.LoadTorque` is now `RollingLoadTorque` while stock is between the rolls and zero
when it is not, times the flow stress the piece's heat has climbed to. **0.34 N·m**, chosen as 85 % of the
0.4 N·m one bridged wheel leaves after friction at ω_max - the *"about 15 % to spare"* the design has
claimed since it was written and never actually had.

★★ **The deletions are the substance.** `RollingPass.ContactLength` and the eight-argument
`LoadTorque(draft, width, R, temp, …)` are gone, `RollingTorqueScale` with them. So are the pass's own
`_draft` and `_width`: nothing downstream of the bite test read either, so `BeginPass` lost a parameter
and the block entity lost two persisted keys (`rmDraft`, `rmWidth` - unread on an old save, harmless).

★★ **No config migration, deliberately.** This is a delete plus an add, not a rename: a saved
`RollingTorqueScale` binds to no field and is dropped, and a `RollingLoadTorque` absent from the file takes
the shipped default. The 0.3.0 firebox row exists because a *rename* can strand a player's tuning under
the old key; nothing is stranded here, and a reset row would be a no-op on a key no player can have set.

★★ **`RollingMillLoadTests` had to change shape, and the new shape is the honest one.** With a declared
demand there is no such thing as a heavy piece, so "heavy" became a property of the run: a drive too weak
to hold a working stand (0.7 N·m against 0.34 of load and 0.5 of standing friction), or a drive that falls
away entirely with a charged wheel and a piece still to draw. That last case is what a flywheel is *for*,
and it gives the small-versus-large comparison a 3.5× margin where the old synthetic 40-wide stock gave
none at all. ⛔ The 40-wide piece was fiction - no such stock exists - and it was the only thing keeping
those three tests alive after the friction re-calibration.

Mutation-checked both halves: `RollingLoadTorque` 0.34 → 0.45 breaks
`One_water_wheel_carries_a_hot_pass_indefinitely`; dropping the `FlowStress` factor breaks three cooling
tests; `LargeWheel` 150 → 20 breaks the flywheel comparison, so it tracks the real margin rather than any
inequality.

⛔ Still open, unchanged: **pass duration** is the last consumer of computed length (`length / ωR` in
`BeginPass`), and the crop count is next.

## 2026-08-13 - two rulings that delete work: length is art, torque is a state

Owner corrections, both taken before anything was built on the wrong footing. The torque half is built
(above); the crop count is not. Recorded in NEXT.md, shear.md, process-extension.md and rolling-mill.md.

⛔⛔ **Length is not defined programmatically - it comes from the art - and cut points are config.** I was
about to add `WorkPiece.Mass` and make length mass-derived (`L = V/(w·t)`, `V = mass/2.5`) so a cropped
remainder would be shorter. Wrong axis: *"this is not a thing that we can calculate, it's purely based on
what modder wants."*

★★ The right shape is much smaller. `ProcessJob.count` **already is** the declaration, so a staged crop
needs **one `int` on the stack - crops remaining** - seeded from `count`, decremented per stroke, spent at
zero. Consequences: `WorkPiece.Mass` comes **off the critical path** and no longer gates the shear; the
48-voxel refusal stops being arithmetic and becomes a **declared** stage property; and `count` is
explicitly the modder's number, with nothing validating it against geometry. The shipped rows dividing
exactly (400/4, 600/6, 3000/5) is whoever authored them applying the density rule, not a loader rule -
process-extension.md said the opposite yesterday and has been corrected.

⛔⛔ **Torque becomes a running/idle state rather than a geometry formula.** A configured constant while a
pass is under the rolls, zero when idle, cold multiplier on top - replacing `LoadTorque(draft, width, R,
temp, coldMult, coldSpan, scale)`.

★★ This is the structural fix for yesterday's defect, not just a simplification. **A geometry-derived load
silently follows any change to the geometry.** The two-round model quartered the draft and the gap re-cut
narrowed the entry piece; the mill's demand fell from a designed 85 % of a bridged wheel's headroom to
16 %, and nothing could have caught it because the formula was still "correct". A declared demand cannot
drift that way, and the `RollingTorqueScale` retune I was going to ask for becomes moot.

⛔ Left open: **pass duration** is the last consumer of computed length (`length / ωR` in `BeginPass`). It
reads a declared length off the stage or becomes a declared time - but the ω coupling must survive, since
"the network speed draws the stock through" is the mill's whole point. The composed mesh's Z scale is the
only other consumer and item 7 retires it.

## 2026-08-12 - item 4, and the skip bound built the same day it was ruled

The config re-cut and the friction calibration it was the precondition for. All five suites pass: 3894.

**Item 4.** Both narrow families walk 2.5 / 2.0 / 1.5 / 1.0 off the stock's own ladder, `flat` runs barrel
**4**, the 0.5 rung is gone, and `slitting` is **retired** - it accepted `"plate"`, which is not a
`StockForm`, and no ladder declared a slitting rung, so it was tooling with no route. ★★ Retired, not
renamed, so **no migration**: remapping a retired code onto a surviving one hands the player an item they
never had, which is the rule `CastingNameMigration` already set. `ShippedRollSetTests` now pins the
dead-set list **empty**, which is the stronger assertion.

★★ **The "2, 2, 4, 4" schedule cost survived the barrel change unchanged**, for a different reason: at
barrel 4 the piece outgrows the rolls one gap earlier, which moves the four-feed gaps up by one and leaves
the total at twelve. The comment that was flagged stale is now true again without being edited.

**The skip bound.** `HotFriction` 0.5 → 0.3 puts δ_max at 0.36 - above one round's 0.25 draft, below one
gap's 0.5 - so the next rung is the only rung that bites. `ColdFriction` 0.09 → 0.055 holds the ~30×
ratio. `MillSchedule.NextGap` stays uncalled: the walk is enforced by friction and by nothing else, which
is what the design always claimed and had never been true.

★★ **The guard that had been checking the wrong model.** `ShippedRollSetTests` walked each route
*gap by gap*, computing a whole-gap draft - the one-bite model the two-round change retired. It passed only
because δ_max used to be 1.0; against a δ_max calibrated below a gap it would have condemned every route a
player can actually walk. Rewritten to walk round by round through the real `WorkPiece`, plus a new
`A_skipped_gap_is_refused_on_every_shipped_route` for the other half of the calibration.

⛔⛔ **The finding: `RollingTorqueScale` is calibrated to nothing.** An ordinary round loads the run at
**0.065 N·m against ~0.4 of headroom - 16 %**, where the design's worked pass claims *"about 15 % to
spare"*, i.e. 85 %. Two changes compound: the two-round model quartered the draft, and the re-cut moved the
entry gap so the piece is narrower at first bite. Restoring the intended ratio puts the scale near
**0.097**. Left alone on purpose - the constant balances the mill against the flywheel, the bridge and the
transmission at once, so it is a plant-wide retune and the owner's call.

⛔ Its visible consequence, and the reason three load tests had to change: **no stock can stall a hot pass
any more.** The deepest legal bite on the widest real stock is 0.43 N·m and a bridged wheel carries it, so
the hot over-heavy case those tests pinned is unreachable. They now use a deliberately synthetic width,
with the arithmetic in the fixture saying so, and keep pinning what they are really about - mp-energy's
"carried or wound down, no equilibrium between" - while the two cooling tests beside them cover the stall
that is still reachable.

⛔ A process note. Sweeping `draft: 0.5f` → `0.25f` across the test tree to fix the `BeginPass` fixtures
also hit `Torque(draft: 0.5f, …)` calls, where 0.5 was load-bearing: it collapsed
`Torque_is_linear_in_draft` into comparing a value with twice itself, and the test still passed the build.
A blind literal sweep is not a refactor. Caught by the assertion, restored from `git show HEAD:`.

## 2026-08-12 - two rulings: the skip bound, and what a crop yields

Both were owed by the two-round model landing. Written into the design docs; neither is built.

★★ **The skip bound: calibrate `δ_max`, do not add an ordering rule.** Halving every bite doubled how far
`δ_max` reaches, and a fresh bar could be walked to the plate gap in two or three feeds against ten or
twelve. Three answers were on the table - refuse anything past `MillSchedule.NextGap`, re-calibrate, or
accept a deep skip as a torque-for-feeds trade. Ruled: **`HotFriction` 0.5 → 0.3**, so δ_max falls 1.0 →
0.36, an ordinary 0.25 round bites and a 0.5 skip skids. Two constants, no new verdict, no new lang key,
and the page's own claim - *physics is the schedule* - becomes true instead of aspirational.
`ColdFriction` 0.09 → ≈0.055 alongside it, to hold the stated ~30× hot/cold bite ratio.

⛔⛔ **It re-orders the forming list.** The shipped `flat` and `slab` ladders open at 2.0 from a 3.0 base,
so their first rounds are 0.5 - under δ_max 0.36 neither could take a first bite at all. **Item 4 must land
first**, which is config only. Building the friction change before it would make the mill unusable in
exactly the way B4 once did.

★★ **The crop: one product plus a remainder - and the two "defensible readings" were never alternatives.**
The question had stood as "two stacks, or one plus a remainder?", with recoverability's *"into 6"* against
the crop-not-convert rule. Reading the **already-built** `ProcessJob` settled it: `count` is the *divisor*
in every published crop row - 400 / 4, 600 / 6, 3000 / 5, because the density rule makes every crop divide
exactly. So "into 6" is the piece's **total yield** and crop-not-convert is **how it leaves**. One stroke
takes `mass / count` and writes the rest back as stock.

★★ **Generalised, it is a rule about the two job kinds already in the schema**: a **staged** job crops (the
input survives, lighter) and a **whole-item** job converts (the input is consumed). The names were already
the rule; nothing in the schema changed, and the shipped example in the contract page stayed valid.

★★ **The mandatory crops stop being a special operation.** Under this reading a player crops until the
piece seats, rather than performing a distinct "divide into 6" action - so the two recoverability crops
need no separate mechanism, and no short-length `StockForm` variants have to be invented to hold the
pieces.

⛔⛔ **`WorkPiece.Mass` is now a hard prerequisite of the shear.** A remainder must weigh less than the
piece, and mass is a fixed figure on the *itemtype* today. shear.md predicted this in those words; the
two-round model brought `Length` but not `Mass`. Order is now: item 4 → friction → `Mass` → the shear.

## 2026-08-12 - the two-round pass model, and the stock forms renamed

Forming rework items 3 and 6, the owner's ruling for what came after the extensibility layer. All five
suites pass: 3892 tests.

★★ **The `Gap` field is what makes the two-round model work without a round counter.** `WorkPiece` is now
one `Thickness` for the whole piece, the `Gap` it is half way through, and a flag per side. Round 1 lands
`(thickness + gap) / 2` and *writes* `Gap`; round 2 sees `Gap == g` and lands `g`. The obvious alternatives
both fail: detecting "am I on a half-step?" by asking whether the gauge is a declared rung breaks the
moment someone declares half-step art (item 7 would do exactly that), and storing the round's target
instead cannot survive the player switching gaps mid-round. Writing `Gap` **only when a round completes**
is the whole trick — during round 1 it still reads as 0, which is why the second side of a two-sided piece
does not accidentally land the gap.

★★ **Side count is the flag array's length, so "the round resets on a different barrel" is free.**
`ForSides(n)` re-sizes the array; a barrel that divides the piece differently gets a fresh array and the
recorded feeds go with it, while a barrel that divides it the same way keeps them. No barrel width is
stored anywhere. Two barrels that give the same side count divide the piece the same way, so that *is* what
"a different barrel" means.

★★ **A side already fed this round reads as `NoReduction`, and that is physically honest** rather than a
new verdict: the side is at this round's gauge already and would pass through untouched. The abstraction
(one gauge until the last side catches up) and the fiction (each side really is thinner) agree here.

**A net deletion of nine members** — `Strips`, `Turned`, `StripWidth`, `StripLength`, `Thickest`,
`Thinnest`, `IsEven`, `Resplit`, `WithStrip`, `IsTurned`, plus `StockMesh.SideOf` and `SidePlacement`. Two
bugs closed as side effects: the refused offer that mutated the held stack (`ForSides` returns a record
instead of writing through, and the write-back moved inside the accepted branch), and `Resplit`'s silent
no-op on an uneven piece, which could strand a piece no barrel would take.

**The composed mesh was repointed rather than retired.** It existed only for lopsided pieces, which are now
unreachable — so it draws the **half-step**, the gauge between two rungs that no ladder declares and no art
draws. `IsBaseState` now tests the gauge alone: a piece merely divided for a narrow barrel is still its
base shape, which the old test got wrong in the other direction.

⛔ **The contract page was wrong about what a rung is.** process-extension.md said "the ladder is drawn per
*pass*: 3.00 → 2.75 → 2.50", with the half-steps as the rungs carrying no `code`. But `MillSchedule` builds
the deck's gap bands straight from the rungs a family accepts, so declaring half-steps as rungs puts twice
as many bands on the deck as the barrel has grooves. **One rung per gap**; the half-step is arithmetic.
Corrected, with the example ladder shortened so nobody copies the old shape.

⛔⛔ **Halving the bite doubled how far a player can skip, and that is now an owed ruling.** `δ_max` is the
only bound on skipping ahead. A fresh 3.0 bar offered the **1.0 gap** takes a 0.5 bite to 2.0 and then
lands 1.0 — **two feeds to plate**, against the twelve rolling.md costs that route at. The old model
refused that gap outright and allowed the same trick one gap shallower at four feeds, so the hole is not
new; it is wider, and it now undercuts the feed economy the wide hall is justified by. `MillSchedule.NextGap`
exists, claims in its own doc comment to be what prevents a rung being skipped, and has no caller. Left for
the owner: walk the branch in order, or leave physics as the schedule.

★★ **A rename is only done when the old name still resolves — twice over.** `bloom`/`slab` →
`shingledbar`/`shingledslab` moves two item codes (save data) *and* the form name each rolled piece carries
on its own stack. `StockForm.FormerNames` answers the second (the registry resolves an old name and
`ToStack` rewrites it on the next feed) and `StockFormRenameMigration` derives the first off the same
field, so a third party renaming its own form gets both free and neither file has to be edited again.

⛔ **The stage art did not move with the form.** The bar's ten generated shapes are drawn off a 16-long base
and `ShingledBar.BaseLength` is now 18. Nothing reads a shape's length — the tests measure the base off
disk and scale the rest from it — so the two are internally consistent and disagree only with the settled
form. Item 7 (wire the authored art, which *is* 18) closes it; flagged in three places rather than fixed by
re-generating art that is scheduled for deletion.

The RU/UK strings («Кричный брусок» / «Кричний брусок», «Кричный сляб» / «Кричний сляб») are drafts and
want the author's eye. The slab's old key read "Cast Slab" for the wrought item, which is now fixed.

## 2026-08-12 - the extensibility layer is complete

Tasks 3d through 7. All 15 test targets pass across 1.20/1.21/1.22.

★★ **`MachineJob` turned out to be `ProcessJob`.** The machining line's sketch and the terminal registry
built for the shear are the same shape, so the second type was never written - it gained `minTier` and
`seconds` instead, which is what a machine tool's work costs beyond a crop's. Two schemas for one idea
was the thing to avoid.

★★ **The C# route re-states none of the rules.** `ProcessExtensions.AddStages` / `.AddJobs` build the
declaration a file would have held and run it through the same `TryParse`, throwing on anything the JSON
route would refuse. One set of rules, reached two ways - and the surface cannot drift wider than the
schema, which is the E1 constraint made structural rather than remembered.

★★ **The guard's corpus derives itself.** A file is a process machine because it *reads a process
registry*, not because it is on a list someone has to remember to update. A machine joins the guard by
adopting a registry and cannot be added outside its reach. Mutation-checked red by pointing
`ClaimFinishedPiece` at a literal code.

★★ **`StockForm` is now a registry** - named in machining-line.md as the literal wall on mill
extensibility. A third party's roll set could declare it accepts their stock and the piece still
dead-ended at `WrongForm`, because nothing could add the form itself. Deliberately **no `Clear`**: a mod
emptying the table would take our stock and every shipped roll set with it.

⛔ **Two things deliberately not built**, both the same call as the legacy-`gaps` reader: the shear's
**crop table** (seven of its nine products do not exist, and shipping dangling codes is exactly the
mistake the four roll-set outputs already made once) and **`RenderSpec`** (no machine renders a job, so
every field would be unverifiable design with no consumer). Both recorded where a reader will look.

**The shape sweep landed.** The off-origin convention holds across every file: `CutRod1..4` + `Beam` in
`item-shingled-bar.json`, `CutRivetRod1..4` in `item-rolled-rod.json`, `CutPlate1..2` in
`item-rolled-beam.json` - all the shear's, now tabulated on its page. ⛔ `NailPlate` inside
`item-rolled-rod.json` is not: it is the one whole-piece conversion, so it is a ladder stopping point.

`docs/wiki/Extending-Processes.md` is the page a modder actually lands on - both registry shapes with
copy-pasteable JSON, the generation rules, the C# route, the schema promise, and a symptom table for when
nothing appears.

## 2026-08-12 - every single-sided piece rendered as unworked

Task 3c. `ItemStockPiece.OnBeforeRender` gained the drawn-stage route: tesselate the family's shape file
at the stage's element when the ladder names both, and fall back to the composed mesh otherwise. Drawn
art wins where it exists because a groove is not a thinner rectangle.

⛔ **The bug the task turned up.** The early return was `piece.Sides <= 1 && piece.IsEven`, and a
one-sided piece is **always** even - so a bloom taken from 3.0 down to 2.0 rendered exactly like one
straight off the helve, and the whole composition path only ever ran for the uneven pieces the settled
two-round model is about to make impossible. The test is now "one side, still at the base gauge".
Mutation-checked against the old expression.

⛔⛔ **Thickness alone cannot address a stage.** A stage is (thickness, family), and a fork draws one
gauge two ways - `Grooved200` and `Flattened200` both at 2.0 - so a renderer keyed on thickness is
visibly wrong half the time. `WorkPiece` gained `Family`, written with the reduction in `CompletePass`
and carried on the stack as `rollerFamily`; the mesh cache key includes it, or two branches at one gauge
would share a mesh and show one piece as the other. Absent on an older stack, which reads as "no branch
known" and falls back to the composed mesh rather than guessing - at a fork a guess is wrong half the
time, and wrong art is worse than generic art.

The handbook trap was already avoided (the cache was keyed on geometry, never a per-stack id), and is
now pinned by a test rather than left to a comment.

⛔ A third repo guard earned its keep: shapes load through `ExMeshCache.LoadShape`, never a raw
`ToObject<Shape>()`. Three style/structure guards have now caught things across this plan that no test
would have.

⛔ **`ShapeTextureSource` does not exist on 1.20**, and only `test all` says so - the 1.22 run was green
with it. The texture source is now `capi.Tesselator.GetTextureSource(this)`, which is what every other
tesselation site in the repo already uses. The multi-version constraint is not a formality; this is the
first thing in the whole plan that compiled on the current game and not on the floor.

## 2026-08-12 - items are generated from the catalogue, which had to move to do it

Task 3b. `ProcessItemEmitter` builds an `ExItemDef` per stopping point, injected at 0.04 through the
path `MetalFamilyEmitter` uses. `ProcessItemRenames` turns declared `formerCodes` into item remaps
through `BlockMigrationModSystem`.

⛔⛔ **You cannot build an itemtype from data that lives on an itemtype.** Generation has to run at
ExecuteOrder 0.04, before the patch loader (0.05) and the object loader (0.2) - both confirmed in
`vsessentialsmod/Loading/` rather than assumed. A ladder carried on an item attribute is not readable
in its final form until 0.2, so the emitter would have had to parse raw pre-patch JSON while the
registry parsed resolved collectibles: **two readers of one contract**, which is exactly the shape of
the `.Raw`/`.Attribute` bug earlier the same day. Owner ruling: the ladders move to
`assets/<domain>/config/stageladders/*.json`. The merged-registry ruling is untouched - only the file's
home changed, and the merge still lets two mods contribute to one family.

The catalogue is now read twice by **one** parser: at 0.04 to generate, at `AssetsFinalize` to fill the
registry the machines read (the post-patch one, which the earlier read cannot be). ⛔ Consequence worth
knowing: **patching our catalogue file adds a route but no item**, since the patch lands after
generation. Shipping your own file is the supported route and the merge puts it in the same family.

⛔ **The `game:` domain is never generated into** - a property the metal emitter never needed. Injecting
an itemtype there would replace one of the base game's own items, so a stage naming `game:rod-iron` is
wired up and never built. Mutation-checked, as was the code dedup that stops a fork reaching one
product down two branches from emitting the itemtype twice.

⛔ **Two repo style guards caught what a human reviewer would have**: no marker glyphs and no HTML
emphasis in doc comments. Those are a `docs/` convention and I had carried them into source.

**B3c is dissolved in mechanism, not yet in content**: no shipped rung names a `code`, because every
shipped stage is a shear crop and the shear does not exist. The first real product arrives with the
shear registry.

## 2026-08-12 - schema versioning, and the finding that a spec attribute is not save data

Task 3. `SpecSchema` in exlib holds the contract every spec parser now routes through - `StageLadder`,
`RollSetSpec`, `MoldSpec`. Absent reads as **schema 1** (absent is not unversioned: the form that
shipped before the field existed has a number whether or not it was written down). An older form is
read as declared. ⛔ A **newer** one is **refused**, with an error naming both numbers - we cannot know
what changed, so reading it as the form we do know would mis-parse someone's content silently, and the
fix is on the reader's side.

⛔ **A spec attribute is not save data**, and that resized the whole task. It sits on the itemtype and
is re-read from the declaration every load, so our own emitters never need a fallback - regenerating
the def replaces the old form outright. The burden exists only for declarations **we do not own**, so
it starts at the first schema a third party could have written against. Task 2 could therefore drop
`gaps`/`outputs` outright at schema 1 and still satisfy E3, and the legacy-`gaps` reader the plan
implied was **not built**: dead code from the day it was written, with a maintenance cost forever.

⛔ **The diagram scan carries no schema because it carries no spec.** A `diagram-*` item is identified
by code shape alone - first code part plus a `type` variant - and holds no data to version. Out of
scope by finding rather than by omission; recorded on the design page.

`MoldSpec` gained the field and `PatternItemDefinitions` emits it (pattern golden regenerated). New
`ShippedSpecSchemaGuards` pins that every spec we emit declares `schema` **explicitly**, even though
absent would parse - our JSON is the template a third party copies, and one that omits the field
teaches them to omit it. Mutation-checked by dropping the ladder's `schema`; ⛔ the first cut of its
sibling assertion threw an NRE on that mutant instead of asserting, which reports the wrong failure,
so it now skips what the other guard already names.

**`ExpandedLib/Process/` renamed to `ExpandedLib/Heat/`** (`HeatBalance`, `HeatBalanceHud`) - it sat one
letter from the new `Processes/` and the pair was a readability trap. 11 code references and 5 doc
citations updated.

## 2026-08-12 - the stage ladder is built, and a registry is contributed to rather than owned

Task 2 of the extensibility plan. `gaps` + `outputs` are gone from `RollSetSpec`; the states a piece
passes through are now a stock family's stage ladder, and the mill reads a `MillSchedule` - the branch
of that ladder its fitted set's family accepts. All 15 test targets pass.

★★ **Owner ruling: a process registry is a merged catalogue any collectible contributes to, not a
field on the tooling item.** The design fixed the stage *shape* but not where a declaration hangs, and
the two candidates are not symmetric in cost. Hang the ladder on the roll set and adding a stock family
means patching every set; hang it on the stock and adding a roller family means patching every stock
item. Merging removes the choice, and it is what makes `acceptedBy` per stage rather than per
declaration. Ours ship on the stock items; nothing stops a third party's set from shipping rungs of
its own.

Merging is on **(thickness, accepting family)** - a stage's address, never thickness alone, since a
fork is two families drawing different geometry at one gauge. Unclaimed pair: added. Same pair drawn
the same way: a no-op, so contributing twice is safe and load order does not matter. ⛔ Same pair drawn
differently: **reported, first declaration stands**. Last-writer-wins would make the ladder depend on
mod load order, which nothing can reproduce - and the mutation check confirms two tests catch it.

⛔ **`accepts` stayed on the roll set and is deliberately not derived from the ladder.** They are
independent facts: the ladder says which states the metal has, `accepts` says whether the tooling can
take that stock at all. Derived, the narrow `flat` set (barrel 6) and `flatwide` (barrel 16) share a
family, so `flat` would have started biting slabs.

⛔⛔ **A ladder written with `ExItemDef.Raw` is invisible to the loader.** `Raw` sets a top-level
itemtype key and `CollectibleObject.Attributes` only exposes the `attributes` object, so the first cut
shipped a ladder nothing could read: every feed refused as `WrongForm`. The **golden was happy either
way** - it pins the emitted JSON, not whether the game can find it. The mill fixtures caught it.

⛔ **A test that cannot fail.** `OutputAt(6f / 3f)` was written to prove the gauge match tolerates
float drift, but `6/3` is exactly `2.0f` in IEEE, so it asserted nothing. Replaced with `2.0f ± 1e-5`
(inside the tolerance) plus `2.01f` (outside it, and must stay stock, or a player claims a product one
pass early). Mutating the tolerance to `==` now fails it.

`BlockEntityRollingMill.Ladders` is settable for the same reason `NetworkSystem` is: a machine reading
only a process-wide static cannot have a route stood up for it without writing to the catalogue every
other test shares.

Left alone deliberately: no shipped rung names a `code`, because every shipped stage is a shear crop
and the shear does not exist. `element` and `shape` parse and are carried but nothing consumes them
until the mid-pass rendering task.

## 2026-08-12 - every machine gets a registry, and a stage is a (form, gap) pair

`docs/design/mechanics/process-extension.md` written — the contract a third-party mod writes against.
Linked from framework-composition and machining-line. Design only; no code yet.

★★ **Owner ruling generalising the terminal-vs-sequence axis to every machine.** `machining-line.md`
already named it for `MoldSpec` vs `RollSetSpec`; it now applies throughout. **Terminal** is input →
output **× count**; **sequence** is a ladder carrying state. Only the mill and the bending roller are
sequences. ⛔ The count is load-bearing rather than cosmetic: the shear crops one `rolledrod` into
**four** rods, so a job that could name only one output could not express the crop table at all.

★★ **The stage schema dissolves the `(form, gap)` problem instead of solving it.** A stage carries
`thickness`, `element`, `acceptedBy` and an optional `code` — and a stage already *is* a (form, gap)
pair. `code` present means a stopping point and an item gets generated; absent means a render-only
intermediate. One field drives the item catalogue, the mill's stopping points and the held-item
appearance from a single declaration. What was scheduled as a breaking change is now a side effect of
adopting the right shape.

★★ **`acceptedBy` makes the ladder a graph, not a line** — the grooved/flat fork is simply a stage two
families accept: same feed, same mass, two routes.

⛔ **I mis-read the drawn gaps as a mismatch.** `Grooved275/250/225` are not off-ladder; **a gap costs
two passes** (in, turn, back), so the art is drawn per *pass*: 3.00 → 2.75 → 2.50 is gap 2.5 taken in
two bites. The half-steps are exactly the stages that carry no `code`. Owner correction; the art is
right and no redraw is needed.

⛔ **Mid-pass states cannot be items.** An uneven piece is never claimed, so a half-rolled piece is
the *same code* with a different thickness attribute — rendered by swapping `renderinfo.ModelRef` in
`OnBeforeRender`, as vanilla's `ItemWorkItem` does for its voxel state. Vanilla's own comment carries
the trap: the handbook clones the stack **every frame**, so the no-attribute path must cache under a
stable shared key and never a per-stack mesh-ref id. This is *why* a family's stages belong in one
shape file — the renderer walks the ladder by thickness and needs every step addressable at once.

★★ **Both shape conventions supported**, `element` optional. And the convention behind `CutRod1`:
an element drawn off the shared origin is **not a stage**, it is another machine's output and belongs
in that machine's registry. It recurs in other shape files, so adopting it is a sweep.

The plan's task list is re-cut around this: stage schema, versioning, the item emitter, mid-pass
rendering, then the shear as the first terminal registry. **B3c largely dissolves** — the rolled
catalogue stops being hand-authored item defs and falls out of the mill's and shear's registries.

Link check over every `.md` under `docs/`: 91 broken, unchanged, all pre-existing.

## 2026-08-12 - planning consolidated into docs/internal, and extensibility becomes a target

⛔⛔ **An excluded plan is a plan that can be deleted with no recoverable history.**
`docs/plans/`, `docs/worklog/`, `docs/workbench/` and `docs/vanilla/` were listed in
`.git/info/exclude` - a **local-only** file, so the exclusion was not even shared. That cost us
`docs/design/master-plan.md`: written 2026-07-13, gone from disk, and `git log --all` has no history
for it because it was never committed. Its only surviving trace is an assistant memory note.

It cost duplicated work too. A1's Task 7 included migrating `PossibleOrientations` off
`System.Text.Json`; nothing recorded the task as open, so it was rediscovered two days later through
the § B backlog and investigated from scratch. Same fix, found twice.

All four trees are now under **`docs/internal/`** and no longer excluded. Nothing ships from there -
the packager takes `assets/` and the dlls, never `docs/` - so tracking costs players nothing. Staging
is the owner's call; nothing here staged anything, and the moves were plain filesystem moves rather
than `git mv`, which writes the index.

**Link integrity was the real work.** 70 path citations and 91 up-links inside the moved tree, then
176 relative links pointing *into* it from `docs/design`. A link checker over every markdown file in
`docs/` reports **0 move-related breaks**. It also found **91 pre-existing** broken links, which
predate this and are recorded as a deferred defect rather than quietly fixed.

★★ **`framework-composition-staging.md` now states per-stage status**, which it never did: A0–A4 all
landed 2026-08-10 → 2026-08-12, with the task-detail plan for each named (A2 and A4 have none - they
were executed directly, which is why the arc reads as unfinished from the outside). Its A1 Task 7
note records the split: the `INetworkConnector` narrowing done, the persistence migration *correctly*
never built (the decision reversed to leave persistence on the block entity), and
`PossibleOrientations` two days late.

**Extensibility is now a stated product target** (E1–E4 in STATE.md, plan in
`2026-08-12-extensibility.md`). Other mods must be able to add tooling to diagram crafting, sand
casting, rolling, the steam hammer and the machining line; people asked for this directly.

★★ **The idiom already existed in three places and was deliberate** - rolling reads its schedule off
the fitted set and "never names a product in code"; `PatternItemDefinitions` builds "a mod's whole
`pattern` itemtype from its own mold table"; the diagram picker scans by code with the **domain
deliberately not matched**. What was missing is that nothing *states* the rule, nothing enforces it,
and the wiki has 17 framework pages and zero on any extension point.

Four rulings: both routes (JSON primary, public C# API alongside); the die carries its job spec;
**every spec attribute is versioned**; and the layer lands **before** the tier spine - because the
schemas are a public contract and `RollSetSpec.Outputs` needs a breaking `(form, gap)` key that must
go in before anyone ships a roll set against the old one.

⛔ One consequence worth stating: **our code-first C# defs are a private convenience and the JSON
attribute schema is the actual API.** A third party cannot add an `ExItemDef` to our assembly. The
golden tests protect our authoring, not the contract, so the contract needs its own guards.

This also un-defers the old master plan's **Phase 2 (taxonomy)**. `MetalRegistry`, `ExLiquids` and
the shipped `liquids.json` were parked as "built, adoption partial, low value" when the only consumer
was us; adding a metal or a medium from your own mod is exactly what this target names.
