## 2026-08-06 — CODE + DESIGN: CHARCOAL gets a price — COMPLETE

✅ **All five suites green on 1.21 AND 1.20**: exlib 865 · **iwex 1357** (+21) · **lpex 193** (+1) · hpex 54 ·
**smex 243** (+10). Seven mutations applied and all seven killed (§ *Verify*).

⛔⛔ **The build-out workflow died mid-flight on a session limit, and it left a live mutation in production
code.** Four of its nine agents were killed — three test-writers and the verify pass — and one of them was
inside a mutation check when it went. `BlockEntityFurnaceCore.CarbonPerUnit` was left as
`return 1f; // MUTANT M1` behind a `#pragma warning disable CS0162`, which made **every fuel weigh coke's
carbon**: 11 iwex reds and 1 smex red, all of them looking like ordinary test failures in the new charcoal
cases. ★ The lesson is not "don't mutate" — the mutation was *doing its job*, and 11 tests caught it. It is
that **a killed agent leaves no `finally`**: a mutation harness that edits the tree in place must restore
from a copy in a process the model does not control. The mutation pass below was re-run through a script
whose restore is in a `finally`.

### ⛔⛔ The request was not the feature. Charcoal already worked — unpriced.

The user asked to "also accept charcoal" in the blast furnaces and cupola. A survey found it has been a fully
accepted shaft fuel since the charge-column cutover: `assets/iwex/config/materialroles.json` grants
`game:charcoal` the `fuel` role, and `IsFuelCode` is pure registry membership. So a charcoal-charged shaft
**lit, burned, made gas, descended and yielded iron at coke's exact rate**, rendered as coke pixel-for-pixel
(`BlockChargePile.ElementOf` is a two-way classifier), and the HUD called it coke. ★ The work is not letting it
in — it is **charging for it**.

### What landed

| | |
|---|---|
| `materialroles.json` | charcoal `0.5 → 1.0` against coke's `2.0` — a **2:1** ratio, ruled by the user |
| `IwexConfig.BfFuelCarbonReference = 2f` | which fuel the calibration is *written in*; move it and every coke figure changes meaning |
| `BlockEntityFurnaceCore.CarbonPerUnit()` | **the** weight function: role value ÷ reference. Coke 1.0, charcoal 0.5, non-fuel 0 |
| `BlockEntityFurnaceCore.IsFuelStack()` | folded the **three** duplicate spellings of "consult `Roles.Fuel`" onto one predicate |
| `Accumulate` | `fuel += units * CarbonPerUnit(...)` — the heat balance now reads **carbon**, not bands |
| `BurnCarbon` | converts **per segment inside the walk** — a raceway slice routinely spans a coke course and a charcoal one |

★ **Coke is byte-identical**: its weight computes to exactly 1.0, and the whole suite stayed green through the
change. ⓘ No new carry was needed — the existing `_burnCarry` absorbs the fractional remainder a sub-1.0 fuel
leaves, which is why the *carbon-density* model was chosen over the user's literal *items-per-band* one. The
latter needed a new fractional currency, serialized carries on both hoppers and three inverse conversions;
both land on the same 2× per-item economy.

⛔ **Why band-counting must not ship**: under a volume-weighted `fuelFrac`, charcoal at 30 % reads 30 %, melts
at coke's temperature, makes the same iron from the same items and merely finishes sooner — a **pure speed
buff** wearing a penalty's clothes.

### ⚠ The user corrected a premise I had priced wrongly

I framed the 2:1 choice as costing the ore mixer's 4× charcoal tax. **There is no mixer.**
`docs/design/layered-charge.md` § *The burdenmaker* already deletes `BlockOreMixer`/`BlockEntityOreMixer`, the
ore bunker and **the burden fuel stamp** together — *"once coke leaves the burden, proportioning collapses to a
single ratio, and a machine that measures stops being necessary."* Fuel is charged directly at the furnace's
hopper as its own bands. `machines/burdenmaker.md` is designed with art drawn (2026-07-31);
`machines/ore-mixer.md` already reads *survival-unobtainable*. ⛔ **I should have found this before asking** —
the ratio was free, not a trade.

⛔⛔ **And the mixer's fuel input is worse than dead today.** Since U3 ruled that carbon comes from fuel bands
and nowhere else, a burden's stamped `Mix.Fuel` contributes **nothing** to any furnace — so coke or charcoal
put into the mixer is consumed to stamp a number nothing reads. **Player fuel destroyed for zero effect, in
shipped code.** No test says so, because every mixer test asserts the stamp arithmetic rather than what a
furnace does with the stamp.

★ **The yield trap is not there, and that is ruling 4 paying off.** Taking fuel out of the burden makes each
unit richer in ore but produces fewer units, and `BfIronPerOreUnit` is stated *per unit of ore content*, so the
two cancel exactly: `16 × 8.5 × 0.75 = 102` and `13 × 8.5 × 0.923 = 102`. **No recalibration.** And the 3 coke
that used to vanish into a stamp goes into the shaft as a band, where it burns.

### THREE shipped bugs — all fixed

1. ⛔⛔ **A code literal where a role test belongs — and it is the one a second fuel exists to expose.**
   `NextChargeColumn`'s band-order rule read `top == material` where the method's own doc said *"fuel only
   onto burden"*. Those two readings give the **identical answer for every case a one-fuel shaft can
   produce** — coke onto coke is refused either way — so nothing in 1300 tests could see it. With charcoal
   priced, a hopper of charcoal laid a second fuel course straight onto the coke one: nothing throws, the
   units add up, the bands draw, the furnace **lights**, and it can never make iron because there is no ore
   between its fuel. Now `IsFuelCode(column.TopMaterial)`. ★ This was found by a test the killed agent had
   written and never run.
2. ⛔ **The hot blast furnace could not be fuelled at all** — and it had **two** halves, not the one I
   reported. `BlockEntityHopperReinforced.Accepts` *and* `BlockHopperReinforced.OnBlockInteractStart:69`
   both gated on `Burden.IsAny`, so fixing only the block entity would have shipped half a fix (a plain
   click with coke in hand did nothing; ctrl-click toggled the bell). The BE now carries the same
   `MultiblockAnchorLink` the bell keeps and delegates to `core.IsChargeItem`. **Untestable through the
   shipped route before** because the smex fixtures `Push` into columns directly.
3. ⛔ **Every fuel band in a cupola read as wrong-family charge.** `PileHoldsRejectedCharge` lacked the
   `Burden.IsAnyCode` guard `ReadChargeMix` has, and `Burden.FamilyOfCode` answers "ore" for any fuel. ★ The
   consequence was worse than "salvage survives": with no free pool cell `SolidifyBottomLayer` froze nothing
   and `ClearMoltenPools` then zeroed the bath — **the player lost the whole molten charge**. Only reachable
   by breaking a *running* cupola, which is what the new case does.

### Verify — seven mutations, seven killed

Run by hand after the workflow's verify agent died. Each applies one edit, **asserts the text actually
landed** before trusting any result, runs the owning suite, and restores in a `finally`.

| | Mutation | Killed by |
|---|---|---|
| M1 | band order back to `top == material` | 1 — `Charcoal_will_not_go_onto_a_column_topped_with_COKE` |
| M2 | `Accumulate` reads bands, not carbon | 3 |
| M3 | `BurnCarbon` spends bands, not carbon | 2 |
| M4 | charcoal draws as coke | 3 |
| M5 | reinforced hopper back to burden-only | 5 |
| M6 | drop the cupola's `IsAnyCode` guard | 1 |
| M8 | an unpriced fuel falls back to **coke** rather than charcoal | 1 |

⓵ M8 is the one worth keeping: "an unpriced fuel is the standard fuel" reads perfectly sensibly and hands
every careless content mod a free 2× on carbon. A furnace running *hotter* than its charge justifies has no
symptom a player can attribute; one running cooler is a charge they can see and fix.

### Fixture defects found (the reason no test could see any of this)

⛔ All three rigs read carbon as `segment.Material == "game:coke"` while their own doc-comments claimed to match
production — which asks `IsFuelCode`. A string compare answers **no** for charcoal, so every campaign-length
assertion would have read zero carbon on a charcoal furnace and passed for the wrong reason. Now split into
`FuelBandUnits` (column volume) and `CarbonUnits` (weighted) — ⚠ conflating those two **is** the double-count
this model already made once. `SalvageAtLocal`'s fuel-skip was the same literal, and would have returned an
empty mix for every charcoal furnace, silently, in the direction that reads as lost salvage.

ⓘ Also fixed: `MaterialRoleSeeds` was missing `game:metalbit-steel` while its own doc claimed it mirrored the
shipped asset "entry-for-entry" — nothing asserted the correspondence, so headless steel bits were not scrap.
`FuelRoleGrantTests.Seed_matches_the_shipped_materialroles_json` now compares the two as **sets in both
directions**, so a seed row that no longer ships fails as loudly as a shipped row that is not seeded.

ⓘ And lpex was getting iwex's fuel roles **only by side effect** — its csproj references
`IronworkingExpanded.Tests` for `PipeTestWorld`, so iwex's module initializer fired on first touch of an iwex
type. Reverting the new explicit seed left the suite green, which is the proof. With no `fuel` grant every
coke band weighs zero carbon, `RacewayHoldsCarbon` is false forever, and the cupola sits at `Idle` while the
assertion blames the furnace.

### Rendering, HUD and strings

* **Charcoal has its own stripe.** `chargepile.json` gained a third `Charcoal` element and `ElementOf` is
  three-way. ⛔ Deliberately **not** `Retextured`: the mesh cache key is the element name, so a retextured
  charcoal would collide with coke on `"chargepile-Coke"`. The element and the key are hoisted into one local
  so they cannot drift, and a test pins that the shipped shape really carries three distinctly-textured
  elements. Keyed on the material **code**, not the role — `ElementOf` runs on the tesselation thread, a role
  lookup is not total (a fuel the config forgot would draw *nothing*), and an element is art: there is no
  element to interpolate to for a value between 1.0 and 2.0.
* **The course line names the fuel the player laid.** `ChargeCourse` gained `FuelMaterial`; one fuel → the
  item's own display name, several → `mixed fuel`, none → `fuel`. Compared as `AssetLocation`, so `coke` and
  `game:coke` are not "mixed". ★ A per-fuel counter was refused as a closed set — the name comes off the
  item, so vanilla's fuels are already translated in every locale and a third mod's fuel names itself.
* ⛔ **`bf-info-heatin` was wrong twice.** It said `coke {1}%` while being fed `hb.FuelFrac`, which since
  `Accumulate` started weighting is a **carbon** fraction, not a volume fraction of coke. Now "carbon".
* en/ru/uk all updated; single `-` throughout, no key removed.

### Next

**The burdenmaker** — its own unit, not smuggled into this one. It deletes the mixer, the bunker, the burden
fuel stamp, the `ore`/`remelt` family lock and `iwex:remeltburden`, and moves the four coke grades onto the
furnace where the player sets them by how they charge.

## 2026-08-06 — CODE + DESIGN: U3.6b — the 38 red cases are GREEN, and two of them were hiding a broken model

✅ **All five suites green on 1.21 AND 1.20**: exlib 865 · **iwex 1336** · lpex 192 · hpex 54 · **smex 233**.
The 38 red cases the derived state broke are rewritten, and `State` now has **no setter at all**.

### The law, and why a private setter was not enough

`FurnaceBranchGuards.NoFurnaceExposesASettableState` — no furnace in any mod may expose a settable `State`,
public, protected **or private**. ⛔ `ReflectionHelpers.SetProperty` reaches non-public setters, so
`private set` would have stopped production code and *nothing* in a fixture. With no setter the same call
**throws**, and it caught two call sites the moment it landed. 27 fixture call sites existed; every one of
them would have gone on passing if the setter had been kept "so the fixtures compile".

### ⛔⛔ Two defects the rewrite exposed, and the second was the model

The tests could not be written honestly against what was in the tree, which is how both were found.

**1. The melt was destroying coke the raceway never burned.** `MeltableUnits` counted fuel bands as "melted"
and `ChargeColumn.Take` removed them — on top of the carbon `CirculateGas` was burning. Traced: **0.36 u/s
while `Firing` and 1.7 u/s the instant it read `Melting`**. So two thirds of a campaign's coke vanished
unburnt, the furnace went out with a third of its burden unmelted, and *"campaign length = coke charged"* —
the headline ruling of `layered-charge.md` § *What sets the rate: the blast* — was false for most of a
furnace's life. Nothing observable said so; the furnace simply ran short. The melt is a **mid-span rewrite**
now (coke is stepped over, never taken), because a round is coke *then* burden and `Take` cannot skip what it
is standing on.

**2. Fixing that exposed the real one: there were TWO throttles.** With coke no longer destroyed by the melt,
burden cleared out of the raceway ~10× faster than the coke beneath it — so coke **banked up** in front of the
tuyeres until no burden was left in the span at all, and the furnace pulsed `Melting`/`Firing` across the gap.
⚠ **User ruled three things** (all written up in `layered-charge.md`, with the numbers):

| Ruling | What it replaces |
|---|---|
| **Production is metered by the carbon**: `carbonBurned × BfBurdenPerCarbonUnit × MeltSpeedFactor` | `BfRacewayMeltUnitsPerSecond`, an independent rate ~5× out of step with the burn |
| **The blast is metered per TUYERE** | one whole-furnace rate, which made the 16-column hot furnace burn exactly as much as the 1-column cupola |
| **The cupola keeps the raceway model and gets a unit SCALE** — `layered-charge.md` Open #4, answered YES | a cupola that, at 3 000 u a block against constants calibrated for 32, **could never melt anything** |

★ 4.0 is the reference grade read as a ratio (80 % burden : 20 % coke), so a furnace eats the column in the
proportion the player laid it. Charge richer → coke banks up; charge leaner → the chill. ⓘ `MeltSpeedFactor`
multiplies it, which is Neilson: a hotter furnace gets more iron from the same carbon, so it *wants* a leaner
charge.

⚠ **The spend had to be ROTATED across the columns, not divided between them.** Coke is items and a band burns
in whole units, so an even split hands each column ~0.04 u a second — truncating to nothing every tick, riding
the carry for ~26 s, then burning every column at once. The furnace rendered its whole minute's iron in
twenty-six-second gulps. Rotating the start makes it one unit every three seconds.

### Mutation-checked, and one mutation SURVIVED

Four mutations, three caught immediately (the melt eating coke → 3 red; the label ignoring the family gate →
1 red; the cupola's unit scale pinned at 1 → 4 red). ⛔ **The fourth survived**: dropping `× tuyeres` from the
carbon rate left **all five suites green**. That is the whole point of the ruling — a bigger furnace being a
faster furnace — and nothing anywhere pinned it. `The_blast_meters_the_carbon_at_the_rate_per_tuyere_it_advertises`
now does, against the furnace's own scanned tuyere list rather than a literal, and it fails under the mutation.

### Retired rules the fixtures were still asserting

⛔ **The quantity threshold does not gate ignition** — `A_shaft_below_the_fire_threshold_never_lights` became
`A_full_shaft_with_no_coke_at_its_raceway_never_lights`, whose burden is still *stamped* at 30 % coke so it
discriminates a band read from a stamp read. ⛔ **A stopped blower throttles the fire, it does not snuff it** —
`Air_starvation_extinguishes_the_furnace_after_the_grace_window` is now
`A_starved_blast_line_throttles_the_furnace_rather_than_extinguishing_it`. ⛔ **A burden's stamped coke is not
carbon** — three mix-reading cases were asserting the double-count that made a 30 % charge burn at ~50 %.

★ A new behaviour worth naming: a **standard burden on cold blast now CHILLS OUT** rather than burning for
ever. Its burden never melts, so the column never makes room to descend, so the fire eats the carbon it can
reach and then finds burden where the next coke course should be. Nothing in the code names it — it is what
*"descent is a consequence, never a rate"* produces.

ⓘ Also: `DeriveState` carries the family gate deliberately (a furnace that will render nothing reads `Firing`,
not `Melting`); `TransitionToMelting_moves_firing_into_melting` was **deleted** rather than ported (it drove
the firebox's machinery through a shaft class); and the serialization round-trip arranges its state by
*loading* it, which exercises one more link than an assignment did.

## 2026-08-06 — CODE: U3.5b — a breached furnace keeps BURNING (and the rule had THREE gates, not one)

⚠⚠ **STILL RED, and more so than before**: iwex 1327/1334 (7), lpex 186/192 (6), smex 208/233 (25).
exlib and hpex green. The growth is expected — the derived state below reaches three suites — but nothing
here is finished. **Do not read the tree as done.**

⛔⛔ **`layered-charge.md`'s one-line instruction is not sufficient, and neither is the plan's two-line
version.** The rule ("a furnace that loses its walls keeps burning; a furnace that loses its *blast* goes
out — the two failure modes are opposites") needed **three** changes, in three files, at three layers, and
each one is individually a no-op:

1. `OnStructureLost` stops calling `Extinguish()` — the design's instruction. **On its own: no effect.**
2. `OnProductionTick`'s `!StructureComplete` guard becomes "return only if idle" — the plan's addition.
   **Also no effect**, because the method was not being called.
3. ⛔ **`BlockEntityMultiblockStructure.StopProductionTick()` on structure loss — the actual blocker, in
   *exlib*, which neither the design page nor either audit mentions.** The listener is **unregistered**, so
   `CanRunProduction` is never consulted at all.

★★ **The failure mode is why this needed a behavioural test rather than a call-site check.** A furnace that
does not tick is **frozen**, not extinguished: it holds its temperature, its charge and its pool for ever.
From outside, frozen and extinguished are the same picture, and `Assert.NotEqual(Idle, State)` passes on
both — `State` is stored, so it survives a tick that never runs. The only observable that separates them is
that a burning furnace is **still consuming**, which is what the test asserts. ⓘ It found gate 3 by reading
"567 units vs 567 units" three times while each of gates 1 and 2 was fixed.

★ New exlib seam `StopsProductionOnStructureLost` (default true, false on the furnace core), with the trap
written on it: **a machine that must keep running while broken has to opt out there, not in
`CanRunProduction`** — the gate is only read by a listener that still exists, so overriding the gate alone
goes quiet with no error and no test failure.

ⓘ Also built: `blastSupplyFrac` is forced to **0** while incomplete, so a breach runs on natural draught
alone (~970 °C, the pinned row) — it burns its coke off and renders nothing. And a breached **idle** furnace
cannot be lit through the hole, which is the clause that keeps breach and choke distinguishable.
⚠ That last case is staged on the permanently-incomplete scene, not by breaching a complete one: the
structure monitor only re-runs every few seconds, so a complete furnace lights on its **first** tick and is
already burning before a breach could be noticed. Correct, and untestable the other way.

## 2026-08-06 — CODE: U3.6 (in flight) — the shaft's state machine is GONE

⚠⚠ **IN FLIGHT AND RED.** iwex 1325/1332 (7 failing, all `ColdBlastFurnaceScenarioTests` lifecycle cases);
smex 7 + lpex 1 still red from earlier. **Do not read this as finished.** The 7 are the break the plan
predicted in terms — every one of them arranges or asserts the stored FSM.

★★ **Prompted by the user, and they were right to push:** I had been patching *around* the state machine —
`MeltStartDelay => 0`, an `AtMeltingTemperature` seam — when the plan's own U3.6 deletes it. Each patch was
a half-measure the next task removes, and each one exposed the next symptom.

**What the shaft does now:** `DeriveState(chargeHandle)` every tick — **Idle** when nothing is burning,
**Melting** when what has arrived at a raceway is hot enough to render, **Firing** in between. Nothing
stored, nothing counting down.

⛔ **Five timers and two thresholds retired on this branch**, each of which was standing in for something
the counter-current model now does for real:

| Timer | What actually decides it now |
|---|---|
| `_fuelBurnSeconds` / `BfMaxFuelBurnTime` | carbon at the raceway runs out |
| `_secondsAboveMelting` / `BfMeltStartDelay` | the charge warming through — which is the soak, for real |
| `_meltSeconds` / `BfMeltIntervalSec` | the descent; whatever passed the raceway this second |
| `_belowMeltingSeconds` | next tick simply reads `Firing` again; there is nothing to reset |
| `_extinguishSeconds` / both thresholds | choked or out of carbon, read directly |

★ **The firebox keeps its FSM**, gated on a new `DerivesState` seam that is false there — a fuel bed has no
raceway and no descent to derive anything from, and `FireboxTickTests` is what proves the branch survived.
ⓘ `Extinguish()` splits: `Shutdown()` is everything going out *does* (sound, ambient, residue, counters),
without deciding that it went out, because a derived branch cannot assign a state.

⛔ **A consequence worth stating plainly: the quantity threshold no longer gates ignition.** A furnace runs
while there is carbon in front of **every** tuyere and air to burn it — positional and pneumatic, exactly as
ruled. `BlastMixRequiredToFire` survives only as `ComputeHeatBalance`'s cold-charge denominator, which is
U3.7's split. `A_shaft_below_the_fire_threshold_never_lights` is therefore asserting a **retired rule**, not
catching a regression.

⚠ **Still owed, and ordered:** U3.5b (breach — `OnStructureLost` must stop calling the extinguish path, and
the tick guard must return only when idle) was supposed to land *before* this; it did not, and it must land
before the 7 red cases are rewritten, or the wrong breach behaviour gets baked into the derived path with a
green suite. **No test in any suite asserts what a breached lit furnace does.**

## 2026-08-06 — CODE + DESIGN: U3.2b — the BLAST drives the burn (user finding)

**iwex green: 1332.** smex + lpex still red (8) — U3.8's. ⓘ Design written up first, in
`layered-charge.md` § *What sets the rate: the blast*, with a table of the real-process references it
corresponds to. The user asked for it there rather than in code comments, and they were right: this is a
ruling, and rulings live in `docs/design/`.

⛔⛔ **Two defects the user spotted in what U3.2 shipped, and I had missed both:**

1. **Gas was made from coke *present*, not coke *burned*.** `CirculateGas` read the raceway slice and never
   spent it, so a thick coke course heated a shaft for ever off carbon that never ran out.
2. **Coke was only consumed inside the melt cycle, which only runs while `Melting`.** So a `Firing` furnace
   consumed *nothing* and campaign length was `BfMaxFuelBurnTime`, a timer — the exact opposite of what
   `layered-charge.md` says (*"Campaign length = coke charged | nothing else burns"*).

★★ **Root cause: nothing decided a burn RATE.** The thing that should is already in the tick and was going
unused for it — **the blast**. Air is a reagent; a furnace burns exactly as much carbon as it has oxygen
for, which is why the blowing engine is the throttle in a real works and why the blast:coke ratio is the
headline operating figure. Carbon per second is now `BfRacewayCarbonPerSecond × HeatBalance.AirFactor`, and
everything follows from that one number: flame temperature, how much gas rises, how far the column
descends, and campaign length. ⓘ Verified in a trace — a full shaft goes out at ~540 s, which is its
carbon divided by the burn rate. `BfMaxFuelBurnTime` is now redundant rather than needing deletion.

⚠ **Descent is a consequence, never a rate.** Nothing pushes the column down; it falls into the space left
by coke that burned and burden that melted and ran out of the taps. That is also *why a chilled column
hangs* — its burden did not melt, so no space appeared. A `units/second` descent constant cannot express
that at all.

⛔⛔ **And the fixtures were charging a fiction.** Every scene laid burden-only, with the coke inside the
stamp. Coke stamped into a burden band **cannot burn away**, so it makes no void, so nothing descends: a
trace showed the bottom band burning off its own carbon in ~300 s, stalling at 1248 °C against a 1482 °C
melt line, and the shaft locking solid with every band above it holding coke that could never come down.
★ **The model was right and the charge was wrong.** The hopper has laid coke as its own bands since U2;
only the fixtures were still pre-U5. `Lay` now lays real rounds — a coke course, then a burden course — and
the whole campaign runs: warms through in ~200 s, melts from ~360 s, 2 000 u of pig, out when the carbon
is gone. ⓘ This is the mechanical argument for U5 that the design only had a tidiness argument for.

★ **Is it physical? Yes** — and the design page now names the correspondences: the raceway as a real
cavity, blast:coke, counter-current exchange, the thermal reserve zone, burden descent rate, and the **Rist
diagram** as the formal reference for what sets the coke rate. ⛔ **Solution loss** (Boudouard,
C + CO₂ → 2CO) is deliberately omitted and the page says why: carbon *is* consumed above the raceway in
reality, but **endothermically**, so modelling it as burning inverts the sign on the one mechanism the
model exists to produce.

---

## 2026-08-06 — CODE: U3.2 + U3.3 — the raceway, the counter-current pass, and the melt condition

⚠ **IN FLIGHT. iwex is green (1332); smex and lpex are RED (7 + 1) and that is expected** — the plan says
in terms to expect it until U3.8, which rewrites their fixtures. All eight either call `SmeltCycle` on its
old signature or arrange cold charge and expect an immediate melt. **Do not read the tree as finished.**

⛔ **U3.2 and U3.3 had to land TOGETHER, and the plan splitting them is a defect.** Removing the
first-order chase (U3.2) while the melt condition still keys off `_internalTemp` (U3.3's job) leaves the
furnace with *no warm-up at all*: the flame is instant, so `_secondsAboveMelting` starts accruing on tick 1
and melting begins 145 s earlier than shipped. Caught by two scenarios; a trace showed the furnace burning
its whole shaft inside their window and going out.

**The model, in three sentences.** All the coke burns at the raceway — the lowest **whole round** of each
column, which is one charge block. The gas that makes rises through every band above it, warming each
toward the flame and arriving at the stockline with whatever is left. Burden melts iff *the temperature it
carried down the shaft* clears the melting point.

⛔ **The design's "carried temperature + heat from the coke burning with it" is ONE term, not two.** That
coke's heat is already in the band — the gas pass delivered it, and the gas is that same coke's. A second
additive term counts the same carbon twice, and nothing would go red; the furnace would just quietly run
richer than it was calibrated to.

★★ **The raceway slice is derived, not a config key.** A round is the coke-to-coke span and block
boundaries quantise nothing, so no constant can track the player's actual rounds — but one **block** is the
span the rest of the model already speaks in: what the hopper's `N coke, M burden of 16` readout counts,
what a player digs out in one break, what burn-out interpolates over. It follows `ChargeUnitsPerBlock`, so
the cupola's remelt quantum moves it with no second override.

⛔⛔ **`RiseGasThrough` lumped a whole band, and a uniformly-charged shaft therefore had NO temperature
profile.** The cold furnace charges each column in one call — one 71-unit band — so the entire column
climbed together and nothing melted until *all* of it reached the melting point. Found by a scenario, not
by review. The pass now resolves at **one block**, splitting as it goes, which is the resolution the wall
draws at and the glow reads at. ⓘ `Coalesce` is **deleted**: laying each piece back through `Push`'s own
`Append` merges them where they are produced, so the separate tidy-up pass had nothing left to do and one
fewer call site can be forgotten. The bound it existed for still holds — 240 drips leave **151** bands.

★★ **The yield is stated per unit of ORE CONTENT (`BfIronPerOreUnit = 8.5`), not per band.** The plan ruled
`BfIronPerBurdenBand = 6.375` and then flagged that it must be re-derived after U5. It does not need
re-deriving — it needs a different **currency**. `6.375 = 8.5 × IronFrac`, and `IronFrac` is exactly what U5
moves (0.75 → ~0.94 once coke leaves the burden), so *any* per-band or per-charge-unit figure is a number
with a live dependency baked into it. Per ore unit, recovery per nugget **is** the constant, U5 changes
nothing, and the guard-rail reads one value instead of deriving one from three. ⓘ This lands the designed
**8.5 u/nugget** and retires the shipped bloomery **parity** of 5.0.

⚠ **The cupola is deliberately NOT on that ladder.** It *remelts* rather than reduces, so there is no ore
fraction in its charge for a recovery figure to be stated against; applying a reduction yield to metal that
was never ore would be nonsense the guard-rail is not even about. Its pair is kept and re-expressed per
unit, which preserves its ratio exactly.

**Also in:** the **glow** — `BlockChargePile.GetLightHsv` off the hottest band a block draws, so the
profile is finally visible instead of being simulated and saved where no player can see it. ⚠ Its redraw
had to learn to **diff** first: the gas pass moves every band's temperature *every second* and ends with a
`SyncChargeBlocks`, so an unconditional `MarkDirty` is thirty-six packets a second for one blast furnace.

⚠ **Two scenarios stopped waiting a fixed 600 s.** That wait is now wrong in both directions — the furnace
can reach Melting well before it, and because a campaign ends when the coke does rather than when a timer
does, it can have finished the whole shaft by then. ⓘ One of them waits on the **product** rather than the
state: entering `Melting` and having melted something are now different moments.

## 2026-08-06 — CODE: U3.1 — the column learns to be warmed (`RiseGasThrough` + `Coalesce`)

**All five suites green on 1.20 and 1.21 — 865 + 1329 + 192 + 54 + 233 = 2 673, zero skips.**
The counter-current operation itself, pure and world-free. Nothing invokes it yet; U3.2 does.

**The rule.** Gas leaves the raceway (index 0) and rises, and **each UNIT of charge takes
`transferFraction` of whatever excess the gas still carries** — so a band of `U` units takes
`1 - (1-f)^U`. ★★ Compounding per *unit* rather than per band is the load-bearing choice: the segment list
is cut wherever charging and `Take` happened to leave it, and `Coalesce` re-cuts it every tick, so a
per-band rule would make the shaft's efficiency depend on the player's **charging rhythm**. Split any band
in two and the gas leaves at the same temperature having given up the same heat; only the resolution of the
internal gradient changes. Pinned, and the mutation that drops the `Pow` fails exactly that test.

**dt rides on the capacity, not on a rate.** The gas crosses the shaft in seconds while the burden descends
over hours, so within a tick the flow is quasi-steady: what scales with the step is *how much gas passed*.
`MaxAwayCatchupSteps` replays up to 600 one-second sub-ticks at chunk load, so this is the difference
between a shaft that reloads correctly and one that melts itself. One pass at capacity C lands within 2 % of
two passes at C/2, and the residual is the explicit step's own second-order error, stated in the test.

⛔ **The `ambientFloor` parameter the task specified is DELETED, and a mutation is why.** Clamping each band
up to ambient reads harmless and is not — the shaft gains warmth the gas never handed over, so a column can
reach the raceway hotter than the coke charged with it could ever have made it. It was also **dead**: the
gas that reaches a band always leaves it above ambient anyway, and on the paths where a floor would bite
(no gas at all) the method returns before touching a band. Removing it left a gap with no test, so it was
replaced by the invariant it was violating — **the column gains exactly the heat the gas lost**.
ⓘ What the floor was reaching for is a *different* operation, a charged column losing heat to the world once
the fire dies. That is `layered-charge.md` **Open #1**, still open, and deliberately not built here.

⚠ **The task's "a taller column's bottom ends hotter" is not true of a single pass, and asserting it would
have been a lie.** Band 0 sees the same gas at the same capacity whatever stands above it. What a taller
shaft actually buys is that a parcel charged at its stockline waits through **more passes** before descending
to the raceway — a descent-time integral, which belongs on the furnace and lands in U3.2/U3.3. What *is* a
column-level fact, and is what the test now pins: the same gas leaves a taller column **cooler**, and the
taller column keeps more of it. A well-charged tall furnace exhausting cooler is the model's own signature.

⚠ **A band the gas cannot warm is passed, not stopped at.** The flame falls whenever the blast or the coke
fraction does, so a band carrying heat from a hotter stretch of the campaign sitting under a cooler gas is
ordinary — and it sits at the **raceway**, so `break` would freeze the whole shaft above it for the rest of
the campaign. ★ My first test for this could not tell `break` from `continue` (it never reached the branch);
the mutation is what exposed it.

**The save leak is real and now measured.** Without a coalescing pass, 240 hopper drips onto a lit shaft
leave **240 bands — one apiece**, because `Push` merges only within a degree and warming moves the top band
out of reach on the first tick. Each band is four more parallel array entries per column, on every column,
riding the chunk save all campaign. With `Coalesce` the same run leaves **151**; the rest is clawed back by
the model itself, since a shaft deep enough to strip the gas before the stockline stops warming its top band
by a whole degree and fresh drips merge again. ⓘ `Coalesce` reuses `Push`'s own `Append`, so there is no
second copy of the merge rule to drift.

★ **Four mutations, four bites** (per-band transfer · no equilibrium clamp · `break` instead of `continue` ·
`Coalesce` no-op), plus the NaN guard and the walk direction. Two of them — the floor and the `break` — were
tests that passed against a broken implementation until the mutation was run.

### The other U3 prerequisite: the firebox's temperature transient

`FireboxTickTests` already pinned the three **cadence** keys the branch reads (built 2026-08-04). It did not
pin the **chase** — `_internalTemp` creeping toward `HeatBalance.TProcess` at `BfHeatRatePerSecond` — and
U3.2 takes that off the shaft branch, because a shaft's thermal inertia is carried on its segments instead.
⛔ `BlockEntityFireboxFurnace` **overrides no tick at all**, so deleting the chase rather than pushing it
down makes every reverberatory hearth, the reheat furnace and both U9 machines reach their ceiling **in one
tick** — and nothing goes red, because the end state is identical and only the journey to it disappears.
*"Buy temperature by building the chimney taller"* becomes a step function with no observable transient,
which is U6.5's entire deliverable. One test, mutation-checked against `_internalTemp = targetTemp`.

## 2026-08-06 — CODE: U2 FINISHED — bell hopper, charging readout, and the coal-pile path deleted outright

**All five suites green on 1.20 and 1.21 — 865 + 1306 + 192 + 54 + 233 = 2 650, zero skips.**
U2.8 + U2.9 + U2.10 + U2.11, on top of the cutover below.

### U2.8 — the bell hopper drips into columns

smex's bell kept a **second copy** of "where does a load land": a hard-coded `Pos.DownCopy(2)`/`(3)` plane
scan, fifteen cells deep, looking for vanilla coal piles. It now resolves the core through the same
multiblock anchor every furnace part uses and asks `NextChargeColumn` — so **both hoppers fill a shaft by
one rule**, which is a thing no assertion on either alone could see. `IsFurnaceFull` became "every column at
**its own** capacity"; a single whole-furnace ceiling reads full while the taller columns still have room.

⛔ **Its `iwex:blastmix` break drop was two defects in one line.** It minted a *different item* from the
graded burden the magazine holds (so the player lost the mix the ore mixer stamped), and it resolved through
a null-guarded `world.GetItem(...)` — so deleting the item in U2.10 would have made a break **silently
return nothing**, with the smex build green and no test red. It drops the magazine's own stack now, grade and
all. ⓘ Also found: `base.GetDrops` returns **null** for a block with no drops and the old code fed it
straight to a `List<>` ctor — a definition change would have turned "break this block" into a crash.

### U2.9 — the charging readout

Charging is a skill now, so a player who cannot see what they laid is charging blind. Three new derived
lines beside the existing count — **course** (`N coke, M burden of 16`), **verdict**, **fullness** — and not
one persisted field. ★ The verdict reads `ComputeHeatBalance` rather than re-deriving a coke threshold, so
it cannot drift from the model and U3.2 moves it for free; its assumption ("on full blast, at ambient" — the
**cold** reading, so it never promises a melt the furnace cannot deliver) is written on the member.

⚠ `TopCourse` is public for the reason `RenderSlabs` is: the headless lang service echoes a key and **drops
its arguments**, so a test reading only the rendered string could tell that a line is present and never that
its numbers are right.

⚠ Two of my own test bugs, both caught by the assertions rather than by review — and both were the *test*
being wrong, not the code. The course case charged one column and expected its course, but the readout
follows the **lowest** column, which was one of the eight empty ones. The verdict case charged thin and got
"melts": the cold-charge loss scales with how much charge is in there, so a thin shaft of the very same
burden genuinely runs hotter. Both now say so in the test.

### U2.10 — the deletions

`CoalPileBlastmixPatches.cs` (the whole Harmony side-table), `ItemBlastmix` + its golden + nine lang keys +
`patches/vanilla/coalpile.json`, the `charge` material role's only grant, and `BlastmixBurnTime`.

**Two rulings written down rather than left to be inferred:**

* ⛔ **`blastmix` is NOT remapped, and it is not migrated to burden either.** The item is gone, so a remap
  has nothing to resolve onto — and the walk enumerates *registered* items, so a stale entry would simply
  never fire, silently. Pointing it at `iwex:burden` would be worse: burden carries a stamped mix and blast
  mix carried none, so the player would get a burden of an **invented grade**. The stack is dropped; the
  loss is bounded (a cheap mixer intermediate, and the mixer itself goes in U5). Pinned by a case that
  *does* register the item, which is the only way this can regress.
* ⛔ **`iwex:slag-block` keeps its block, BE, lang key and drops with no in-world producer.** Its only writer
  was `BlastmixPiles.ConvertToSlag` — the unmanaged-firing path the layered charge retired. It is a live
  **migration target** (existing worlds have them standing), and nothing on the extinguish path has ever
  made slag: a furnace that goes out hands its burden back as salvage. Recorded on the class so a later
  cleanup does not read it as dead.

⚠ `MixerRoleParityTests.Charge_role_matches_the_old_blastmix_token` was **retired, not edited**: its oracle
was the literal `Path == "blastmix"`, so keeping it green by changing the expectation to `false` everywhere
would have turned a parity guard into `false == false` four times — which still reads like coverage.

★ smex's fixtures moved from `iwex:blastmix` to **unstamped `iwex:burden`**, which inherited its meaning
exactly. That keeps every calibration anchor at its old value *and* keeps
`Legacy_blast_mix_reads_as_a_standard_grade_burden` pointed at a live subject rather than retiring it with
the item.

ⓘ `IwexCodes.ChargeShaft` still admits `coalpile` and the **reason changed**: not "the hopper makes them"
(neither hopper does now) but "a shaft charged before the cutover has real ones standing in it, and dropping
the branch makes that furnace read *incomplete* — which on a lit furnace is an extinguish". Nothing migrates
those piles into columns.

### U2.11 — the stale notes

Five source comments still named Phase 3 as future work this unit did (`ChargeableCells` "nothing reads this
yet", the columns region "nothing reads these yet", `OnColumnChanged`, `BlocksTall`, `PileCode`). ⓘ No
golden re-bless and no `IwexBlocks.g.cs` regeneration were needed: blastmix was an **item**, absent from the
block-code table, and the goldens-completeness test passes with its golden deleted alongside its def.

## 2026-08-06 — CODE: THE CHARGE-COLUMN CUTOVER — the shaft furnaces stop reading vanilla coal piles

**All five suites green on 1.20 AND 1.21 — 865 + 1306 + 192 + 54 + 229 = 2 646, zero skips.**

U2.3 + U2.4 + **U2.5** + U2.6 + U2.7, as one move. The plan billed this as four atomic tasks; it is five —
see *"Why U2.5 was not optional"* below.

### What the machine does now

`BlockEntityShaftFurnace.CollectCharge()` returns the furnace's own `ChargeColumn`s instead of a walk for
`game:coalpile` block entities, and all three consumers turned with it:

* **`ReadChargeMix`** walks every segment of every column, unit-weighted across the whole shaft. A **coke
  band** contributes `fuel += Units` and nothing else, so the coke fraction the heat balance and the
  blast-pressure demand read comes off the **geometry** of the charge rather than off a per-stack stamp.
* **`TryIgniteCharge`** is derived, with no stored state and **no tunable**: every column must hold charge at
  its raceway (the *complete course* — the furnace's own column count, nine cold and one cupola, so a redrawn
  layout updates it for free), and the raceway must hold **carbon**.
* **`SmeltCycle`** takes from the **bottom**. The old top-down sort existed only because vanilla's pile
  collapses its own column from inside its block entity; a column has no collapse in it, so the gap now
  appears at the **top**, which is where the hopper already drips. Spread round-robin over the columns that
  still hold charge, so unequal columns descend together.
* **`BurnOutCharge`** moved onto the shaft branch (abstract on the core) and rewrites the column **per
  block** — which is the granularity because *what a player digs out is a block*, and it is what keeps the
  bottom course at exactly `RetainedBottom` and the top at exactly `RetainedTop`.
* **The tall hopper** stopped walking the world. It asks the anchored core for a column
  (`NextChargeColumn`: lowest first, fuel only onto burden) and pushes at an **ambient-quantised**
  temperature.

### ⚠ Why U2.5 was not optional

Two scenario cases break the instant the handle flips, and neither is in the "atomic four":
`SolidifyBottomLayer`'s free-cell test looked for `game:coalpile`, but the shipped drawings mark the crucible
`Chargeable` **and** `Pool` at once — so that cell now holds `iwex:furnace-chargepile`, reads as *not free*,
and the pool freezes **nowhere**. And `BurnOutCharge` walked `EnumerateChargePiles`, which after the flip
returns nothing, so burn-out became a silent no-op with the suite still green on everything else.

### ⛔ Three defects the task bodies would have shipped

1. ★★ **`!Burden.IsAnyCode` is not the fuel test.** Legacy `iwex:blastmix` is neither burden nor fuel — it is
   count-only charge that burns as the standard grade — so "not burden ⇒ carbon" reads an old world's entire
   shaft at a coke fraction of **1.00** against the reference 0.20, pinning the heat balance's fuel factor at
   its ceiling. The test is the **`fuel` material role**. ⚠ Found by **mutation**, not by review.
2. ⛔ **Fuel must not go through the family gate.** `Burden.FamilyOfCode` answers `ore` for everything that is
   not remelt burden, coke included — so a **cupola** would tally every lump of its own fuel as wrong-family
   charge and refuse to convert for ever, while the HUD named "ore" as the offender in a furnace that burns
   remelt. The rejection question is asked of **burden only**.
3. ⛔ **A dead furnace could relight off its own salvage — for legacy charge.** The pile path left unstamped
   charge untouched at burn-out and got away with it because releasing a pile also put the pile's own fire
   out. A *derived* ignition gate reads unstamped charge as carrying the reference coke fraction, so the gate
   re-arms every tick: relight, starve, burn out again, multiplying the retention each round. Burn-out now
   **stamps** legacy charge as spent.

ⓘ For stamped burden the loop closes by itself and that is the nice part: `BfBurnoutFuelRetainedBottom` is
**0**, so a burnt-out shaft has no carbon at its own raceway. No "was lit" bit anywhere.

### ★★ Mutation-checked, per the rule this repo learned the hard way

Four mutations, each reverted after confirming exactly one test failed:

| mutation | caught by |
|---|---|
| empty columns no longer block ignition | `A_shaft_piled_into_ONE_column_never_lights_however_much_is_in_it` |
| any charge at the raceway lights | `A_furnace_left_alone_after_it_goes_out_keeps_burning_its_own_salvage` |
| the old `!IsAnyCode` fuel reading | `Legacy_blast_mix_reads_as_a_standard_grade_burden` |
| burn-out leaves legacy charge alone | `Legacy_charge_is_stamped_as_spent_so_a_dead_furnace_cannot_relight` |

⚠ The **first** one is why the discriminating scene had to be written at all. Every existing scene charges a
full course, so on all of them "the raceway course is complete" and "the total clears the threshold" are true
*together* — a furnace checking only the total passes every one of them. `OneTallColumn` piles three times the
fire threshold into one column with eight tuyeres facing empty air.

### Fixtures changed shape, not just backing store

Every scene now charges **every column**, weighted by each column's own cell count — the crucible well makes
three of nine a cell taller, so an equal split fills the short six past their own roofs while the tall three
stand short. Charge above a column's roof is charge no block can draw and no player can dig out, which is the
same defect keying columns off the bounding box was. Charging also moved **after** `Structure.Complete()`: a
furnace owns no columns until its layout has arrived, so an earlier push lands nowhere, silently.

### Two assertions changed beyond U2.6 Step 6's allowance, both forced

* The cupola's salvage read went from `pile.inventory[0]` to the column. A charge pile is a **window** and
  holds nothing — that is what makes breaking one safe. The claim is unchanged.
* smex's `Melting_does_not_draw_from_a_wrong_family_pile` asserted a per-slot filter that **contradicted the
  model the HUD and the salvage path already followed**: wrong-family charge is real mass burning in the
  shaft, counts family-blind toward the total, and comes back as salvage. It descends; it never becomes
  metal. The tick already blocks the whole melt cycle while `ConversionBlocked`, so the filter could never be
  reached with anything to filter.

### Deleted

`CollectChargePiles`, `EnumerateChargePiles`, `GetBlastMixCount`, `CheckHearthBurning`, the core's
`BurnOutCharge` body (both are **abstract** now), `HopperTallPileCap` + `HopperTallDropDepth`, and the
hopper's nine-offset `Columns` table with its `DripAngle`. ★ That last pair was **B16 twice over** — once as
a plus (four corner columns permanently unchargeable) and once unrotated (a south- or west-facing cupola
charging outside itself); columns are structure-local now, so there is no offset left for a facing to rotate
wrongly. `FurnaceOrientationMatrixTests` guards the deletion by name.

### ⛔ Not built, deliberately

The **propagating fire front** — torch an open tap, a beat, the bottom piles catch, a per-column *lit height*
climbing neighbour-to-neighbour and stopping dead at a burden-only gap. That is U3.2's, and
`layered-charge.md` itself defers it. Lit-ness stays furnace-level, so a tall shaft lights all at once, and
`bf-info-partiallylit` lost its producer. ⚠ It was **not** repointed at "only some columns are coked": that
looks like the same sentence and is not, and spending the key now would leave the front's own state with none.

## 2026-08-06 — CODE: U2.2c COMPLETE — the charge scale moves to items, and the tree is green again

**All five suites green on 1.21 — 2638, zero skips.** *(Supersedes yesterday's IN-FLIGHT entry, which left
18 iwex tests red.)*

### The model

**A band is measured in ITEMS** — 16 bands / **32 items** per ore pile block — and there are **two kinds of
pile**: an **ore** charge (blast furnace) counted in items, and a **remelt** charge (cupola) that takes coke
first as items then metal by **UNITS** to a **3 000 u/block** cap. ★★ That dissolved two blockers rather than
answering them: no burden mass-share is needed, and pig's 117 %-of-solid-iron collision cannot arise because
pig never occupies an item slot.

### What landed

* `ChargeUnitsPerBand` (8 u) retired → `ChargeItemsPerBand` = **2**, `CupolaChargeMetalUnitsPerBlock` = **3 000**.
* ★ `BlocksTall` / `BandsAt` take **units per BLOCK, not per band** — forced by the remelt quantum being
  `3000/16 =` **187.5**, which no integer per-band constant can express. Boundaries multiply-before-divide, so
  both kinds stay exact and `BlocksTall` lost a rounding step.
* `ChargeUnitsPerBlock` virtual on the core (ore default 32), overridden by `BlockEntityCupolaFurnace` — the
  seam between the two pile kinds.
* ⛔ **`ExConfigMigration` added** (`ToVersion 0.2.0`), resetting the charge keys. ⚠ The scale changed
  **currency**, not magnitude: a saved config carries the old numbers with no way to tell which currency they
  are in, so 8 "units" a band reads as 4× the shipped charge and a `BlastMixRequiredToFire` of 320 becomes a
  threshold no shaft can reach. **Reset rather than converted on purpose** — a conversion would have to assume
  the player never retuned, and a silent mis-conversion is unrecoverable where a reset is visible.

### ⚠ Two bugs of my own, both worth keeping

1. The pile fell back to `Core?.ChargeUnitsPerBlock ?? **0**`. `BandsAt` clamps its quantum to ≥1, so a zero
   fallback put every band boundary at 0 and drew whole columns as one empty stripe. Now falls back to the ore
   default, with the reason on the member.
2. ★★ **The "impossible" failure was a stale guard, not arithmetic.** `(units: 1 → 1 block)` was failing, which
   `BlocksTall(1, ≥1) == 1` says cannot happen — because a leftover `Assert.Equal(128, PerBlock)` fired *first*.
   Insisting on that diagnosis before touching expectations was right: the remaining 17 were then mechanical
   (÷4 preserves band counts exactly, 8 u/band → 2 items/band), and none of the numbers got fitted to a bug.

ⓘ Test literals converted with intent preserved, not blind-scaled — e.g. the deliberate "a course shorter than
one band" went 3 → **1** (a new band is 2 items), not 3 → 0.

## 2026-08-05 — CODE: U3.0 tuyeres raised to y=2, passthroughs deleted; QW bookkeeping; U2.2c blocked

**All five suites green on 1.21 — 2638.**

### U3.0 — the blast now enters the top of the hearth

Tuyeres moved to layer 2 at `(0,2,-2)` / `(0,2,2)`, the vacated y=1 cells returned to brick, the fixture's
pinned cells moved with the drawing, and `blastcore` re-blessed — the golden confirms both tuyeres at y-level
**`[2]`** and no passthrough anywhere.

★★ **The `P` passthrough legend is DELETED, and that came from the host's reading of the geometry:** *"the
pass-through existing is the result of [the tuyeres being] too deep in the furnace structure."* Exactly right —
a passthrough only ever existed to carry blast through brick to a buried tuyere. On the wall face the tuyere
**is** the outermost cell, so the player's pipe butts straight against it. ⓘ The orientation pin survives for
the same reason it worked before: walled on three sides, the cell admits exactly one connector face.

⛔⛔ **Two docs were lying about this, and the plan believed one of them.** `layered-charge.md` said the move
was *"already applied in layouts.md § 1"*. It was not — that draft still drew `Y` at **layer 1**, showed no
passthroughs, and its legends resolve to nothing (`ExCodes.Refractory`, `IwexCodes.Tuyere`, …). Both pages now
carry a ⛔ banner pointing at the code. ⚠ U3.0 also described itself as *"a pure drawing edit"*; the tuyere row
is only **one of four** coupled changes in that table — the crucible widening and both tap moves are **not
built** and belong to U4.5 / U4.6.

### Phase-0 bookkeeping

QW1 / QW3 / QW5 / QW10 marked ✅ against verified tree state, and the three stale claims that cited their
*pre-fix* state struck: the HOLD_CLIPS collisions row, the "catalogue is UNENFORCED" row, and the burdenmaker
note. ⛔ **U3.7 Step 7 struck outright** — it told the implementer to re-point `HeatBalanceTests.FullHearth` at
a config value, which would re-create the exact tautology QW3 landed to remove.

### ⚠ U2.2c is BLOCKED, and it is not a small blocker

Its deliverable is a per-material `UnitsPerBand`, but `layered-charge.md` refuses to ship the ore-burden
figure: *"lands somewhere near 200 u/band — but the third factor is a design choice (it is the burden recipe)
— **do not ship the estimate**."* ⓘ I checked whether it is derivable from shipped config and it is **not**:
`BfDefaultFluxFrac` / `BfDefaultFuelFrac` are item-**count** fractions (`IronFrac = Iron/(Iron+Flux+Fuel)`),
not the **mass** share the formula needs. Pig is fine at 375; ore burden and coke — the two things a blast
furnace actually charges — have no value. Host ruled *"rule it now, ~200"*; the roasted figure still needs
its direction confirmed (roasted ore is **more** porous, so if band content moves at all it should move
**down**, not up).

## 2026-08-05 — CODE: passive cooling was dead, and the fixture is why nobody noticed

**All five suites green on 1.21 — 2638.** Third and last of the pipe rulings.

⛔ **The bug was a misnamed field.** `ApplyPassiveCooling` was gated on `pass.Consumers == 0` — "only an idle
run cools" — but `ClassifyOpenings` increments `Consumers` once per node whose BE is an `IPipeNode`, i.e.
**once per pipe segment**. The guard was unsatisfiable on any run containing a pipe, so a hot main held its
temperature for ever. ⚠ Not cosmetic: `gas-system.md` prices buffering in heat — *"stored gas comes out cold;
smoothness or temperature, never both"* — and an un-cooling run was **a gasholder that dodged the price**,
which would have made the holder block strictly worse than ordinary pipe at its own job.

✔ Guard removed entirely rather than refined; `PipeGasCoolPerSecond` / `PipeAmbientTemperature` added to exlib
config and the rate is now **dt-scaled** (it was a flat per-tick figure). ★ *"A flowing line stays hot"* is now
**emergent** from the volume-weighted blend in `TryProduceGas` rather than special-cased — and a longer main is
harder to keep hot, because the injected litres are a smaller fraction of a bigger pool. That is the first real
cost run **length** has ever carried.

### ⛔⛔ The fixture lesson, which is bigger than this bug

My first three tests **passed with the broken guard restored** — worthless as regression guards. Cause:
`PipeTestWorld.Run` places blocks with **no block entities**, so `Consumers` is **0** in a headless test where
in game it equals the segment count. ★★ **The feature worked in tests while being dead in play** — that is how
it survived to be found by reading, not by failing.

Added `PipeTestWorld.LiveRun`, which attaches real `BlockEntityPipe`s, re-pointed the tests at it, and
**mutation-checked**: restoring the old guard now fails 2 of the 3. ⓘ The third (`a fed line stays hot`) passes
either way by design — it documents the emergent behaviour rather than guarding the bug, and saying so beats
pretending it is a guard.

## 2026-08-05 — CODE: the pipe throughput gate — mechanism landed, calibration deliberately NOT

**All five suites green on 1.21 — 2635.** ⚠⚠ **But read the last section: the gate currently binds nothing,
on purpose, and that is the open half.**

**What landed.** `IThroughputLimitedPipe` in exlib + a per-domain `RegisterThroughput` registry on
`BlockPipe`, mirroring the burst rating exactly; `MinThroughput` as a cached weakest-link walk invalidated
from `OnTopologyChanged`; and a clamp on all six pool entry points (four, since the two `*Measured` variants
delegate). Three config keys + registrations, and both `PipeBurstParityTests` extended — the fixture restates
the lpex/hpex numbers because it cannot reference those assemblies, and a stale copy is silent.

⚠ **Deliberately NOT gated on `CanBurst`.** Burst exemption is about a fitting being reinforced; a valve is
still a length of that tier's pipe and does not widen the line. Exempting fittings would let a player raise a
run's throughput by splicing valves into it.

### ⛔ The bug I shipped and then removed, because it is the shape this repo keeps paying for

The first implementation kept a **per-tick budget refilled in `OnTick`** — more accurate when it worked, and
**silently wrong when it did not.** Every furnace scenario fixture drives the *machine's* tick without pumping
the *network's*, so the budget never refilled and a per-second rate quietly became a one-shot allowance for
the entire run. **28 tests failed in a way that looked exactly like bad calibration.** ★★ Replaced with a
stateless per-call clamp: 28 → 17 failures immediately, and iwex went fully green. A rate that is only correct
when something else remembers to tick is not a rate. ⓘ What the stateless form gives up is written on the
member: two producers on one run can each move a segment's worth per second. With one producer and one
consumer — every shipped layout — it is exact.

### ✅ Shipped at the intended calibration — 50 / 120 / 250 L/s, and it BINDS

*(User ruled: "smex can require better pipes.")* All 17 failures at the tight figures resolved, three ways:

- ⛔⛔ **The furnace TUYERE must not limit throughput — and finding that reversed a decision I had already
  written down.** I had argued fittings *should* limit ("a valve does not widen the line"). True of a valve,
  false of the case that matters: the tuyere is an **`iwex` block on its own single-node network**, because it
  is the machine's intake **port** rather than a length of main — so limiting on it would pin **every furnace
  in the suite to the iron tier's rate forever**, including the hot blast furnace two tiers above. That is the
  exact opposite of what a pipe tier is for. Now gated on the same "plain segment" rule as `CanBurst`.
  ★ This alone fixed 5 of the 8 smex failures.
- ★★ **smex's plant mains re-plumbed in CAST** — the converter's blast main and the cowper's hot-blast and
  exhaust runs. Its engine blower runs **144 L/s** (`AirBlowerOutputPerSecond` 48 × an **undocumented
  hard-coded 3**), which the plated tier's 50 refuses. **A hot furnace now needs better plumbing than a cold
  one**, which is the tier gate doing its job on the day it shipped rather than a promise for later.
  ⓘ The ×3 is undocumented; I found it, left it, and recorded it.
- **The "do it in one call" fixture idioms are gone.** `TryProduceGas(1000f, …)` to saturate a run and
  `TryConsumeGas(float.MaxValue)` to empty one both silently became partial. Replaced with a shared
  `PipeTestWorld.Saturate` helper and bounded drain/fill loops — which is what a blower actually does over a
  few seconds. ⓘ Those tests were about **pressure ceilings**, never rates, so their intent is unchanged.

⚠ **Behaviour change worth stating plainly:** a run can no longer be filled or emptied instantly. Anything
that relied on that — in tests or in a machine — now takes as many seconds as the plumbing needs.

## 2026-08-05 — pipes: throughput gate ruled, bore ruled OUT, and the tier renamed `bolted` → `plated`

**All five suites green on 1.21 — 2633, zero skips.**

**Three rulings, written into `mechanics/pipe-network.md`:**

- ⛔ **A run now gates its own throughput**, as a weakest-link walk mirroring `MinBurstPressure`. ★★ Before
  this there was **no rate cap anywhere in the pool** — production bounded by headroom, consumption by
  availability, so a consumer asking for a million litres drained the run in one call — and `State.FlowRate`
  was written once per tick and read **only by the HUD**. The implicit ceiling was already ~75 L/s through a
  single pipe, above every line in the mod. ★ This is what finally makes a tier mean something beyond burst.
  ⚠ It does **not** retire the machine-side rates; `STATE.md`'s "a rate rule at the machines" still stands —
  machines gate what they draw, the run gates what the plumbing carries.
- ⛔ **No large-bore pipe family.** Three independent reasons: `fluid-tank.md` already rejected
  capacity-bearing nodes (15 re-derivation sites, "highest risk"); it is **physically backwards** — `σ = pr/t`,
  so a wider bore bursts at *lower* pressure, and `PoolVolumeCeiling` would make a fatter pipe buy *more*
  over-pressure headroom; and a full mirror costs **+20 blocktypes / +394 variants / +20 goldens / +18 shapes**
  on top of B5/B19, which say the cast and rolled tiers still have no recipe. ★★ Also, the numbers to size it
  are UNDERIVED — a bore set sized against the 48 L/s exhaust figure would be sized against a placeholder.
  ⛔⛔ **Recorded now: if bore is ever added it is ORTHOGONAL to tier, never a fourth rung** — history is
  unambiguous that the *largest* pipework carried the *lowest* pressure (`Δp ∝ Q²/D⁵`), so a large-bore plated
  blast main and a small-bore rolled steam pipe are both correct.
- ⛔ **Stored gas cools, including gas parked in a run.** A long main was a gasholder storing gas **hot**,
  dodging the price today's buffering ruling set, because passive cooling is dead code on any run with a pipe
  segment. Fixing it makes the ruling hold universally — and gives run **length** its first real consequence.

**The rename, `bolted` → `plated`** *(user's call, after correctly objecting that "riveted" would put riveted
plate mains before cast iron ones, which is backwards by a century).*

★★ **"Bolted" was wrong twice over**: it named the *joint* rather than the tube, and the joint family is
already `flanged` — **and shared with the cast tier**, so it never distinguished the tier at all. The other two
tiers are named for fabrication (`cast`, `rolled`); `plated` puts all three on one axis. ⓘ It also matches
what the recipe actually makes — hammer + plate + nails, both vanilla-obtainable — which is *crude smith-made
pipe*, not an engineered riveted main. That is why the tier can precede cast in a bootstrapping works even
though cast iron mains predate riveted plate ones industrially.

- Renamed across 38 C# files, 3 locales, the design docs, and the handbook authoring source.
- `iwex:molten-barrel-bolted` → `-plated`, with **`BarrelPlatedRenameMigration`**; shapes renamed including
  the editable source. ⚠ `BarrelConstructionMigration`'s **target** moved (targets are current codes) while
  its **sources** stayed verbatim — and its doc-comment's *history* had to be restored by hand, because the
  blanket rename rewrote it. That file's own warning was about source literals; this is the same trap one
  level up: **prose recording what a code used to be is a historical fact too.**
- ⚠ Caught and reverted my own over-reach: **"bolted through flanges" and "the bolted flange" are literally
  correct** and must not change. Same for a flywheel bolted at the rim, stands bolted onto a hall, and
  regenerators bolted onto a hearth.
- ✔ **The rename CLOSED a documented inconsistency in three places** — `fasteners.md` Gotcha 6,
  `heading-machine.md` and `STATE.md` all argued "the pipe is *called* bolted, so it should cost bolts". The
  name no longer promises a fastener the recipe does not use, so all three were rewritten: what survives is
  the honest half — **bolts still have no consumer anywhere**, which is now an open question rather than a
  naming defect to be tidied away.
- ⓘ RU/UK said "клёпаная"/"клепана" (**riveted**) — reintroducing exactly the claim EN rejected — so both moved
  to the plate sense (листовая / листова), parallel to their own литая / лита.

⚠ **Pre-existing inaccuracy left alone, flagged not guessed:** `HpSteamPlantScenes.cs:57` and `:152` say "a
plated pipe bursts at 5, so feed it through cast" — but plated is **2.5** and cast is **5**, and the Cornish
band is ≥6, which neither reaches. The comment was wrong before this rename; I did not invent a correction.

## 2026-08-05 — fourth fork pass: the last four iwex forks, and the coke oven gets simpler

- **`MaxWidth` lives in BOTH places — the effective cap is `min(roll-set barrel, stock-form cap)`.** ★★ The
  only answer serving all three of `rolling.md`'s demands: the barrel becomes physical, the bar carries a form
  cap of 9 so its terminal plate stage is legal, and **`skelp` stays capped at 8** — not optional, since skelp
  is the only feed for the conical roller → rolled pipe, i.e. hpex's 12 atm requirement.
  ⛔⛔ **Struck the plan's "U7 does NOT have to settle this" — it was false and load-bearing**: the shipped cap
  is 8 and the bar reaches 9.0, so today the last flat stage **silently clamps** and U7's own gate fails.
  ⚠ `min()` does not close the 50-voxel hole on castbloom's 1.0 stage; that needs a declared mandatory crop,
  which the crop table already expresses for the billet's 2.25 half-step — a new row, not a new mechanism.
- ⛔ **The coke oven is TWO SEALED CHAMBERS running VANILLA's coking process, scaled up** — *(user's answer,
  and it corrects **both** options I offered.)* Charged with coal piles, lit **without access to air**. ★★ The
  mod's contribution is **efficiency and scale, not a different mechanism**: an **arched ceiling** for
  efficiency, **many piles per charge** for scale, so the player gets coke faster and in larger quantity.
  ⛔ Rejected: the self-heated, air-regulated beehive (no aperture control). ⛔ Rejected: gating on a coking
  temperature off the shared heat balance — **so `CokeOvenTempC` is never created and no transfer-loss override
  is needed**, which is exactly what made that route expensive (without both, the oven stranded at ~1042 °C and
  blocked every coke-fired machine). ⛔ **No regenerator** — regenerative practice is much later historically;
  if ever built it is a **separate regenerative coke oven in smex**. ⚠ U9.1 Step 2's rationale *"a beehive oven
  burns part of its own charge, so Firebox is not a lie"* is retired — the chambers are sealed.
  ★ U9.2 Step 5 (yield **and** rate vs vanilla) becomes the task's centre: a bigger box at the same rate is
  precisely the failure this ruling forbids, so pin both in the tests.
- **Roasting: porosity alone. The mod works with VANILLA ores only** — no limonite, no siderite, no new ore.
  Ships with the ruled 6.375 → 6.9 ladder unchanged. ★ Narrower than the historical mechanism, not false:
  porosity *is* what drives the fuel saving; the water/carbonate half simply has no substrate in vanilla.
  ⓘ Extensibility to other mods' ores is a **later compatibility concern** and the natural home for anything
  limonite-shaped — do not pre-build the variety axis for it.
- **A bank of machines is THROUGHPUT ONLY** — *(user: "machines don't start to magically be more effective if
  grouped")*. New page `mechanics/plant-layout.md` owns it, closing four separately-flagged notes (cupola,
  ladle, coke-oven residual-heat ignition, crucible room). ★★ **But the positive half matters as much:** good
  layout buys **less hassle and smoother operation** — shorter hauls, a line that flows one way — which is
  **emergent and the player's problem**, not a stat. ⓘ Shared infrastructure the player *builds and pays for*
  (one line shaft, one gas main, one canal) is untouched — that is plumbing, not a free grouping bonus.

## 2026-08-05 — third fork pass: an 8-area sweep, 4 more ruled, and two of the answers changed the work

Swept every remaining source of open questions — both audits, `docs/design`'s Open sections, the plan's
surviving "do not guess" markers, the gas retune, and cross-mod. ★★ **Zero of the coherence audit's 46
BLOCKING/HIGH findings still need a ruling**; they are all defects with one correct answer now.

**Ruled:**

- **Machine tooling WEARS — one rule, metal grade sets the life.** ⛔ Three pages each carried a different
  answer and the code carried a fourth (`roll-sets.md` "does not wear" vs `PatternItemDefinitions`' shipped
  24-impression durability). ★★ Without wear, `STATE.md` D9's "longer-lasting machine heads" **buys nothing**
  and the whole crucible-steel branch has no consumer. Same failure shape as the gas system, so it got the
  same fix: a new owning page, `mechanics/tooling-wear.md`, with `dies.md` and `roll-sets.md` re-pointed at it.
  ⓘ The rejected "steel buys capability" option was half right and that half survives — the boring machine's
  bit-material gate is a **separate axis** (capability = *whether*; life = *how long*).
- **An idle machine DOES cost power — and the counterpart is a BLOCK, not a per-machine flag.** *(User's
  reframing, and it is better than the option offered.)* Every connected consumer contributes standing torque;
  to stop paying, **declutch the branch**. ★★ The clutch turned out to be **already fully built** — a
  `BlockTransmission` variant, 2×2, lever cell, persisted `_engaged`, *"a disengaged clutch leaves the two runs

- **Puddling: ONE BALL PER RABBLE** — 16 gestures, 16 balls, each drawn out separately. ★★ The only reading

  `FillerLayoutBuilder.Host(char, …)`'s own doc-comment uses `M` for exactly this, `BEBehaviorMPFillerPort`

⚠ **One live arithmetic dependency the earlier yield ruling missed:** `BfIronPerBurdenBand = 6.375` derives
from `IronFrac = 0.75` = `1 − 0.05 flux − 0.20 coke`, but **U5 removes coke from the burden**. A post-U5 stack
reads ≈ 0.94, so shipping 6.375 delivers ~6.8 u/nugget against the ruled 8.5 — the furnace silently drops from
1.7× bloomery to ~1.36×, and the guard-rail divides by a fraction no shipped stack carries. Calibration, not a
ruling; belongs in U3.3 Step 4 as an explicit re-derivation against the post-U5 burden.

ⓘ **Four phase-0 quick wins (QW1/3/5/10) are already done in the tree** but still listed unchecked, and three
later units cite their pre-fix state as VERIFIED. Bookkeeping only.
