## 2026-08-05 — ★★ the gas transition starts at smex because of the CLOSED TOP, and it reaches backwards

**Confirmed: smex enables an almost-full transition to fuel gas, and it is an upgrade to the earlier tiers
rather than a replacement of them.** ★★ And the tier boundary is not a gate anyone chose — it falls out of the
two furnace drawings already shipped:

| | **cold blast** (iwex) | **hot blast** (smex) |
|---|---|---|
| top | open stack, 3 air cells — *"my open top is my chimney"* | **sealed**: bell hopper over a 1-cell throat |
| exhaust | **none** | **2 outlets**, feeding cowpers and the main |

**An open-topped furnace cannot capture its gas** — it burns it at the throat as the tunnel-head flame.
`blast-furnace-hot.md` already calls the closed top the hot furnace's defining build difference. ⓘ
Historically exact: capture needed a closed top (Parry's cup-and-cone, 1850), decades after the cold blast
furnaces the iron tier models.

★★ **So the cold furnace's wasted flame is not a missing feature — it is the visible argument for the closed
top.** The player watches fuel burn for nothing at the tunnel head, and the sealed top is what captures it. The
flare-as-indicator idea arrives a tier early and costs nothing, because it is already in the drawing.

**And the reach-backwards is the property worth protecting:** iwex's puddling and reheat furnaces take a **gas
burner** in the firebox cell with the hearth untouched; smex ships the **gas Cornish** for lpex; hpex ships
**both** Lancashire variants. ★★ **Nothing the player built becomes obsolete — the way it is FED changes.** A
later tier that improves earlier machines instead of superseding them is the mod thesis at tier scale: buy the
plumbing and the producer, stop stoking ten fires.

## 2026-08-05 — gas plumbing: the burner swaps for a firebox, surplus is FLARED, and gas may be buffered cold

- **One piped gas-burner block replaces a firebox anywhere** — functions exactly as a firebox, consumes gas.
  No per-machine variant. ⓘ The same move the firebox itself made when it replaced `@(air|coalpile)`.
  ⚠ **Boilers are the deliberate exception and get shape variants**, because the vessel visibly differs: smex
  ships the gas Cornish, hpex ships both Lancashire variants.
- ⛔ **Excess gas is FLARED, never vented up a smokestack.** A stack carries *combustion products*; raw BF gas
  is CO-rich — toxic and explosive — so venting it unburnt would waste fuel and, in reality, kill people. The
  historical answer is the **bleeder/flare** at the furnace throat: released *and ignited*. ★★ And it is the
  best kind of indicator — **a flare visible from across the map means you are wasting fuel.** A well-plumbed
  works barely flares; nothing needs to tell the player their gas balance is wrong.
- **★★ Buffering reversed, and its physics kept as a PRICE rather than a prohibition.** The old table row said
  producer gas is *"fed hot, in lock-step — no storage"*, on the correct observation that a holder is a large
  cold vessel. That does not forbid storage, it **prices** it: **any gas may be stored, and stored gas comes
  out COLD**. Direct feed keeps the full `ProducerGasTempFactor` value; buffered feed is ambient. So the player
  trades **smoothness for temperature, never both** — which is exactly what absorbs a blast furnace going quiet
  during a charge without starving the steam engines. The superseded row is marked in place.
- **The gasholder is the tank's sibling, not a new machine** — one storage family, two shape variants,
  behaviour forking on whether the medium is compressible. ⓘ Honours `lpex.md`'s *"not a new block"* while
  respecting that they are not the same object: a **rigid** vessel with fill-dependent pressure from head,
  versus a **telescoping bell** in a water seal at constant pressure set by its own weight, because gas is
  compressible and rigid gas storage needs compression (later technology). ★★ And the bell earns its keep as an
  indicator — **its fill is readable from across the map**, the same family as the flare and the slag spout.
  The works states its condition by its silhouette; nothing opens a GUI.

## 2026-08-05 — ★★ the gas transition: coke stays where carbon is a REAGENT, gas takes over where fire is HEAT

**Asked whether a gas burner could slot into any reverberatory furnace, and whether that means an almost-total
transition from coal to gas. Historically: yes to both, and the boundary is principled rather than a balance
call.** Siemens regenerative gas firing (1856–61) was applied to exactly these — puddling furnaces, reheating
furnaces, crucible melting, glass tanks, and the open hearth, which *is* a gas-fired regenerative
reverberatory. A producer plus regenerators was bolted onto an existing hearth: the hearth did not change, the
**firing** did. Standard in large works by the 1870s–90s.

★★ **The line: coke stays where carbon is a REDUCTANT — blast furnace, cupola, and the coke oven that makes it
— and gas takes over everywhere fire is only heat.** You cannot pipe carbon into an ore column. So the
transition can be as complete as it likes without erasing coke, and the rule is chemistry rather than taste.

**Two grades of one gas is correct, and it fixes a shipped problem.** Lean gas really does deliver less — lower
flame temperature, less steam from a boiler, a lower ceiling in a furnace. Modelling BF gas and producer gas as
**grades of one medium** also repairs the `Compatible`-returns-true-for-any-gas rule: mixing gives a **weighted
average**, which is what physically happens, instead of a priority relabel that silently discards the
difference. ★★ And it explains the open hearth: regenerators exist *because* the fuel is lean — preheating air
and gas with the exhaust is what reaches steel temperature on weak gas. The trick is a consequence of the gas.

**Why the producer still gets built when BF gas is free** — the user's point, and it holds: **the blast furnace
runs intermittently and the gas chain must not.** Steam engines, boilers and heating furnaces need supply that
survives the end of a campaign or a reline. The producer is **baseload**, BF gas is **opportunistic surplus**;
works ran both for exactly this reason.

★★ **And it gives lignite a job.** A producer gasifies slack, dust and poor coal that cannot be burnt on a
grate — which lands well, because lignite was ruled out of the coke oven earlier the same day. Without the
producer lignite is nearly dead content; with it, lignite is a tier entry. ⓘ Historically why producers spread:
they monetised fuel nobody else could use.

**On effectiveness: raw gas is NOT stronger than coal per unit.** The advantages in the order they mattered are
(1) ★★ **centralisation** — one plant stoked in one place, piped to everything, no hand-feeding ten boilers;
(2) **regeneration**, the only route to steel temperatures, where the *regenerator* is the upgrade and the gas
is what makes it possible; (3) control of flame and atmosphere; (4) cleanliness — no ash, no sulphur contact;
(5) poor fuel becomes usable. ★ So the mod's shape is **coal is stronger per unit and demands attention; gas is
weaker per unit and demands none** — buy the plumbing, stop stoking.

ⓘ **And BF gas breaks a circular dependency the design already recorded:** `gas-producer.md` notes *"producer
gas needs steam, and steam needs a boiler"*. BF top gas needs no steam, so a gas-fired boiler is what makes
producer gas bootstrappable at all.

## 2026-08-05 — ★★ the blast furnace becomes a power station: gas surplus, emergent stove count, bigger stoves

Three cowper rulings, and the first has the widest reach of anything settled today.

**★★ The furnace produces a gas SURPLUS, and the surplus powers the works.** Today's tuning says *"the hot
blast furnace's 48 L/s exhaust is exactly two stoves' intake"* — an exact fit, so no surplus exists **by
construction**. That is now wrong on both counts: historically stoves took roughly a *third* of top gas and the
rest ran the plant, and an exact fit forecloses the loop entirely.

```
  blast furnace ──gas──> blowing engine ──blast──> blast furnace
                   └────> stoves ──hot blast──────────┘
                   └────> boilers
```

★★ **The furnace powers its own blower** — the most satisfying industrial loop of the era, and genuinely
circular. ★ It also gives lpex a reason to touch iwex's output: the twin-tub blower is the iron tier's only air
source, so a **gas-fired blowing engine** becomes the steam tier's upgrade to it, bought with *plumbing* rather
than with a bigger blower. ⓘ Cross-mod direction is the normal one (iwex produces, lpex consumes).

**★★ Stove count becomes EMERGENT, and today's ratio is inverted from reality.** The page records *"with
anthracite it charges 5.3× faster than it blows, so a pair can never get ahead"* — but a real stove takes
roughly **twice as long to heat as to give up its heat**, which is exactly why works ran **three**: one on
blast, two on gas. Fix the ratio, not the count: at 2 stoves the blast **visibly sags** between swaps, at 3 it
is steady, at 4 there is headroom. ⛔ Nothing should *say* three — the player learns it by watching the blast.

**Stoves get bigger** — a tall megablock reading as the furnace's twin rather than an accessory. ★ The build
cost is what makes running three a commitment; the count only matters if each one is expensive. ⓘ Fixed size,
not player-scalable: height-as-capacity is the chimney's lever and does not need a second owner.

⚠ **What has to move with this:** the 48 L/s exhaust figure, the 5.3× charge advantage, the two-stove
assumption wherever it is baked into layout and lang, and `blast-furnace-hot.md`'s exhaust arithmetic.

## 2026-08-05 — fork batch 3: fire propagation, the cowper remake, U6.11 scope, the rolled rod

- **Fire propagation: a PER-COLUMN LIT HEIGHT, front travelling up and sideways.** Each `ChargeColumn` carries
  how far the fire has reached; it climbs through coke-bearing bands and spreads laterally to adjacent columns
  at the same height. ★ Keeps the pile a **near-stateless renderer** — no per-block burn flag, no per-block
  save, it persists with the column for free — and it is the only option consistent with *each column has its
  own height*: a burden-only gap in **one** column stops **that** column's front, which is what makes
  band-order charging a skill. ⛔ Downward spread is impossible and needn't be — new charge lands on top.
- **U6.11 is in scope for U6.** Without the ball, the helve recipe and conserved stock masses, U6 would ship a
  furnace whose product nothing consumes, and U7's entry condition would be false. The deliverables table that
  stops at U6.4 is what is stale.
- **The rolled rod re-enters the mill**, and ★★ **rod-sized stock must also heat in vanilla's own firepit/forge**
  — the reheat furnace is for *big* stock, and gating a rod behind a megablock puts the early rivet route out
  of reach. ⚠ Left OPEN deliberately: whether `iwex:rolledrod` should exist at all or the mill should output
  **vanilla's rod**. ⓘ The drawn `item-rolled-rod.json` is a *rolling-stage* shape, so the art forces neither.

**★★ The cowper: asked whether a gas-fired remake was historically right — it is, and it is ALREADY the
design.** `cowper.md` lines 11–17 already specify a **gravitational dust catcher** turning raw furnace exhaust
into clean fuel gas, explicitly deleting the coal pile ("a real Cowper stove is not coal-fired"). ⓘ Fourth time
this session an answer was already written down — though this time I grepped *before* answering rather than
after.

What was genuinely new, and is now recorded:
- **Lighting.** Hot stove ⇒ the gas **self-ignites** (BF gas autoignites ~600–650 °C, so a stove on its second
  cycle relights itself); cold stove ⇒ the player torches the heat sinks through a **side hatch**, which is a
  real lighting port. ★ So it is a **first-light** cost, not a per-cycle one — once a pair is in alternation
  they keep themselves going, which is the reward for getting the loop running. ⚠ Trap: torching an empty
  stove must do nothing. **Gas first, flame second.**
- **Clearing the condensed waste SHOULD be a mechanic — because it gives back.** Flue dust is fine ore and
  coke carried out of the throat, it is **iron-bearing**, and works recovered it into the burden rather than
  dumping it. So emptying the catcher yields something worth having and reads as *reclaiming your losses*, not
  as cleaning a filter because the game says so. ★ That closes the loop twice: waste **gas** fuels the blast
  preheat, waste **dust** returns as charge. ⚠ Neglect should choke gas flow — visible as a failing hot blast —
  and must never silently destroy the gas. ⓘ CO toxicity deliberately not modelled.

**2631 green on 1.21.**

## 2026-08-05 — `castshell` moved back to iwex; vessel cooldown ruled to derive from charge volume

**★★ Generalised the ladle's heat ruling to every vessel**, recorded in `mechanics/molten-network.md`: heat
loss from a ladle, converter, hearth, canal, barrel or mould **derives from its surface-to-volume ratio**, not
from a per-container constant. The mod currently ships **three per-container cooldown coefficients** — exactly
the hand-tuned constants this replaces. They give identical numbers *today* and diverge the instant any vessel
is resized, at which point nothing fails and the numbers are merely wrong. ⓘ One factor then explains three
machines: thin canals chill fast, a crucible hearth does not, a ladle barely does. ⚠ `SyncCooldownSpeed`
already rebases to current temperature every tick, so a rate derived from the *current* charge volume applies
to metal already in the world — a partly-drained ladle cools faster as it empties, which is also right.

**`castshell` is back in iwex** (it went to lpex on 2026-08-04 for want of a consumer; the ladle is one).
★ The settled shape is **iwex owns it, lpex consumes it** — the normal dependency direction, and *cleaner than
the move ever was*: the cross-mod pattern indirection I built for it is now unnecessary, because the pattern
and the part live in one mod again. Deleted `LowPressureExpanded/Items/{CastPart,Pattern,Diagram}ItemDefinitions.cs`
and `Recipes/Grid/PatternRecipeDefinitions.cs`; moved the shape and the diagram texture from `assets/lpex/` to
`assets/iwex/`; restored the mold entry and the 600 u constant.

⚠ **The test that guarded the old arrangement was inverted, not deleted.** `Iwex_ships_no_castshell_of_its_own`
became `The_shell_came_back_to_iwex_and_ships_exactly_ONCE` — the failure it guards is unchanged and is *not* a
clash: a leftover lpex definition would register a **second** castshell in a different domain, and the two
would be different items that look identical in the handbook and never stack. Added
`The_shell_and_the_wheel_section_cost_the_SAME_iron`, because 600 = 600 is the content (N3: cast and fabricated
are alternatives, not tiers), not the value.

Four goldens re-blessed by explicit path; four orphaned lpex goldens removed. **2631 green on 1.21.**

## 2026-08-05 — TWO ladles, and the square–cube law closes the "molten cools too fast" problem

**Settled: a static iwex merger and a movable lpex one**, offering two routes rather than one machine.

| | mod | route |
|---|---|---|
| **static ladle** | iwex | cheap, low throughput — in-line canal merger; gates every alloy |
| **movable ladle** | lpex | expensive, high throughput — gathers from several furnaces, pours a cavity fast |

★★ **lpex is the right home for a non-arbitrary reason: moving a 24 000-unit vessel needs POWER.** A steam
engine is what makes it move, so it belongs to the tier that has one — and it gives lpex **an upgrade to a
previous tier's loop** rather than another parallel system, which is the thesis. ⓘ smex is the customer:
3 000-unit slabs poured from metal gathered across several converters or open hearths.

**★★ The physics claim checks out, and it is the square–cube law.** Heat loss scales with *surface area*, heat
content with *volume*, so cooling rate ∝ area ÷ volume ∝ **1/L** — double the linear size, halve the cooling
rate. Historically it is the defining fact of molten-metal logistics: torpedo cars move hundreds of tonnes
between blast furnace and steelworks over *hours* losing tens of degrees, while a hand ladle loses that in a
minute. Refractory lining and **preheating** are the other two levers (a cold ladle chills the first metal in).

⚠ **So it must be implemented as a DERIVED quantity** — `BEBehaviorMoltenCell`'s cooldown takes a
surface-to-volume factor, so a big vessel holds heat *because it is big*. ⓘ One factor then explains three
machines: thin canals chill fast, a crucible hearth does not, a ladle barely does. ⛔ A hand-tuned
`MoltenCooldownSpeed` per block gives identical numbers today and diverges the instant anything is resized.

★★ **And this closes the "molten metal solidifies too fast" problem with no special case**: the answer is not
to slow cooling globally, it is that **big vessels hold heat** — the player's lever is to build the bigger
machine.

**⚠ Skip hoist corrected to smex.** I had listed it as the movable system's co-consumer *in iwex*. Wrong: the
cold blast furnace is late-18th/early-19th century and was **charged by hand** — barrow over a bridge from a
hillside, which the tall hopper stands in for. Skip hoists are hot-blast-era, so the machine is **smex's**. The
movable-entity subsystem still has two consumers (lpex's ladle, smex's hoist), just not in iwex — so it stays
worth scoping once, and nothing in iwex U2–U10 waits on it.

## 2026-08-05 — the ladle: moved to iwex, Bessemer-sized, and it does NOT pour through a canal

**Asked whether a ladle was worth building at all.** Checked `machines/ladle.md` *before* answering this time,
and it already carried a far bigger reason than either candidate under discussion: ⚠⚠ **R3 reserves the ladle
as the only block that merges molten canals**, so *every alloying rule in `materials.md` is unreachable without
it* — the whole alloy table, hadfield, the bronzes, HSS — **and the Bessemer route cannot work without it**
(mandatory recarburisation). It is not a convenience to justify; it is the gate on the alloy catalogue.

⛔ **The proposed merge-multiple-cupolas role, though, has no demand.** Under the one-shared-volume ruling a
cupola hearth caps at 10/16 × 640 = **~6 400 u**, and the largest sand-cast part in the design is **600 u**.
One cupola already holds ten times the biggest thing anyone would pour.

**Rulings taken:**
- **Home: iwex** (was smex). The alloying role is smex-tier but the *pouring* role gates iwex casting, and it
  is the same object in both eras — smex later extends it rather than shipping a second one.
- **Two shape variants:** **plate** (bolted iron plates — ⓘ **vanilla items, so NOT gated on the rolling
  mill**, which lets the ladle exist early) and **cast** (cheaper, and reserves plates for what needs them).
- ⚠ **`castshell` returns to iwex.** It went to lpex earlier the same day *"and not invent uses for it"*; the
  ladle is a real use. ★ Resolution is **iwex owns it, lpex consumes it** (water tank, crushers, engines) —
  the normal dependency direction, and *cleaner* than the original move, because the cross-mod pattern
  indirection I built for it becomes unnecessary.
- **Size: Bessemer-scale** — 3×3×3, hemispherical vessel, **~24 000 u**, about 4× a cupola hearth.

**★★ And the sharp question back: "the ladle pours into the same canal, which is flow-rate capped — do we need
larger canals?" ⛔ No — that would solve the wrong problem.** The canal and the ladle are **parallel routes,
not series**: historically the launder is the *furnace's* delivery (tapped iron to a pig bed) and the ladle is
the *mould's* (carried to the casting and tipped over it). Feeding a ladle into a launder recreates exactly the
bottleneck it exists to escape. So **the ladle pours directly into an adjacent cell at its own rate.**

★ The asymmetry is the mechanic: **filling** the ladle is slow and nobody cares — nothing solidifies inside a
hot vessel — while **pouring** is fast, and pouring is the only moment rate matters, because of the one-pour
cold-shut rule. ⚠ Consequence: casting cells must sit **adjacent** to the ladle, so the casting hall is laid
out around it. That belongs in `casting-cell.md` rather than being discovered. ⓘ Whether molten canals should
gain tiers *at all* (as pipes have bolted/cast/rolled) is a separate feature with its own case — just not what
the ladle needs.

## 2026-08-05 — four more high-severity forks ruled (yield anchor, crucible volume, coking, shingling)

**★★ The blast-furnace yield had TWO anchors that disagreed by ~2×, and the user's reasoning resolved it
rather than just picking.** `roasting.md` fixes recovery at **8.5 u/nugget raw, 9.2 roasted**;
`blast-furnace-cold.md` fixed output so *a full shaft ≈ one full casting bed (7500 u)*. Under the new unit
scale a full shaft yields ~3670 u — half a bed. **Recovery wins**, and the consequence is the *point*:
★★ **a full shaft making half a bed is what forces bed ROTATION** — one bed filled from two campaigns, so the
player runs beds in a cycle instead of one bed per heat. The pressure `ironmaking.md` already wanted to exert
falls straight out of the yield instead of needing a rule of its own. `blast-furnace-cold.md`'s 200 is struck;
its *second* half (the hard-coded 12 u/s tap ceiling must be lifted) stays live regardless.
⛔ Recorded in U3.3 Step 4: **`BfIronPerBurdenUnit = 3.75` is today's 60 re-expressed — exact bloomery parity**,
so writing it down would have frozen the defect the guard-rail was built to catch.

**One shared crucible volume, not two budgets.** Slag floats on iron in a single layered cell, so neglecting
the slag tap eats the space iron could occupy until it is flushed. Two independent `MaxUnitCapacity` values
would have deleted the operating rhythm the whole two-tap layout was paid for, and the reason the crucible is
layered at all. ★★ The cap is the **slag-spout height (10/16)** — a level the player can look at — so
**plugging the slag tap is what lets the hearth fill**. ⓘ That mechanic had **no task**; U4.3 Step 6 now owns it.

**Only bituminous coal cokes** — lignite and anthracite do not. ⛔ So the coke oven's chamber cannot inherit
`BEBehaviorFirebox`'s fuel filter: that list refuses lignite but *accepts* anthracite, now wrong in the other
direction, and it is a reverberatory-**fuel** list never designed for a retort. The chamber gets its own accept
list (U9.1 Step 1b, placed immediately before the "decide the chamber substrate" step it constrains). Coal type
becomes a real prospecting constraint on entering the iron tier — chosen as the historically sharp reading, and
`coking.md`'s "all three grades" row is updated to match.

**Shingling is voxel accumulation** — RMB one ball at a time onto the anvil (200 u, then 400 u), then the blow.
⛔ `PigRecipeDefinitions` is 1:1 and accumulates nothing, so "copy it verbatim" could never have expressed this.
⚠ The work item must accept a **second input mid-build**, which has no precedent in the mod — budgeted rather
than discovered halfway.

## 2026-08-05 — ★★ the blast-furnace ignition sequence, fully specified by the user

Recorded verbatim into `layered-charge.md` § *The ignition sequence, fully specified*, because it settles
several things the plan was still arguing about:

> **torch an open tap → a couple of seconds → the hearth catches → the bottom piles light → the fire spreads up**

**Two gates, and neither is a tunable number.** (a) ⛔ **AIR** — the tuyeres must deliver at ≥ the required
pressure or there is no ignition at all. (b) ★★ **The raceway course must be COMPLETE — a charge pile on every
column**, or a tuyere has nothing to blow into.

★★ **(b) is the best-shaped constant in the design so far, because it is not a constant.** It replaces
`BlastMixRequiredToFire` with something **the layout answers for itself**: the requirement is simply the
furnace's own column count — nine on the cold blast furnace, one on the cupola. Nothing to tune, nothing to
drift, and a redrawn layout updates it for free. ⛔ It must never be re-expressed as "≥ N units": 2 000 units
piled in **one** column clears any threshold while eight tuyeres blow into empty air. Numerically it is a
fraction of capacity (one course of four or five), which is the middle ground the "full shaft vs a fraction"
fork was groping for and could not express — the historical reading is exactly it: **lay a complete bottom
course before you light.**

⛔ **"All piles burning" is gone** — a burning pile is *already* raising its own temperature, so the furnace
runs from the moment the bottom is lit and never waits for the column to finish catching.

⚠⚠ **And "spreads" means the vanilla coal-pile mechanic literally — neighbour to neighbour on a tick**, not a
furnace-wide flag. That is a **front climbing the shaft**, with consequences the instant reading does not have:
a tall shaft takes real time to light, so blowing in is a process the player *watches*; the heat ramp is
**emergent from the front's advance** rather than a separate curve; and a burden-only gap **stops the front**,
leaving the top of the shaft cold indefinitely with nothing to warn the player but the sight of it.

★★ **Charging a running furnace now has teeth.** A new pile catches iff it holds **at least one coke band**;
burden alone sits cold on a hot furnace. So a properly laid round (coke *then* burden) is always accepted and a
careless one is **legible on sight** — which is what turns band-order charging from bookkeeping into a skill.

**⚠ This corrected a fork I had put to the user badly.** I offered "full shaft to blow in" vs "a fraction of
capacity" — and **both were wrong**: this page already said *"a furnace runs when a lit round sits at the
raceway"*. Ignition is not a quantity condition at all; it is **positional and pneumatic**. The *fraction*
answer survives only for `MinChargeToIgnite`'s **other** job, the cold-charge-loss denominator.

⚠ Two sub-decisions left open deliberately rather than guessed: **where lit-ness lives** (the pile is specified
as a *near-stateless renderer*, so per-BE burn state contradicts the model — the natural home is a per-column
**lit height** the front advances, with lateral spread between adjacent columns), and **whether propagation is
6-neighbour or upward-only**. Both flagged in U2.3 Step 6.

## 2026-08-05 — four more forks ruled; the crucible damper was backwards for its own machine

**★★ The damper ruling checked against history, at the user's prompt, and it holds — more strongly than the
argument I first gave.** A Huntsman crucible furnace is a deep hole: pots on a grate, coke packed around, ash
pit below, flue above, air drawn up through the grate by chimney draught alone. Melting steel at ~1600 °C is at
the limit of what coke and natural draught reach — which is *why* those furnaces had famously tall stacks.
**Open flue damper = full draught = fiercest.** So U9.10 was not merely inconsistent with the puddling furnace;
it was **backwards for its own machine**.

★ The pot-cracking mechanic survives intact — only its polarity flips, and the historical sequence is a better
rhythm than the draft: **preheat shut/partial** (a cold pot brought up fast cracks — the crack check belongs
here), **melt OPEN** (full draught, the 1600 °C gate), **hold/anneal shut**. The damper is opened **once, in
the middle** — one deliberate act per heat rather than a state left set.

**Four more forks ruled and written into their owning tasks:**
- **Ignition: torch on the shaft branch, automatic on the firebox branch.** ⛔ This *rescopes* U4.9 — the
  automatic branch is on the shared core and the four firebox machines have no tap, so it must be **pushed down
  onto both branches**, not deleted. Lands in the file U3.6 already rewrites, so sequence it after.
- **Rivets yield MORE than nails** — a bundle at 12.5 u, 8 per 100 u against 4. That advantage *is* the trade
  U8.7's substitution rule sells. ⚠ Carried consequences: nails become dominated wherever iwex takes either, so
  the nail machine now survives on **build cost alone** (check it is meaningfully cheaper), and U8's Gate clause
  asserting equal yield must be **struck**.
- **The hopper delegates to the anchored core's `IsChargeItem`** — and the user widened this past the question
  asked: *"needed not only for the cupola; the heating furnace and beehive coke oven also use a hopper."* So one
  hopper serves four machines and each declares its own charge; the hopper stops being a fourth copy of a
  predicate that already exists in three places. ⚠ Each core must then admit its own **fuel** as well as its own
  charge, or a machine refuses the coke it burns.
- **The first-order heat chase is pushed down onto the firebox branch, not deleted.** It is on the *shared*
  tick and the firebox overrides no tick — deleting it makes every hearth, the reheat furnace and both U9
  machines hit their ceiling in **one tick**, and "buy temperature by building the chimney taller" becomes a
  step function. U3.7 Step 3 now **renames** the two rate keys off the `Bf` prefix rather than deleting them.

**And two more "forks" turned out not to be forks** — the docs already settle them, so they are closed by the
doc-wins rule rather than by asking: chilled charge is recovered by **splice-and-drop** (settled 2026-08-02 and
re-opened here by mistake; ⚠ the TakeTop escape hatch must not be taken, because a chill sits at the *bottom*
by definition, so "recoverable from the top" leaves the exact failure the task exists for unrecoverable while
the gate reads green), and U4.5 does **not** touch smex's hot furnace.

## 2026-08-05 — audit fixes applied: 4 rulings landed, 2 new tasks, 6 blocking defects closed

**Four forks ruled by the user and written into the tasks that own them**, not just into the header:
- **A shut damper = less draught = cooler** (U6.5's rule). ⚠ **U9.10 is the task that changes** — its Steps 1
  and 5 said the opposite; the crucible furnace now melts with the damper *open* and anneals with it shut.
  `CellRole.Damper` keeps meaning the chimney damper, so U9.9's reuse of `BlockPuddlingChimneyCap` survives.
- **Ignition stays a FRACTION of capacity** ⇒ `MinChargeToIgnite` **splits in two**: the fire threshold stays
  fractional, the cold-charge heat-penalty denominator becomes full geometric capacity. U3.7 Step 2 no longer
  collapses them, and U2.9's "27 of 36 charged" stays a status rather than a countdown.
- **The raceway slice spans a whole round** — `fuelFrac` reads the charged ratio steadily (~2.09 atm against a
  2.2 atm blower). The rejected reading swings 1.2 → 3.5 atm and closes the tuyere gate on every burden course.
- **`PuddlingProcessTempC = 1400`**, chosen physically rather than for convenience at the user's instruction.
  ★★ The mod's own melting points bracket it — `1200 < 1400 < 1482` — and *that bracket is the process*: as
  carbon leaves, the metal's melting point climbs past the bath and the iron **comes to nature**. The ball is
  emergent, not scripted. Both alternatives break it symmetrically (1482 melts it, 1200 freezes it hard).
  ★★ And 1400 sits **7.5 °C above** the flat-draught ceiling of 1392.5 that `puddling-furnace.md` computes —
  so the chimney is **load-bearing**, and the two fixes that page lists as *alternatives* are the same fix.

**Two blocking defects had no owning task; both are now real tasks:**
- **U2.2c** — `UnitsPerBand` becomes per-material, item-scale constants retire, plus the `ExConfigRegister`
  version reset. ⛔ Must land **before U2.7**, the first task to put coke and burden in one column. Its first
  step is the *mixed-density* test, because a single-material test passes under both models.
- **U3.0** — move the cold furnace's tuyeres y=1 → y=2. A pure drawing edit that four other places already
  assume has happened, that the cupola has already done, and that U10.4–U10.6 block on.

**Four more closed in place:** U9's entry condition and execution-order #6 now name **U6.4 + U6.5** (U6.3
produces neither, and the signature is 3 params not 2); U6.11 gains a step for the **firebox recipe** without
which U6's own gate cannot pass; the **crop-table collision** is resolved in U7.5's favour (its
`(form, section, stage)` key is strictly more expressive — its own first two rows are the same form at the same
thickness with different answers — with U8.3 keeping only `ShearFeed` and ruling 4's hundredths stage key); and
`SyncChargeBlocks` is now **public**, which is a code change: the tall hopper and smex's bell hopper both call
it from outside the class, the latter across an assembly boundary. Safe because it reads the columns and writes
the world, never the reverse — an outside caller cannot invent charge with it. **2638 green on 1.21.**

## 2026-08-05 — the plan audited twice: 170 defects and 22 forks

After being told twice was a pattern, swept the plan systematically instead of spot-checking: 14 agents across
two workflows, both read-only.

**Audit 1 — plan vs `docs/design/**` (9 agents, 80 findings: 4 blocking, 29 high, 33 medium, 14 low)** →
`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-05-iwex-plan-audit.md`. The rule applied: docs are authoritative for
*decisions*, the plan for *sequencing*. ⓘ **26 of the 80 run the other way** (`DOC_STALE` — the plan is right
and the doc is behind), so this is not "trust the docs"; it is "know which question each answers".

**Audit 2 — plan vs itself and vs `src/` (5 agents, 90 findings: 14 blocking, 32 high, 35 medium, 9 low, plus
22 forks)** → `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-05-iwex-plan-coherence.md`.

The blocking set, in the order it will bite:
- **`ChargeUnitsPerBand` must become a per-material lookup, not a bigger constant.** A band is a *volume*
  slice; a column holding both coke and burden has no single units-per-block — and **U2.7 creates exactly that
  column**. No task implements the table, and no task owns the recalibration at all: `IwexConfig.Migrations`
  is still `[]`. Every later formula ends up at the wrong scale with **nothing red**.
- **The tuyere move y=1 → y=2 is attributed to U3 in four places and exists in no U3 task.** The cupola's
  tuyere has already moved, so the two iwex furnaces currently disagree with each other; U10.4–U10.6 declare
  themselves blocked on a step nobody owns. ⚠ And U4.4 puts a live molten cell at y=1 while the blast still
  enters at that course — the exact arrangement the move exists to prevent.
- **`iwex:furnace-firebox` has no recipe and is a required cell in both reverberatory layouts.** U6.11 crafts
  "the four puddling blocks", a count inherited from a doc written before the firebox became a block. U6's own
  gate cannot pass. U9.4 does this diligence for the coke-oven lid; U6 needs the same step.
- **U9 is gated on the wrong task** — its entry condition names U6.3 for `NaturalDraughtFor` and the loss
  virtuals; U6.3 produces neither (U6.5 and U6.4 do), and the signature has 2 params against 3.
- **U7.5 and U8.3 both delete `RollSetSpec.Outputs` and both build a crop table** with incompatible keys.
- **U2.7/U2.8 call `SyncChargeBlocks()` from outside the class**, and it is `protected`.

Also corrected two things I had written into the plan myself hours earlier: the F1 ruling block still carried
the superseded "0.625 air ÷ 16 = 400 u = 4 pig ingots" arithmetic, and still framed the recalibration as *one
bigger constant* rather than a per-material table. Both now say what the 375 u/pig ruling actually implies.

## 2026-08-05 — ⚠⚠ the blast furnace is at bloomery PARITY; the guard-rail that would say so now exists

Chasing the last unit-scale number, the user pointed out the ×2 decision was already in the design docs. It
was — `roasting.md` § *Quantities* carries the whole recovery ladder: bloomery 50 % → **5 u/nugget** (the
anchor), blast furnace on raw ore ~85 % → **8.5**, on roasted ~92 % → **9.2**. So all three "missing" numbers
existed and none needed inventing. ★ Lesson: I asked before searching hard enough.

**The chain is 1:1 throughout** — `1 nugget → 1 crushed iron → 1 burden item` (`BlockEntityOreMixer` sets
`_burdenCount = TotalRaw`) — so at the reference grade (75 % ore / 5 % flux / 20 % coke) a band of raw ore
burden yields **6.375 u** and roasted **6.9 u**. That is the per-grade `UnitsPerBand` pair the ruling needed.

**⚠⚠ And deriving it exposed that the shipped furnace delivers no recovery advantage at all.**
`BfIronPerMeltCycle` 60 ÷ `BfBlastMixPerMeltCycle` 16 ÷ 0.75 ore share = **exactly 5.0 u/nugget** — bloomery
parity. The whole ironmaking chain currently buys the player nothing per ore over the bloomery it replaces.
★ This is the failure `roasting.md` § 7 predicted the missing guard-rail would hide, one notch subtler than it
expected: the chain is not a *downgrade*, so the invariant it specified would have passed. It is a **no-op**.

**Built the guard-rail it asked for and nobody wrote** — `OreRecoveryGuardRailTests` (3 cases). It derives
recovery from the three constants that imply it (they were tuned independently, which is exactly why it could
drift unseen), asserts the floor *"the iwex chain must never yield less iron per ore than a vanilla bloomery"*,
carries a non-vacuity control, and pins today's 5.0 so a rebalance is a deliberate edit with a visible diff.
**Hitting the designed 8.5 means `BfIronPerMeltCycle` 60 → 102** (8.5 × 0.75 × 16) — not applied; that is a
balance call, and both recovery figures are still marked *placeholder* upstream. **2638 green on 1.21.**

## 2026-08-05 — cutover mapped before cutting; one bounding-box bug fixed; the unit scale is a fork

Ran a 5-agent read-only map over the cutover surface (the three scene fixtures, smex's lifecycle tests, the
production deletion surface, and `ChargeColumn`'s exact API) rather than starting to edit. It found five things
the U2.3–U2.7 task bodies do not account for; all are now written into the plan under
*Pre-cutover findings*. The two that matter most:

**⛔⛔ F1 — the unit scale is undecided, and `mixCount` feeds three unrelated systems.** The pile model counted
coal-pile **items**; a column counts burden **units**, and 8 u/band × 16 bands = **128 u per cell** against
`HopperTallPileCap`'s **16 items** per cell. `mixCount` drives the ignition gate, `ComputeHeatBalance`'s
cold-charge loss term and the `DisruptionMixFloor` extinguish counter — return units where items were expected
and all three move, each still producing a plausible number. Exactly the class that hid the cupola threshold
bug for months.

**✅ Ruled by the user, and the ruling is bigger than the three options I offered.** The rate is **not a
constant**: it is **per ore grade** (roasted burden gives more units per item than raw), the blast furnace
yields **≈2× vanilla per crushed-ore item**, and cupola charge is **iron with air gaps over a coke layer**,
dense enough that its units come off the metal density rather than a burden grade. ★★ The structural
consequence — which none of my options had — is that **charge units and metal units are one currency**, both
on `1 vx³ = 2.5 u`. **★★ Then "one pig is 375 u" settled the cupola side outright: one pig is one BAND.** It is the only pig count
per band a block of loose iron can hold — 1 → 6 000 u/block = 58.6 % of solid iron, which is squarely where
loose irregular solids pack; 2 → 12 000 u, denser than solid iron. So the band quantum *is* the charge item:
16 pigs a cell, 4 cells, **64 pigs / 24 000 u** in a cupola shaft, each pig a visible layer, nothing to convert
in the player's head. ⓘ This replaced a guessed ⅝ air coefficient with a **derived** 58.6 % — the number was
not fitted to the 55–65 % packing range, it fell into it.

⛔⛔ **And it proves `ChargeUnitsPerBand` cannot stay a single constant.** A band is a *volume* slice, so pig
iron, raw burden and roasted burden have different units per volume — which is exactly what makes the per-grade
ruling implementable rather than decorative ("roasted yields more per item" = "roasted has a higher
`UnitsPerBand`"). Model: **one charge item = one band, `UnitsPerBand` is a per-material lookup.** Every
consumer that multiplies or divides by the global — `BlocksTall`, `BandsAt`, `SyncChargeBlocks`, `LowestUnits`
— has to take the segment's material instead, because a column holding both coke and burden has no single
units-per-block, and U2.7 is what introduces exactly that. ⚠⚠ That recalibrates `ChargeUnitsPerBand` from **8** to ~400–640 — a ~50–80× change — and retires
`BlastMixRequiredToFire`, `CupolaMixRequiredToFire` and `HopperTallPileCap` as item-scale constants, needing a
config version-reset so no world reinterprets saved numbers in the new currency. Written up in
`layered-charge.md` § *Charge units are METAL units*, with the three numbers still missing before it can be
built (the vanilla bloomery baseline, the roasted-vs-raw rate pair, and the ore-burden band density, which is
lighter than the cupola's).

**F2 — `BurnOutCharge` cannot be written as a `ChargeColumn` consumer at all.** `Segments` is a real
`ReadOnlyCollection` and there is no in-place segment rewrite; burn-out scales each band's Fuel part while
preserving Iron and Flux, interpolated by height, and Take-then-Push would collapse the bands through the merge
rule. U2.5's `Rewrite` API is a prerequisite, not a nicety. Also recorded: F3 (`Push` merges on exact float mix
equality **and** ≤1 °C, so a hopper dripping at live internal temperature starts a new segment per drip —
unbounded growth, no error), F4 (the charge caches ride the save tree, so changing `ReadChargeMix`'s meaning
changes values already on disk), F5 (the deletion surface includes smex's bell hopper's six `coalpile` sites,
and ⚠ `IwexCodes.ChargeShaft` must keep `coalpile` until the hopper stops making them — dropping it early makes
a hopper-charged shaft read *incomplete*, and incomplete means extinguish).

**✅ Fixed on the spot: `EnsureShaftColumns` minted columns off the shaft BOUNDING BOX.** A shaft that is not a
solid rectangle in plan got live, pushable columns for footprints the drawing marks nothing chargeable in.
Charge pushed there is counted by `ShaftChargeUnits`, walked by every handle and written to the save — but
`SyncChargeBlocks` skips it (no cells), so it could never become a block, never be dug out, and inflated the
fire threshold and the heat-balance term for the furnace's whole life. Nothing threw. Now keyed off
`ChargeableCells`. ⓘ Unreachable on the shipped shafts — both are solid rectangles above the hearth — so no
world is affected; pinned by an L-shaped fixture, the only thing in the suite that can distinguish the two key
sets. **2635 green on 1.21.**

## 2026-08-05 — ⛔ `run-tests.sh` was reporting PASS from a stale binary; cupola ignition bug; U2.3 groundwork

**⛔⛔ The harness defect first, because it invalidates numbers I reported earlier today.**
`scripts/run-tests.sh` wrote build failures to `$log_dir/buildfail` and **never read the file**. The test phase
then runs with `--no-build`, so a project that stopped compiling was tested as its **last good DLL** and
reported PASS. `LowPressureExpanded.Tests` had a `CS0104` (ambiguous `PatternItemDefinitions` — both iwex and
lpex now declare one, which is the seam `CastShellTests` exists to guard) and the runner printed
`PASS … Passed: 190` from a binary dated **Aug 4 23:42**.

⚠ The floor check could not catch it: a stale assembly has exactly the test count it went stale from. Nor could
the exit code — `dotnet test` succeeded, on the wrong bytes.

★ **Correction to today's earlier entries: the "2607 / 2612 / 2626 green" figures counted a stale lpex.** The
real lpex number is **198**, not 190 — the 8 missing were `CastShellTests` (6, written earlier this session)
and the two new cupola cases below. Nothing was wrong with the other four suites. **True total now: 2634.**

Fixed by failing the run before the test phase when anything failed to build, printing each failing target's
first errors. ⓘ `run-tests.ps1` was **already correct** (it carries `BuildOk` into the parallel block and
returns `build failed`) — only the bash runner, the one in use, threw the result away. This is the third
silent-pass mode this script has had to close; the note in it now names all three, because they share a root:
a green line must mean *the code in the tree ran*.

**★★ Cupola ignition: the furnace disagreed with itself about "full".** `BlockEntityShaftFurnace`'s charge read
set `isFull` from the literal `IwexValues.BlastMixRequiredToFire` (320) instead of `MinChargeToIgnite`, which
the cupola overrides to `CupolaMixRequiredToFire` (160). `isFull` is **not** a HUD flag — it *is* the ignition
gate (`State == Idle && StructureComplete && _cachedIsFull && !IsChoked`), while the same furnace's heat balance
used 160 for the same question. So a cupola demanded exactly **double** its own threshold to light.

⚠ Nothing caught it because `CupolaRig`'s default charge is **320** — the fixture had been set to the number
the bug wanted. A scenario suite can only ever prove its fixture's premises. Verified the new case genuinely
discriminates by reverting the one-line fix: 1 of 10 cupola tests fails, the expected one (the −1 negative
control does not light either way, which is what makes it a control).

**U2.3 groundwork, deliberately stopping short of the cutover.** `CollectCharge` cannot flip yet: nothing in
`src/` writes to a column (verified by grep — only `BlockEntityChargePile` and `BlockChargePile` *read* them),
so flipping the handle alone makes every shaft read empty and never fire. The cutover is atomic with U2.4
(consume), U2.6 (fixtures) and U2.7 (charging). Landed the parts that stand alone and green:
`Burden.IsCode/IsRemeltCode/IsAnyCode/FamilyOfCode` beside their `ItemStack` siblings, and
`BlockEntityFurnaceCore.IsChargeCode/AcceptsChargeCode`. A `ChargeSegment` holds a material **code string**,
not a stack — that is what lets a band split without splitting a stack — so every charge gate needs a code
form. `ChargeCodeGateTests` (14) asserts each case against **both** forms rather than a literal, because the
failure mode is silent drift: the same burden accepted through one route and refused through the other, with
each answer individually plausible.

## 2026-08-05 — the shaft stops writing over blocks it did not place (+ a second index bug next to it)

**Provoked by the question** *"when the furnace starts smelting the hearth cells become iron blocks, so
chargepiles cannot be there — should we dynamically adjust column height?"*

**⚠ I answered it from the code first and got it wrong; corrected on the same day.** `BlockEntityShaftFurnace`
keeps its pool in a `float` pair and spawns blocks only at extinguish, so I read the collision as a one-shot at
shutdown. **That is the old model.** Per `layered-charge.md` § *The crucible*, the pool is a **live**
`iwex:hearthmetal` cell that appears the moment melting starts and stands for the whole campaign — which is
what let the taps become two authentic notches in one wall rather than two courses apart. U4.4 deletes the
float pair and the extinguish-only spawn. Lesson: on this subsystem the design doc leads the code by two
tasks; read § *The crucible* before reasoning about the hearth.

**Ruling: heights do not adjust dynamically — the crucible stops being a charge cell instead.** That is
already **U4.5**, planned in full, and both layouts carry a comment predicting it. A floor that moved would be
the wrong shape of fix anyway: it makes shaft *capacity* a function of world state, and `ChargeColumn` cannot
express "more units than I have cells to draw" — the surplus silently stops rendering.

**The bug the question exposed, and why it still matters given U4.5.** `SyncChargeBlocks` wrote over
*whatever* stood in the cell. U4.5 lands well after U2 cuts the shaft to columns, so across that window the
crucible is chargeable *and* the furnace puts blocks in it — U4.4 Step 4 has `ConsumeForMelting` place
`hearthmetal` in a free pool cell, and an unguarded sync replaces it with charge on the very next call. Not one
overwrite: a **per-tick fight between two writers**. Now a cell holding anything but air or the shaft's own
pile is **skipped**; the column draws one block short while the cell is taken and heals when it frees up, and
the units stay in the column so nothing is silently eaten.

**★★ And a second bug found while fixing the first, unrelated to the crucible.** `blockIndex` was
`y - ColumnFloorY` — a height difference. Placement fills the cell *list* from index 0. Those are the same
function only for a **contiguous** column, and every shipped column is contiguous, so nothing could see it.
Give a column a hole (chargeable at y=1, 2, 4; brick at 3) and the pile at y=4 is placement's third block and
`y - floor`'s fourth — it would draw bands 48–63 of a column holding only 0–47 and render empty.
`ChargeCellsOf(x, z)` is now the single ordered source both sides read; `ColumnFloorY` survives as the floor
and is no longer what any index is measured from.

**Plan corrections that fell out.** U4.5 Step 3 said the cupola's chargeable set drops "from 6 to 5" — it is
**5 to 4** (four `c` cells plus one `h`; `The_cupola_offers_the_five_cells_of_its_single_column` pins 5 today).
U2.5 Step 0 rewritten to stop treating the crucible as an open question and to warn against designing the
residue from `SolidifyBottomLayer`, which U4.4 Step 7 deletes. The U4.5 cost is now recorded once in
`layered-charge.md`: −7.7 % cold, −5.3 % smex hot (⚠ explicitly *not* in scope), **−20 % cupola** — the one to
watch, and per § *Sizing* the answer there is a taller course, never the dual role back. Both iwex shafts
become uniform-floored afterwards, so `ColumnFloorY` / `ChargeCellsOf` keep coverage only through the synthetic
hole fixture — flagged in U4.5 Step 3 so it is not deleted as unused.

Tests: `ChargeMaterialisationTests` gains *A hole in the column* (2) and *Cells the furnace does not own* (3).
Both new fixtures fail against the old code. **2612 green on 1.21** (was 2607), zero skips.

## 2026-08-05 — two rulings recorded before compaction

**★★ Each column has its own height — that is what the column IS.** Not one shaft height with nine blocks
drawn off it: nine independent columns, each with its own `TotalUnits`, its own `BlocksTall`, and its own
floor. A furnace charged unevenly stands unevenly. ⓘ This is what `SyncChargeBlocks` and `ColumnFloorY`
already do, so nothing changed in code — but it is now stated in `layered-charge.md` as the thing anything
touching this must not give up, with `ShaftBox` named as the standing temptation (it is right there, it is one
box, and it is a flat-floored bounding box larger than the marked cells).

**★ The slag block becomes a player-placeable, layered slag pile** — coalpile-style, height = count, place and
take a course at a time. Recorded as **U2.2b** in the expansion and as a design section in
`layered-charge.md`. Two things make it more than a chore: it **closes the producer gap** noted the same day
(the player placing it *is* the producer, so a tapped-slag route can arrive later as a *second* source rather
than the only one), and it turns `SlagCount` from a number only a Harmony patch could write into a pile's own
layer count. ⚠ `SlagCount` is already live and already saved, so the conversion has to decide explicitly
whether an old block's count maps to layers 1:1 — otherwise the first load silently changes what a player's
block is worth. ⓘ `BEBehaviorFirebox` is the closer model, not `BlockChargePile`: a slag pile holds one
material and needs no window into anything.

## 2026-08-05 — U2.1 + U2.2: the charge becomes visible, and the shaft turns out to have no floor

Five suites green on 1.21: **2607** (+16). U2 has started; the cutover's first half is in.

**U2.1** was mostly already done (QW1 fixed `PileCode`, the pin test exists, the relight anchor is green).
What was left was one recorded consequence, and it is worth stating because a later cleanup would read it
wrong: `BlastmixPiles.ConvertToSlag` is the **only writer of `BlockEntitySlag.SlagCount` in the mod** —
verified, every other mention is the property, its save/load pair, its HUD line, and `GetDrops` *reading* it.
Deleting burn-to-slag therefore leaves `iwex:slag-block` with block, entity, drops and lang but **no
producer**. ★ That is a reason to give it one (tapped slag freezing in a canal), not a reason to delete it —
a grep finding "nothing writes `SlagCount`" is finding a gap in the *tap* path, not a dead block.

**U2.2 is `SyncChargeBlocks()`** — idempotent reconciliation of world to columns, walking
`CellRole.Chargeable` and never `ShaftBox`, calling `OnColumnChanged()` on every surviving pile in a touched
column. Not yet wired to a tick; U2.4 and U2.7 do that.

**★★ And it found a defect the task map did not predict: the shipped shaft has no single floor.** The cold
blast furnace's drawing marks three crucible cells `Chargeable` at **y=1** — the middle row only — and nine
per course at y=2..5. So `ShaftBox`'s floor is 1 while **six of the nine columns** cannot hold charge below 2.
Two consequences, and the second is the nasty one:

- placing from the box floor would write charge blocks **into the tuyere and brick cells** either side of the
  crucible;
- `ChargeColumnAt`'s `blockIndex` was *already* measured from the box, so an outer column's first pile would
  ask for bands 16–31 of a column holding only 0–15 — **a physically present block rendering empty** until
  the column stood two blocks tall.

Both now measure from `ColumnFloorY(x, z)`, each column's own lowest chargeable cell, which is what makes
them agree with `ChargeColumn.BlocksTall` (it counts from the column's base). ⓘ **On a uniform shaft the two
readings are identical** — which is exactly why every test written before the crucible was widened passed
either way, and why `A_shipped_furnace_still_resolves_its_own_square_shaft` asserted `4` for an outer column
and was believed. It is renamed, re-argued and now asserts `3`, plus the outer floor reading `0`.

`StructureRig` gained a `World` accessor: `FurnaceLayoutRig.Stand` builds the world itself, so a caller that
needs to register a block type or a BE factory had no handle on it, and reflecting into a private field is not
something a test harness should make its callers do.

## 2026-08-05 — the casting names settle on the drawn art, and two molds retire

Five suites green on 1.21: **2591**.

**The editable names were the correct ones, so the code moved to them, not the other way round.**
`bfc` → `furnace-coldblast`, `cf` → `furnace-cupola`, `heavyplate` → `castheavyplate`,
`molddoubleingot` → `castingotmold`, and — the interesting pair — `castbillet` → **`castbillets`**,
`castbloom` → **`castblooms`**.

★★ **The plurals are a rule, not a spelling.** A pattern is named for what its *impression yields*: three
billets from one pour, two blooms, one slab. The item stays singular, because one item is one billet. That
broke the old `pattern type = "cast" + form` derivation, which is a good thing — it was a string
concatenation standing in for a decision. `CastStockItemDefinitions.Forms` now carries both spellings
explicitly. ⚠ The same slice lived in a test (`type["cast".Length..]`), where it would have quietly asked
`UnitsOf("billets")`, got 0 back, and passed `lanes * 0 == 0` by luck; that test is now parameterised on the
form and looks the pattern up.

**Two molds retired, and both for the same reason: the mill already makes it.** The plate mold cast
`game:metalplate-*`, a rolled product — a second route to the mill's own output. The double-ingot mold went
with it. What survives is **one single-bay ingot mold**, kept because crucible steel has to be poured into
something (U9.11). It follows the drawn tray: `requiredUnits` 200 → **100**, drops 2 ingots → **1**, and the
block is `casting-mold-ingot` on the new one-bay art rather than the old two-bay export.

**`molten-barrel` as a diagram type is gone too.** The barrel's cast route is planned by the *pattern*
diagram `item-castbarrel`, which makes the blank the `molten-barrel-cast` variant is lined from; a second
structure diagram for the finished vessel planned the same thing twice. Both barrel variants already have
their shapes, so nothing else changes.

**★★ Two migrations, and they are the part I would not skip.** `CastingNameMigration` remaps every renamed
pattern and diagram variant, `CastMoldIngotMigration` the mold block. A pattern is a **durable tool the
player carved**, from a diagram they drafted; an item whose code stops resolving is dropped silently on world
load, with no error and no way to tell it from a bug. ⓘ The three *retired* codes are deliberately **not**
remapped — remapping a retired thing onto a surviving one hands the player an item they never had. A retired
block is allowed to lapse.

**Where the diagram audit stands now:** 68 drawn, 27 declared, and **one** name mismatch left —
`item-castwheelsection` (declared, and the user's own word for the item) against
`diag-item-castflywheelsegment.png` (drawn). Everything else reconciled. That one is a question, not a
finding.

## 2026-08-04 (later) — the shell goes to lpex, the flywheel gets its plan, and lpex gets a casting bootstrap

Four directives, all landed. Five suites green on 1.21: **2592**.

**★★ `castshell` moved to lpex, and the move paid for itself.** iwex had no consumer for a shell and I had
been about to invent one (a U8.8 step spending it on the mp benches — now struck). The honest fix for an
orphan part is to put it where something already wants it: lpex's water tank, ore crusher and engines. What
that required was the thing worth having — **lpex now has a casting bootstrap**. Two factories in iwex went
public (`PatternItemDefinitions.Itemtype`, `DiagramItemDefinitions.Itemtype`), so **iwex owns the pattern
system and each mod contributes entries**; lpex ships its own `pattern` and `diagram` itemtypes, its own
carve recipe, and `lpex:castshell`. The iwex casting cell reads the mold spec off whichever pattern is held,
so **neither mod names the other's item codes anywhere**. That indirection is the whole reason the spec lives
on the pattern, and this is the first time it has been exercised. `CastShellTests` guards it from the lpex
side — the only side that can see both. ⓘ `patterns.md` Open 3 drops from *"lpex has no casting folder at
all"* to one `Molds` row per remaining part.

**★★ Both flywheel grids are diagram-led, and flat.** Per `diagram-crafting.md` a diagram-crafted recipe is
**the plan plus a bill of materials** — one cell per distinct ingredient, carrying its quantity. My first
version spread the four rim sections across the corners of a 3×3 so the grid *looked* like a wheel. It reads
nicely and it is the wrong idiom: the grid then encodes the assembly the diagram is already responsible for.
Now `DW,PG` and `DSW`. The test asserts the diagram **and** the one-cell rule together, because the pretty
version is the one that creeps back.

**⚠ I generated placeholder diagram art that was not needed.** The user pointed at four already-drawn
sheets; the real set turns out to be **68 textures** covering every furnace, the burdenmaker, the three mp
benches, the crucible hearth, the charge doors and the whole finished-item family. Placeholders deleted, real
art wired. Two lessons recorded in memory: **check `assets/editable/textures/diagrams/` before drawing
anything**, and *generated* assets go to `assets/editable/remake/`, never into a domain folder.

**Two flywheel diagrams, not one.** I had declared a single `flywheel` type on the reasoning that the large
wheel is an upgrade of the normal one. The art declares `diag-mpenergy-flywheel` **and**
`diag-mpenergy-flywheellarge`, and it is right — a 5×5×2 wheel is a different drawing. Type names now follow
the art, which follows the block codes.

**Audit while I was in there:** every declared diagram type has its shipped texture — **0 missing, 0
orphaned**, across 29 types in two mods. But the *editable* names have drifted from the shipped type names in
nine places (`bfc` ← `furnace-coldblast`, `item-heavyplate` ← `item-castheavyplate`, `item-castbillet` ←
`item-billets`, …), and `item-moldplate` / `molten-barrel` have no editable source under any name. Same
rename-in-flight as the shapes; a reconciliation, not a defect. Recorded in the expansion.

⚠ **Untracked and yours to `git add`:** `assets/lpex/shapes/item/castshell.json`,
`assets/lpex/textures/item/diagram/diag-item-castshell.png`,
`assets/iwex/textures/item/diagram/diag-{item-castwheelsection,mpenergy-flywheel,mpenergy-flywheellarge}.png`,

## 2026-08-04 — QW2 + QW9: the firebox branch's tick gets its first test, and a hearth turns out to be completable

**★★ The premise QW2 was written under was wrong, in our favour.** `FireboxChargeTests` said driving the
charge hooks directly was *"the only way to reach them at all"* because neither reverberatory hearth can
complete a structure. In game that is still true (U6.1 — both layouts declare filler cells no part produces).
**In the harness it is not**: `StructureRig.Raise` synthesises a stand-in for every empty footprint cell, so
the hearth completes and its own `OnProductionTick` runs. The note has been corrected in place.

So `FireboxTickTests` stands a **shipped** heating-furnace layout, replaces the rig's stand-ins in every
`CellRole.Firebox` cell with a real full `BEBehaviorFirebox` bed, and drives the furnace's own tick.
`A_loaded_hearth_lights_itself_on_its_own_production_tick` is the guard QW2 was actually for: U3.7 deletes the
`Bf*` cadence keys, and without something driving this branch's tick that deletion leaves both hearths
permanently Idle with a green suite — every existing firebox test asserts on hooks reached *before* the
cadence is consulted. Two controls sit either side of it (empty, and one unit short of full) so a hearth that
lit unconditionally could not satisfy it.

**⚠⚠ The fuel clock could not be pinned, and finding out why was the interesting part.** The first version
asserted "fires for `MaxFuelBurnTime`, then goes out" and failed. The cause is **B8's fifth, undocumented
cause**, which U6.3 already names: a full firebox holds `cells × 12` units against `DisruptionMixFloor`, the
shaft's **144**, inherited unchanged. A full hearth is therefore simultaneously *full enough to light* and
*too empty to stay lit* — it fires, counts one disruption on the same tick, and snuffs on the extinguish
grace. `MaxFuelBurnTime` is unreachable. That is now asserted both as arithmetic and as behaviour, so
**U6.3's fix will make the test fail**, and its failure message says what to rewrite it into. A guard that
goes quiet when the defect is fixed would have been worth nothing.

**QW9, widened.** The plan asked for one assertion that `FurnaceLayoutRig.HearthGlyph` still equals
`IwexCodes.HearthCell`. The rig hand-copies **two** of these strings — `ShaftGlyph` from `ChargeShaft` as
well — and both are literals, so changing a production constant compiles cleanly and leaves the rig asserting
against the old spelling. Both are pinned. ⓘ The duplication itself is deliberate and stays: a rig deriving
its glyphs from the production constants could not catch a wrong constant, because both sides would move
together.

Five suites green on 1.21: **2588**.

## 2026-08-04 — U1.5 closed: `castshell` + `castwheelsection` ship, and a mass invariant nobody had

Both parts existed as art and as prose in five design docs; neither existed as an item. Ownership was settled
earlier the same day (they are **iwex's** — the wheel section feeds the flywheel, the shell is generic
machine-structure stock), which is what unblocked this.

**What shipped.** Two itemtypes (`CastPartItemDefinitions`), two `Molds` entries — which carry two diagram
variants and two diagram→pattern carve recipes for free, since both derive from `PatternItemDefinitions.PatternTypes`
— two runtime item shapes exported from the renamed editables, two placeholder diagram textures, ten lang keys
in each of en/ru/uk, and six goldens re-blessed **by explicit path** (never `=1`), each read before being kept.
Five suites green on 1.21: **2578**, up 6.

**★★ 600 u, not the 540 the density rule computes — and this is the interesting decision.** Both parts are
drawn at 216 vx³, which at 2.5 u/vx³ prices them at 540; `cast-parts.md` recorded exactly that, under a column
headed *"Rule says"*. It was never a settled mass. The rule sizes art **plausibly**; the mass is declared so
the economy divides — the discipline `CastStockItemDefinitions`' header already states.

The argument for 600 is N3's: a cast structural part and its rolled/riveted equivalent are **alternatives, not
tiers**. `bending.md` already pins both fabricated halves at 600 u (a `boilerplate` 15 × 1 × 16 bent into a
shell; a `heavyplate` 12 × 2 × 10 bent into a rim). At 540 the cast route is quietly 10% cheaper in metal and
the choice stops being a choice; at 600 the two cost the same iron and differ only in the **plant** they demand
— cupola and pattern versus mill, roller and rivets. That is the mod's core trade stated in one number. It also
lands on the cast-stock ladder's 600 u quantum, so one shell is one billet of metal.

**★★ The new invariant is the general one, and it was missing entirely.** A pattern declares `capacity`; the
item it casts declares `materialUnits`; **nothing connected them**. Drift either way mints or eats metal with
no error, no log line and no visibly wrong cast — the same silent shape as an output naming nothing, one field
along. `CastMassParityTests` pins `capacity == cavity.Length × materialUnits` across every item-output pattern
(a single-cell pattern is the one-lane case, so there is one rule rather than two), with a canary asserting the
reader can see through `attributesByType` — otherwise the loop would `continue` past every row and pass forever.
It holds for all seven today.

**The flywheel's rim is now what it looks like.** `WPW,PGP,WPW` — four arcs closing a circle, four plates
spoking it to the hub, the gear coupling it to the run; the large wheel is `SSS,SWS,SSS`, eight arcs around the
normal one, because a 5×5 rim has twice the circumference. ⚠ **This is a deliberate cost increase**: 8 heavy
plates (1280 u) → 3040 u. The network's whole energy reservoir being two blooms of iron never read as the
capital purchase it is, and the wheel is precisely what buys the efficiency four machines share.

**⚠ `castshell` has no consumer**, and it cannot have one yet. A shell is a *cupola* product, so gating the
tall hopper (blast-furnace charging) or the burdenmaker (which makes the burden the furnace eats) on it
deadlocks a fresh world — the same trap the tuyere's `lpex:pipe-straight*` ingredient once was. U8.8 gained a
**Step 0** spending one shell per bench, which is the earliest legitimate point.

**Docs trued up, because five of them asserted these parts did not exist.** `patterns.md` filed both under
**lpex** (wrong, and it would have made an iwex machine depend on an optional mod) and listed its orphan count
at twelve — now six, with Opens 1 and 2 struck as already done. `casting-cell.md`'s Gotcha 10 said six orphan
impressions; four. `cast-parts.md` still claimed `castplate-heavy` had "no recipe, no RCC stage, no code
reference" — it has two consumers. `bending.md` and `bending-roller.md` said the outputs did not exist; three of
four now do, so what is actually missing there is the *fabricated route to a part that already exists*, which is
the easier half.

⚠ **Two placeholder textures.** `diag-item-{castshell,castwheelsection}.png` are generated line drawings on the
authentic parchment (base lifted from a shipped diagram, shipped ink colour) — readable and on-palette so
nothing renders as a missing texture, but they want redrawing by hand with the rest of the set.

⚠ **Four new files are untracked** and `git add` is the user's: `assets/iwex/shapes/item/castshell.json`,
`…/castwheelsection.json` and the two textures — plus the 38 runtime shapes already noted below.
