## 2026-08-04 — Furnace failure modes settled; four rulings; one correction to my own record

**★★ The two failure modes, and they are opposites** *(user spec, now in
[`layered-charge.md`](../../design/layered-charge.md) § *Two failure modes, and they are opposites*)*:

| | **Breach** — walls broken while lit | **Choke** — no air through the tuyeres |
|---|---|---|
| Physically | the shaft is **opened**; natural draught means *more* air reaches the coke | the shaft is **sealed and starved**; nothing gets in |
| The fire | ★ **keeps burning** — it stops being a furnace, not a fire | **smothers**, quickly |
| Temperature | falls to `blastSupplyFrac = 0` → **~970 °C** | falls to ambient |
| Product | none — 970 °C is below iron's 1500, so it burns without smelting | none |
| Ends when | the raceway coke runs out, on its own | the extinguish grace expires |
| Recovery | break the piles for **burden *and* remaining coke** | `BurnOutCharge` salvage, unchanged |

★★ **~970 °C is not a new number.** It is the shipped heat balance's blowers-off row, already in the
calibration table de-tautologised earlier the same day ("natural draught only — stalls"). The breach
behaviour is already computable; the code merely has to stop killing the furnace before it gets there.

★★ **It retires the worst trap in the U2 map.** That map's first ⚠⚠ was that with no stored lit bit, the only
thing stopping a furnace re-firing the tick after burn-out is that burn-out left no fuel — and today's
`BurnOutCharge` only scales `BurdenMix.Fuel`, leaving `game:coke` segments whole, so it would re-fire and
decay its own salvage geometrically. It cannot: the ignition gate is
`State == Idle && StructureComplete && !IsChoked`, and **whatever ended the campaign is still true**. Both
failure modes get their correct ending from the gate that already exists.

⚠⚠ **Correction to my own record.** I wrote that the settled ruling was *"lit is stored **nowhere**"*. That
was an over-statement of the design, not the design: `layered-charge.md` has always said **lit is
furnace-level**. What dissolves is the *timer-driven FSM* — `Firing`/`Melting` become derived reads and the
`Bf*` cadence keys become emergent. The failure spec is what makes this unambiguous, because a breached
furnace has to stay lit after losing its blast. The decision row in the parent plan has been corrected.

**Two code consequences, written into U2:**
- `BlockEntityFurnaceCore.OnStructureLost` calls `Extinguish()` today — the wrong response to a breach. It
  must force `blastSupplyFrac` to zero and keep ticking. The choke path is already correct.
- `BlockChargePile` carries `NoDrops`, justified as *"a pile that dropped items would duplicate the charge"* —
  sound only while the furnace is authoritative. It must **debit the column** via the mid-span removal
  `layered-charge.md` already calls for, and drop what it removed. ⓘ Breaking one pile takes **one block's
  slice** (128 u at the shipped 8 u/band), not the column; everything above falls, so the block that
  disappears is the **top** one.

**Three further rulings** *(recorded in the expansion's amendment)*:
- ★ **`castshell` and `castwheelsection` are iwex's** — wheel section for the flywheel, shell as generic
  machine-structure stock. This settles the ownership conflict that deferred U1.5's part items. **U1.5 is
  unblocked.** `castframe` stays lpex's, existence still open.
- ★ **`OreProcessing/` ends up holding the burdenmaker alone.** Mixer and bunker predate the burden/furnace
  rework and are to be removed (U5.7). An **ore crusher** joins later with **lpex**.
- ⏳ **Open: which systems become behaviours.** `BlockNetworkNode` is an abstract base class with 8
  subclasses, and the cost is already visible — `BlockFlywheel` and `BlockRollingMill` hand-implement
  `IFillerHost` because they cannot also extend `BlockFilledMegastructure`, and **zero** megastructures
  derive from `BlockNetworkNode` because none can. `INetworkConnector` (9 implementors, all machines) is the
  escape hatch for exactly that. ⚠ Before recommending the extraction, check whether the graph walk depends
  on `Block` identity in ways a behaviour cannot reach — **not yet checked**.

Docs only this entry; no code changed. **2572 tests green on 1.21.**

## 2026-08-04 — U2–U10 expanded and verified; phase 0 executed; the bed's sand variant dropped

**The expansion.** `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-04-iwex-u2-u10-expansion.md` — **87 tasks, 707 steps**,
produced by nine parallel mapping agents each required to verify every claim against `src/` and cite the
file:line it read. It is the companion the parent plan always intended: that document specifies U2–U10 at
task level *by design*, to be expanded once the interfaces they consume exist.

★★ **They found 92 stale claims in the parent plan, and four of them change the execution order:**

1. **"U5 blocked by U4" is wrong** — `BlockStructures/OreProcessing/` is five files, none touched by any
   furnace unit. U5.1–U5.7 can run in phase 1. That is a whole parallel lane the plan gave away.
2. **"U10 blocked by U4" is false twice over** — `BlockNetworkNode.cs` contains no `ExOrientable`
   reference at all, and `BlockFurnaceTap` is not a `BlockNetworkNode`. U10.1–U10.3 are startable today.
3. **"`CollectCharge()` and `EnumerateChargePiles()` have no overrides anywhere" is half false** —
   `BlockEntityFireboxFurnace` overrides `CollectCharge`, and its own doc-comment calls itself "the firebox
   branch's cutover". So U2 is the *second half* of a seam that exists, not the creation of one. The
   companion claim that the reverberatory hearths "keep returning vanilla piles" is simply wrong.
4. **U3's deletion of the `Bf*` cadence keys has a fourth reader the plan never named** — the cupola's
   parallel `Cupola{MaxFuelBurnTime,MeltStartDelay,MeltIntervalSec}` triplet.

⚠ All nine agents independently reported being **misdirected by the plan's line anchors** — the coalpile
sniff is not at `:968`, the scenario literals are not at `:482-483`, and there is no `BlockEntityBlastFurnace.cs`
at all. The expansion carries a "navigate by symbol, not by line number" warning at the top.

**Phase 0 — executed.**

- **QW1 — `BlockChargePile.PileCode` was a live latent defect**, and the plan had it filed only as a trap.
  It said `iwex:chargepile`; the block has **never** shipped under that code — every furnace part shares
  `iwex:furnace` with a `type` variant (N7), so the def renders `iwex:furnace-chargepile`. Nothing read it
  yet, so nothing was broken — but the first materialisation written against it would have resolved
  **null**, skipped every `SetBlock`, and left a charged furnace drawing no piles with the suite green.
  Fixed and pinned against the rendered def *and* the generated table.
- **QW3 — the heat-balance calibration table was a tautology.** `FullHearth => IwexValues.BlastMixRequiredToFire`
  put the same config key in the numerator and the denominator of `mixCount / requiredMix`, so all six
  expected temperatures survived any change to the one key the table exists to pin. Now the literal `320`.
- **QW5 — one line in `convert-shape.py` that would have shipped three self-closing doors.** The comment
  above `HOLD_CLIPS` names the ore mixer's `open` clip as *the precedent* and the set did not contain it,
  so any gate, door or lid whose held state is `open` exported as `Repeat` and animated itself shut. ⚠ No
  C# test can see this — the rewrite happens in the export.
- **QW10 — the cost catalogue was decorative as a gate.** Its own summary calls itself "a curated list of
  every grid recipe this mod ships"; the only check was for *overlapping* selectors, so a block with **no
  row at all** passed. **25 craftable families had none** — the cupola core, the twin-tub blower, the tall
  hopper, all four bolted pipes, the entire mp-energy line, all three casting stations, the slag brick set,
  the design table and the rolling mill. Added, plus the coverage assertion that found them. ⓘ Adding a row
  is behaviour-neutral at `normal` (the baseline is read from the live recipe); it only stops the content
  being silently exempt from the mod's own economy switch.
- **QW4** — the five per-suite totals recorded as a dated baseline in `scripts/test-floors.txt`, so a
  future floor *drop* has something to justify itself against. U5 and U2 both delete tests on purpose.

⚠ **My own bug worth recording:** the first version of the coverage assertion matched against the
catalogue's dictionary **keys** and reported 231 uncovered blocks. The keys are deliberately decoupled
stable config identifiers (`blastfurnacecore-grid`) so a rename does not orphan a player's edited
`ex_recipes.json`; the selectors are the `Match` values. A test that reports on absence is very easy to
make over-report.

**The casting bed's `sand` variant group deleted** *(user correction)*. The bed still filled from raw
`game:sand-{sand}` and carried the rock type over vanilla's 20-state `block/rock` property — twenty times
the block codes to record a purely cosmetic fact. Green sand replaced raw sand as the one moulding
material, so there is nothing left to record: the RCC stage now requires `iwex:greensand`, the code is
`casting-sandbed-{brick}-{side}`, and the `andesite` texture key is a constant exactly as on the 1×1 cell.
ⓘ iwex has never shipped, so no migration is owed for the dropped group.

**2572 tests green on 1.21**, zero skips.

## 2026-08-04 — U1.4 the long cell, and U1.5's catalogue invariant

**U1.4 — the long cell. Done.** `LongCellLayout` (one filler at `(0,0,1)`, `MaxLaneLength = 24`),
`BlockSandCastingLongCell` (1×2 `BlockFilledMegastructure`, `StructureAngle = side + 180`, filler clicks
rerouted to the principal through `IFillerInteractionTarget`), `BlockEntitySandCastingLongCell`, a grid
recipe, lang in three locales, block-code table regenerated and two goldens written and read.

★ **The block entity is a four-line subclass, not a second state machine.** The long cell *is* the 1×1
cell's loop at a bigger size — ram, imprint, pour, shake out — so `BlockEntitySandCastingCell` grew two
`protected virtual` members (`AcceptedSize`, `WrongSizeErrorCode`) and the long cell overrides them.
Copying the entity would have meant two copies of the intake, the misrun rule, the harvest denomination
and the renderer, drifting apart on the first fix applied to either.

⚠⚠ **A recipe bug caught only by reading the golden: the first draft was a 3 × 4 grid.** The vanilla
crafting grid is 3 × 3, so it would have passed every test in the suite and been **uncraftable in the
world** — the same shape of failure as the ore mixer that shipped with no recipe at all. Doubled through
quantity instead: 12 bricks and 4 fire clay in the 1×1 cell's own 3×3 pattern. ★ This is the argument for
reading a golden rather than accepting it: nothing in the suite models the grid's dimensions.

ⓘ The long-cell test asserts the filler's position as a **property** — one cell away, same Y, four facings
giving four different cells — rather than four hard-coded positions, plus one behavioural statement
(*the body extends away from the side the cell faces*). At 1×2 the filler position is the only thing that
can be wrong, and a hard-coded table would have to be recomputed rather than re-read if the rotation
convention were ever re-based.

**U1.5 — the catalogue invariant. Done; the new part items deferred, with a reason.**

★★ **`DefinitionCatalogue` (new, in exlib.testing) + `CastPartCatalogueTests`.** A mold output naming
nothing does not throw and does not log: the stack resolves to null, so the cast fills, hardens, and
shake-out yields **nothing** — the metal is gone and the player cannot tell it from a misrun. This is the
mold-pattern twin of the grid-recipe check written earlier this week after six recipe outputs turned out to
name no block. It resolves **items and blocks together**, because a `JsonItemStack`'s `type` decides which
registry it lands in, and matches with `WildcardUtil` rather than equality (worldproperty groups cannot be
enumerated headlessly). A stack from an outside domain reads as resolvable — the harness cannot see
vanilla's registry, and answering "missing" for every `game:` output would bury our own dangling codes.

⚠ `The_catalogue_check_can_actually_fail` is deliberate: this test reports on **absence**, which is the one
shape of check that cannot prove itself by passing. A resolver stuck at `true` would keep it green forever.

**⚠ Deferred: the remaining cast-part items** (`castshell`, `castwheelsection`, `rollers-blank`,
`cylinderblank`, the gear blanks). Not scope creep avoidance — **the docs contradict each other on which
mod owns them**, and nothing in `src/` settles it:

- `iwex.md:103` lists `castframe`, `castshell`, `castwheelsection`, `castplate-heavy` as things the
  casting/long cell casts;
- `PatternItemDefinitions`' own class doc says lpex ships its own `pattern` itemtype for the machine parts
  (axle, gear blanks, cylinder, **frame**);
- `long-cell.md:259` calls `castframe` "an lpex product that does not exist" and leaves its existence open.

Verified: **lpex has no `pattern` itemtype**, and `castshell` / `castwheelsection` / `rollers-blank` are
referenced by no C# anywhere. Guessing here would put an iwex pattern on another mod's product, which is
exactly the coupling the spec-on-the-pattern design exists to avoid. The invariant above means whichever
mod adds them cannot add one whose output names nothing.

`IronworkingExpanded.Tests` floor raised 1132 → 1179. **2570 tests green on 1.21**, zero skips.

## 2026-08-04 — U1.1–U1.3 executed; U1.4 blocked on one measurable design call

**U1.1 — the casting cell refuses a long-cell pattern. Done.** `MoldSpec.Size` parsed, validated and
round-tripped through two tests while being **read by nothing in `src/`**. It has a consumer now: a
non-`Cell` spec is refused with `iwex-castingcell-wrongsize` (en/ru/uk). ★ The test that matters is
`The_refused_pattern_leaves_the_cavity_capacity_untouched` — `Imprint` sets the molten cell's capacity from
the spec and nothing else ever clears it, so a slipped-through longcell pattern left a 1×1 cell claiming a
**3000 u** cavity permanently. New `CastingCellScenes` fixture drives real `OnInteract` routing, not the
private handlers, because `CastingCellLogic.Decide` is half the behaviour.

⚠ Fixture bug worth remembering: a bare `new Item{}` has `Durability = 0`, so the first `DamageItem` takes
it to −1 — the **tool-breaks** path, which nulls the slot and reaches for `byEntity.SidedPos`. A pattern is
a durable tool; a fixture pattern that shatters on contact tests a state the game never reaches.

**U1.2 — the cast-stock masses. Settled and pinned.** `CastStockItemDefinitions`: billet **600**, bloom
**1000**, slab **3000**, with a `Forms` table the item defs, the lane capacities and the tests all read.

★★★ **I derived a redraw list twice and it was wrong both times. Recording why, because the pull to
re-derive it is strong.** I treated the density rule as an invariant binding a voxel count to a mass —
first against the item shapes, then against the sand cavities. Neither is authoritative:

- **item shapes are settled art**, already mapped for derived material and siding; the rolled stages and
  `StockForm`'s spread widths are computed off their base dimensions, so they are sized for the forming
  line and re-sectioning one to satisfy a mass cascades through every stage derived from it;
- **sand cavities are illustrative** — they draw the rammed sand and the fill glow. `capacity` carries the
  mass, which is exactly why they are separate fields.

So the rule **sizes art plausibly**; it is not a cross-check, and a voxel/mass mismatch is not a bug to
chase. `CastStockMassTests` now asserts nothing about voxels — it pins the masses, the `Forms` table and
the crop divisions in `iwex.md`, which are the things that must actually agree. The `DensityTolerance`
constant and the band tests from the first attempt are **deleted**.

⚠ **The arithmetic is still worth keeping** even though it no longer constrains anything: a 3-voxel square
section has mass `22.5 × length`, so it is a whole number of 200 u plates for **no** length. That is why
`shingledbar` (162 vx³ → 400 u) reads as 2.469 while `castbloom` and `castslab` read 2.500 — the art draws
the difference as real crop loss, with `CutRod1-4` taking 160 vx³ out of a 162 vx³ bar.

★ **A doc bug this found:** `long-cell.md` claimed the `iwex.md` ladder said "243 / 400 / 1200 **u**". That
column is **cubic voxels**; the mass column beside it reads 600 / 1000 / 3000. The three sources were never
as far apart as that section claimed. Corrected.

★★ **The whole wrought-side chamfer is withdrawn** — `shingledbar` 162 → 160 and `beam` 81 → 80 across
sixteen stage elements. An artefact of the exactness claim, not a real defect.

**U1.3 — the long-cell patterns. Done.** `castbillet` / `castbloom` / `castslab` added to `Molds`, all
`size: "longcell"`. The generator chain worked as designed: adding a mold type carried its diagram variant
and its diagram→pattern grid recipe automatically (three goldens diverged, all additively). `Box` widened
from `int` to `float` — the cavity is a `Cuboidf` and the billet lanes genuinely land on half voxels.
Lang for patterns + diagrams in en/ru/uk; three 32×32 diagram icons generated in the existing parchment
palette (⚠ **placeholder art** — procedural parchment plus an isometric silhouette, not hand-drawn).

ⓘ **`castframe` deliberately excluded.** It is an lpex machine part, lpex ships its own `pattern` itemtype,
and whether it stays at all is still open (`long-cell.md` § Open questions). iwex owning a pattern for
another mod's product is exactly the coupling the spec-on-the-pattern design exists to avoid.

**U1.4 — not blocked after all.** All seven long-cell shapes already ship (`sandcastinglongcell` + six
fillings), so the export step is a no-op, and once the cavities are understood as illustrative there is no
mass conflict to resolve. The patterns ship with the ruled capacities and the measured cavity boxes.

**Two findings from measuring the fillings, both kept:**

1. ⚠ **A long-cell lane cannot exceed 24 voxels in length.** The interior runs `z −14 … 14` with 2-thick
   end dams, so the 25- and 27-long pieces the ladder's item dimensions imply will not fit. Cheaper to know
   before drawing than to discover in Blockbench.
2. ⚠⚠ **The drawn billet lanes are 2.5 / 3 / 2.5 wide** — one pour casts a heavier centre billet than its
   neighbours, and they are the same item. A real defect; noted at the cavity boxes for whenever the
   fillings are next touched.

**2540 → 2536 tests green on 1.20 and 1.21**, zero skips. (Net −4: the density band theory cases were
deleted as testing the wrong thing.)

## 2026-08-04 — The four open decisions settled, and U0 (`ExOrientable`) executed

**The rulings.** All four decisions the completion plan tabled are now answered and recorded in its
§ *The four open decisions*:

1. **Cast-stock masses** — the `iwex.md` ladder wins (billet **600**, bloom **1000**, slab **3000**, pig
   **375**) and the density rule binds **exactly**, so the art moves to the numbers.
2. **Where "lit" lives** — ★★ *nowhere*. `FurnaceState` stops being stored state; burning is per column,
   governed by that column's raceway coke, and a column with none **hangs**. The enum survives only as a
   derived HUD label and sound trigger. This is not a new direction — `layered-charge.md` § *The state
   machine mostly dissolves* already specified it, and the plan had failed to find it.
3. **Charging temperature** — quantised world ambient, rounded to 5 °C.
4. **Half-step shape key** — hundredths.

**⚠ The lit ruling reshapes U2 and U3**, and it is much larger than the option set I offered: both
extinguish thresholds, `BfMeltStartDelay`, `BfMeltIntervalSec`, `BfMaxFuelBurnTime`, `BfHeatRatePerSecond`,
`BfCoolRatePerSecond` and the disruption floor all become emergent rather than configured.

**★★ The editable art is well ahead of every doc that describes it**, and measuring it settled three more
things the plan had open. `assets/editable/shapes/` was renamed *and substantially redrawn*; the memory
note calling the `D` + `??` wall "just a rename" is now only half true.

- **The hundredths key is the art's own convention already** — `RolledRod200`, `Grooved275`,
  `Flattened125`. Ruling 4 matches the art rather than imposing on it.
- **Every rolled stage is a named element inside one file per stock family**, not one file per stage.
  So **U7.4 needs no new art** — the products are `SelectiveElements` exports off files that exist.
- **The `Cut*` elements are the MP-shear ladder, drawn in place**, and they close an open trap:
  `NailPlate` measures 40 vx³ = 100 u = exactly half a 200 u plate, so U7's "nailplate has two routes and
  they must agree" is **already settled by the art**.
- **U8's three machines are fully drawn including `idle` and `cycle` clips** — cutter 3×2×2, nailcutter
  2×3×3, riveter 6×3×2. U8 is layouts, entities, ports and recipes only.

⚠ Two corrections from the user, both recorded: `shingledslab` is **lpex** (steam-hammer output), not
iwex's to re-mass; and the wrought-side chamfer (`shingledbar` 162→160, `beam` 81→80 per half) touches
nine and seven stage elements respectively, so it is **deferred to U7** rather than done in U1.

**U0 — `ExOrientable`: correct the record. Done.**

- **`OnPickBlock` added.** It was the one *behavioural* gap: `GetDrops` answered the canonical stack and
  `OnPickBlock` did not, so a middle-clicked west-facing block would not stack with a broken north-facing
  one and only one of them satisfied a grid recipe.
- **15 tests, from zero.** The behaviour had *no* direct coverage — the only thing pinning it was a golden
  asserting the string `"ExOrientable"` appears in a `behaviors` array, which would still pass if every
  method returned garbage. New `ExOrientableRig` fixture + `ExOrientableTests`.
- ★ **The omni vertical branch had never executed anywhere.** No block declares `mode: "omni"`, so
  `TokenFor`'s vertical arm was unreachable in production *and* untested. It runs now.
- **Two harness gaps found by using it.** `TestWorld`'s fake accessor wired neither
  `GetBlock(AssetLocation)` nor `SetBlock(int, BlockPos, ItemStack)` — the two calls a real `TryPlaceBlock`
  makes. Unwired, the first returns null, which the orientation path correctly reads as "that variant was
  never declared", so **a working block looked like an authoring mistake**. Both wired.
- **Three doc-comment paragraphs corrected**, which was the actual point of the unit: they asserted the
  network half, the layout guard and `ApplyOrientation`'s wiring as *done*. Dead code nobody calls is
  inert; a comment saying a trap is guarded when it is not is how the trap gets sprung. Each is now marked

★ One test-design note: `A_multi_segment_code_keeps_every_segment_before_the_side` is the case worth
keeping. The plan's draft only probed a single-group code, which would have passed under vanilla's broken
`CodeWithParts` too — so it would not have pinned the crash the behaviour exists to prevent.

## 2026-08-04 — Three plans merged into one: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-04-iwex-completion.md`

Docs only, no code. **2506 tests, 1.20 and 1.21 green**, unchanged.

### ⚠⚠ The finding that forced the merge: there were TWO programmes, and the stage list was one of them

`iwex-bringup.md` reads like the plan for finishing iwex. It is not. The **layered-charge rework** is a
second, larger programme — Phases 1, 2, 2.5 and 2.6 landed, **3–6 did not** — and it rewrites the furnace
most of the bring-up content hangs off. **Phase 6 is the burdenmaker, which deletes the ore mixer and the
ore bunker** — two machines Stage 0 had just made craftable.

★ Caught by the user asking "what about burdenmaker, mpenergy…?" against a plan that mentioned neither.
The first pass planned the stage list alone and would have built on a furnace about to be replaced.

### What the merge absorbed

| Source | Landed (kept as record) | Moved |
|---|---|---|
| `2026-08-01-iwex-layered-charge.md` | Phases 1 · 2 · 2.5 · 2.6 | Phases 3–6 → **U2–U5** |
| `2026-08-03-block-renames-and-codegen.md` | Stages B · C | Stage A `hearthmetal` → **U4.2**; Stage D connector check → **U10** |
| `iwex-bringup.md` | ★ still owns the **art queue** and the **playtest gates** | Stages 2–6 → **U1 · U6 · U7 · U8 · U9** |

Both superseded plans carry a banner at the top; the bring-up doc says what it still owns.

### ★★ Two merge insights that only appear once the three are side by side

* **`hearthmetal` belongs inside the hearth rework.** Rename-Stage A merges `solidifiediron` +
  `solidifiedcastiron`; layered-charge Phase 5.1 **replaces the mechanism that spawns them**. Separately
  they touch `BlockEntityShaftFurnace.cs:421`, `BlockEntityCupolaFurnace.cs:51` and `IwexCodes.HearthCell`
  twice and bless the same goldens twice. Now one unit.
* **The connector check cannot be written first.** Rename-Stage D wants `MultiblockLayoutBuilder` to refuse
  a self-orienting block — which needs `IsNetworkOriented` to be *set by something*, and no block declares
  `mode: "network"`. So D sits behind `ExOrientable`'s network half, which sits behind U4's tap work.

### Verified, not copied: what the design docs claim vs what `src/` does

Every unit was re-checked. **Six page-level claims were stale** — cold blast furnace and cupola
"unbuildable in survival", mixer/bunker/blower "survival-unobtainable", flywheel-and-shafting "not one has
a recipe", casting bed "no recipe" — all fixed. Two the other way: the **pig still ships 150 u** against a
settled 375, and puddling's B8 is only *partly* fixed (Phase 2.5 landed causes 1 and 3).

★★ **mp-energy is built, and its gap is demand rather than supply.** `IMpEnergyConsumer` has exactly one
implementation in the entire suite — `BlockEntityRollingMill`. The flywheel's whole argument stays
asserted until U8 adds three more consumers.

⓵ Also swept the `processes/` and `items/` pages, which the earlier pass had not: that is where **roasting**
turned up (designed, nothing built) — and its own page says it would live on the reheat furnace *and the
ore mixer*, which **U5 deletes**. Filed as U7.8 with the host decision reopened.

### ⚠⚠ `ExOrientable` is half built and its doc-comment says otherwise

Raised by the user mid-session. The `horizontal` route is live and correct — it is the fix that stopped
vanilla's `CodeWithParts` building `iwex:crafting-w` and crashing the client. Everything else is declared
and unreached: `mode:"omni"` and `mode:"network"` are declared by **no block**, `ApplyOrientation` is
**called by nothing**, and `OnPickBlock` is not overridden though `CanonicalStack` documents itself as
serving it.

★ **The false claims matter more than the missing code.** Three paragraphs assert guards that do not
exist — most sharply *"lets `MultiblockLayoutBuilder` refuse it at build time"*, where the builder has no
such check and the trap the paragraph describes at length is wide open. Dead code nobody calls is inert; a
comment saying a trap is guarded is how it gets sprung. **U0** corrects the record, adds `OnPickBlock` and
gives the live route its first tests (it had none); **U10** builds the rest.

### Shape of the result

11 units, U0–U10, in execution order with an explicit dependency graph. U0 and U1 are bite-sized (48
checkbox steps); U2–U10 are task-level and expand at the start of their unit — the same ruling the
layered-charge plan made, for the same reason: their steps consume interfaces the previous unit creates,
so writing them now means inventing signatures. Four open decisions are tabled against the unit that
consumes each. ⓘ Only **U1 ∥ U2** may run in parallel, and **U6 must not overlap U2/U3** — all three touch
the shared `BlockEntityFurnaceCore`.

## 2026-08-04 — `side` → letters, finished: 97 red → green, and three live bugs it was hiding

Picked the in-flight side-respelling up from a red tree (5 / 53 / 15 / 6 / 18 failing) and closed it.
**2506 tests, 1.20 and 1.21 green, zero skips.** ⚠ 1.22 still reports the **same 20** upstream
`IPlayer` `TypeLoadException` failures it did before this work — unchanged, unrelated, and not ours.
Nothing committed.

### The triage, which is most of the story

Of the 97, **three were real defects and the rest were the rename catching up.** The three:

#### ⚠⚠ 1. 32 released codes lost their path to a live block

`CodeRelocation.Remap` carries a live block's variant suffix across to the old code *unchanged* — which
is right for a rename and wrong for a **respelling**. Every live `side` is a letter now, so it emitted
`ppex:boilercornish-n`, a code that never shipped, while the 32 word-spelled codes that **did** ship
matched nothing. Silent: `BlockMigrationModSystem` drops an unresolvable pair with a `Logger.Warning`, so
a player's boiler simply stops loading.

★ Fixed with a per-row `legacySideWords` flag rather than a global rule, because **the spelling is a fact
about what that mod shipped**: ppex spelled `side` as words, and **smex spelled every facing as a letter
already** (`smex:blastfurnacetap-n`). A blanket respelling would have orphaned exactly what the smex rows
cover today. Guarded by the flag keying off the block's own `Variant["side"]`, never off "the last segment
looks like a direction" — an `orientation` group renders single letters too and has since it shipped.

#### ⚠⚠ 2. A migration went circular and would have deleted every placed intake

`CowperStoveIntakeOrientationMigration` rewrote `-n` → `-north` for the cowper intake when it stopped being
a network node. The respelling took the block back to `-n`, so the migration now claimed a **live** code as
its source and pointed at a target no block carries. Deleted, not inverted: `ReleasedCodes.Smex` lists no
`cowperstove-intake` at all, so the word form only ever existed in dev worlds and is owed nothing.

#### ⚠⚠ 3. Six recipe outputs named no block — in **both** directions

Four were left behind by the sweep (`iwex:ore-mixer-north` and the three transmission housings). Two were
the sweep **over-reaching**, exactly as predicted: `slag-brickstairs-up-n-free` and its path twin. The
stairs load their facing from vanilla's `abstract/horizontalorientation` worldproperty, so their states are
the game's words and our respelling must not reach them.

★★ **A dead output does not throw.** The recipe just never resolves, so the ore mixer — the only source of
burden — was quietly uncraftable with every test green. New `RecipeCodes` checker + `{Mod}RecipeOutputTests`
in all four content mods; mutation-proven by re-breaking the mixer. ⓘ It matches against **patterns**, not
codes: a worldproperty group has dozens of states the headless harness cannot enumerate, so comparing by
equality reported every rock-skinned canal as unregistered.

### ★ A gap the migration exposed rather than caused

`ExVariantGroup.IsHorizontalFacing` compared `FromProperties` to `"abstract/horizontalorientation"`
exactly — and the two slag stair defs spell it `"game:abstract/…"`. So the **one** word-spelled facing
group left in the suite was the only one with no `WithSide(BlockFacing)` overload, i.e. the only one a
layout author had to hand-type, in the exact spot where typing the letter everything else uses names
nothing. Matched domain-insensitively now, and `IwexCodesTests` gained the case.

### The rest: 91 expectations, and where they were wrong

* **Generated tables were stale.** `WithSide(BlockFacing)` still emitted `asLetter: false`, so every
  layout legend built through it demanded a word. Regenerating fixed ~40 and **broke 77 more** — because
  the layouts then correctly wanted letters while the fixtures still placed words. ⓘ Worth stating: a
  drop in the failure count is not progress on its own here, and a rise is not a regression.
* **The rigs convert now, the theories do not.** `FurnaceLayoutRig.SideToken` is the one place a direction
  becomes a spelling, so `[InlineData("west")]` stays readable and the code comes out `-w`. Same principle
  as `WithSide(BlockFacing)`: the caller states a direction, the machinery picks the vocabulary.
* ⚠ **Three assertions were vacuous, not merely wrong.** `FurnaceRoleCellsTests`, smex's
  `FurnaceGeometryTests` and iwex's tap-role theory built tap codes with `asLetter: false`. Those name no
  block, so `CellsAccepting` returned empty — and the assertions are **set equality against another
  possibly-empty set**. They passed by comparing two nothings until the roles diverged.
* ⚠ `BlockFacing.FromCode` returns **null** for a letter, and two fixtures fed it one the moment their
  tap-facing constants became letters. Both switched to `ExOrientation.FacingFromSide`, which is the whole
  reason that helper exists.
* **50 goldens re-blessed, scoped by path list** and diffed structurally before and after. The only
  changes are `*-north` → `*-n`, `HorizontalOrientable` → `ExOrientable`, and `loadFromProperties` giving
  way to an explicit `["n","e","s","w"]`. **No unrelated value moved** — checked by a recursive compare,
  not by reading a 234-line whitespace-shifted diff.

### Docs

`naming.md` N4 rewritten — it still claimed `side` carries vanilla's words. It now states the merged
vocabulary, **why only letters compose** (a bend is `nw`, a tee `uns`; there is no word form), why the
behaviour swap and the respelling are coupled and cannot land separately, the one surviving word-spelled
group, and the released-code split. Discovery floors raised to ~95 % of the new counts.

## 2026-08-03 — The def is the handle: `IwexCodes` all but deleted, layouts off raw strings

**2466 tests, all three game versions, zero skips.** Nothing committed.

### The question this answers

*"Can I ditch the codes files and use blocks in layouts directly?"* — counted all **89** legend references
first, because the answer turns on what they actually are: **35 are `VanillaCodes`** (the game declares
those, there is no class in our source and never will be), 6 are cross-domain alternations that are not any
one block, and **17 were raw hand-typed strings**. So no, a `Legend<TBlock>()` cannot replace the file — but
the raw literals were the real hole, and they are gone.

### 1. `ExBlockDef` owns its own rendered code

`Any`, `WithVariant(group, state)`, `QualifiedCode` and `VariantGroups` are now instance members of the def,
and **`BlockCodeEmitter` calls them** instead of recomputing the wildcard rule. ★★ That is the point: the
generated table and the definitions are now one implementation with two callers, so they cannot drift into
disagreement. The emitter used to hold its own copy of "single-state groups are baked in, the rest
wildcarded" — a decision written twice and checked nowhere.

⚠ `WithVariant` **throws** on an unknown group name. That is a typo at definition time, not a runtime
condition, and the alternative is a code that silently matches nothing.

### 2. The generated accessors take a `BlockFacing`

`WithSide(BlockFacing)` / `WithOrientation(BlockFacing)` are emitted for any group whose states really are
facings — and the emitter picks the spelling: **words** for a `side` group, **letters** for a network node's
`orientation` group (`ExOrientation.TokenOf`). Which spelling a part uses is the N4 split, a fact about the
block and not about the cell, and a layout author should not have to remember it.

⚠ Deliberately **not** emitted for an axis group (`ns|we`, `nw|ne|se|sw`): no `BlockFacing` names one of
those states, so the overload would only invite a code matching nothing.

★ That deletes `IwexCodes.HopperTall(BlockFacing)`, whose whole job was that conversion.

### 3. All 17 raw literals replaced, and `IwexCodes` is down to two constants

Every layout legend now reaches its block through the generated table. What is left in `IwexCodes` is
`ChargeShaft` and `HearthCell` — alternations, the one thing a generator genuinely cannot emit.

⚠⚠ **The canal wildcards came out too, and the reason they existed was wrong.** `IwexCodes` carried
`MoltenCanalStart`/`Straight`/`Tap` on the stated belief that "any canal start is not any single entry's
`Any`", because the canal ships one def per rock skin. It is: `Any` wildcards the skin group whatever it is
called, so the brick entry's `Any` and the cobblestone entry's are the **same string**. New
`MoltenCanalSkinsAgreeTests` pins that, because the day one skin gains a group the two diverge and the
converter's footprint silently narrows to whichever skin it names.

### ★★ What this pre-emptively fixed

The tiering impact pass (run in parallel) found **two blockers**, and both were raw literals this change had
already removed:

> `BlockPuddlingFurnaceCore` and `BlockHeatingFurnaceCore` pinned their parts as **wildcard-free literals**
> (`"iwex:furnace-puddlinghearth-north"`). Add a `tier` group to those parts and the string names no
> registered block — an exact-match demand nothing satisfies — so **the puddling and heating furnaces could
> never complete, at any rotation**.

They now read `IwexBlocks.FurnacePuddlinghearth.WithSide(BlockFacing.NORTH)`, which re-renders itself when
the grammar changes. ⚠ And the existing guard would **not** have caught it: `MultiblockCodes.Unresolvable`
compares a legend against each def's **base** code only, and every furnace part shares the base code
`furnace`, so `furnace-puddlinghearth-north` reports RESOLVED. That guard is still variant-blind.

### What the tightening exposed

`.Any` is stricter than the literals it replaced (`-*` where the hand-written form had a bare `*`), and that
surfaced **fabricated codes in the fixtures** — the same class of bug as the dead `iwex:blastfurnacetap`
found this morning:

* ~30 fixture sites stood a furnace core up as `iwex:furnace-blastcore-north` — **no tier segment**, a code
  no block has ever carried. The old one-star literal was loose enough to accept it.
* `SteelPlantScenes` placed `iwex:molten-canal-start-s`, missing the **rock-skin** segment.
* Five assertion sites compared against hand-typed anchor glyphs rather than the generated code.

All now name real blocks. ⓘ Goldens re-blessed: 4 iwex furnace cores, both boilers, smex core, cowper and
smokestack intakes, converter control — each scoped by path, verified by mtime.

---

## 2026-08-03 — Typed furnace taps + orientation-pinned tuyeres

Two layout defects of the same shape: **a legend that could not say which part belonged in a cell**. Both
were raised by the user off the rename wave. **2451 tests, all three game versions, zero skips.** Nothing
committed.

### 1. The taps split in two

`iwex:furnace-tap-{side}` became **`iwex:furnace-irontap-{side}`** and **`iwex:furnace-slagtap-{side}`** —
two `type` states on the shared furnace code, two defs off one class, exactly as `BlockChargeDoor` does its
pair. `BlockMoltenMetalTap`/`BlockEntityMoltenMetalTap` → `BlockFurnaceTap`/`BlockEntityFurnaceTap`.

★★ **What it actually fixes.** The three shaft layouts drew `T` and `S` on the *same* code and told them
apart only by `CellRole` — a workaround for there being one block, not a design. A player could build the
iron notch in the cinder notch's cell and the structure completed. Each glyph now carries its own code.

★ **The test gain is an oracle, not tidiness.** `FurnaceRoleCellsTests` could only play `CellsAccepting` off
the *union* of the two roles, and said so in a comment ("half an oracle"). It is now per-role in both iwex
and smex, so a drawing that swapped the two fails.

⚠ **Deliberately NOT done, and the reasons are in the code:**

* **The drawn shapes are not adopted.** `furnace-block-{iron,slag}tap.json` put the height difference in the
  *geometry* (iron spouts from 2/16, slag from 10/16), which only reads correctly once both taps sit at y=1.
  Today's drawings still stack them a course apart, so adopting the art alone would double-count the height.
  It belongs to the layered-charge layout move. Both types share `iwex:furnace/tap` until then.
* **The taps' facing is still wildcarded.** `layered-charge.md` reverses the tap orientation convention along
  with the shapes, so pinning it now would pin the *old* convention and have to be redone.

⚠⚠ **The migration says out loud what it cannot know.** smex shipped ONE tap blocktype for both notches, so
its code carries no record of which a given block was. Every shipped tap lands on `irontap`; a player's slag
tap comes back as an iron one. New test covers all four orientations, not just the default.

### 2. The tuyeres are orientation-pinned

The layouts passed `iwex:furnace-tuyere-*`, so **a tuyere fitted facing into the hearth completed the furnace
exactly as a correct one did**. They now pin the letter — `n` for the north wall's inlet, `s` for the south's
— which needs two glyphs (`Y`/`y`) for what is one block.

★ **The machinery was already there.** `MultiblockFacings` rotates single direction letters, not just full
side words (`ExOrientation.IsHorizontalSideWord` takes `n`/`s`/`e`/`w`); only the layouts were passing a
wildcard. And the pin is safe because a tuyere is walled in on three sides, so
`BlockNetworkNode.RecalculateAndSyncOrientations` exchanges a placed node onto the one admissible face as
soon as the brick goes up — the player cannot get it wrong and then be stuck.

⚠ **Trap found while writing the tests:** an assertion about a pinned cell must rotate its expectation. A
west-facing furnace wants `iwex:furnace-tuyere-e` in the cell drawn `n`; asking with the authored letter
finds nothing and *passes vacuously*. Both suites failed on exactly this before it was fixed.

### 3. Harness: `EXLIB_WRITE_GOLDENS` is now scopeable

★★ **The reason this was worth doing mid-task.** Re-blessing two goldens meant re-blessing the whole domain,
and the tree carries ~110 hand-checked uncommitted goldens — accepting them unread is how a drift nobody
noticed becomes the record. The variable now also takes a **comma-separated path list**:

```
EXLIB_WRITE_GOLDENS=iwex/blocktypes/furnace/blastcore  dotnet test …
```

`1` still means everything, for creating the tree from nothing. Verified by mtime that exactly the three
intended goldens were rewritten.

### Also fixed, found on the way

* ⚠ **`BlockFurnaceTap.OnPickBlock`/`GetDrops` named a dead code.** They built
  `new AssetLocation("iwex", "moltenmetaltap-north")`, which the furnace rename left pointing at nothing —
  and the `?? this` fallback swallowed it whole, so the block still dropped, merely un-normalised, and no
  test could see it. Now derived from the block's own code, normalised to `south` (what the creative entry
  and the grid recipe both produce) and carrying the type through.
* ⚠ **`BlastFurnaceTapTests` stood its tap up as `iwex:blastfurnacetap-{side}`** — a code no definition has
  produced since the tap left smex. Same trap `SmexToIwexMigrationTests` was built on.
* Two grid recipes now, and their **patterns had to differ** (`BPH` vs `B_H`): two recipes sharing a pattern
  and overlapping ingredients collide and the loser silently never resolves. The slag tap takes no iron
  plate — a cinder notch is a shallow slot with a spout.
* `blastfurnacetap-grid` keeps its cost-catalogue KEY (so an edited `ex_recipes.json` is not orphaned) and
  points at the iron tap; the slag tap is a new row.

### Docs

`layouts.md` (both sections rewritten from ⏳ to ✔, plus a new tuyere section), `naming.md` (14 furnace parts;
the pending-migration note), `layered-charge.md` (what shipped vs what is still held back, and *why* the
shapes cannot land alone), the rename plan's Stage B (marked done, with why the spelling differs from the
plan), `conventions.md` R10 (the stale "1 family of ~9"), and the cold-blast handbook page + all three lang
files.

## 2026-08-03 — Rename wave, family 8 of 9: `furnace`. **iwex is fully converted**

The last and largest iwex family, and the only one that was more than a rename. **2449 tests, all three
game versions, zero skips.** Nothing committed.

★ **A sweep of all five generated tables now finds no non-conformant iwex def** outside the 12 declared
molten-canal skin exceptions. Every remaining N1 miss is lpex/hpex/smex.

### What changed

All 13 furnace parts now share the one code **`iwex:furnace`**, with `type` naming the member — the pipe
grammar, forced by N7:

| Was | Now |
|---|---|
| `iwex:blastfurnacecore-{tier}-{side}` | `iwex:furnace-blastcore-{tier}-{side}` |
| `cupolafurnacecore` · `heatingfurnacecore` · `puddlingfurnacecore` | `furnace-cupolacore` · `-heatingcore` · `-puddlingcore` |
| `chargedoor` · `puddlingchargedoor` · `chargepile` | `furnace-chargedoor` · `-puddlingchargedoor` · `-chargepile` |
| `heatinghearth` · `puddlinghearth` · `puddlingchimneycap` | `furnace-heatinghearth` · `-puddlinghearth` · `-puddlingchimneycap` |
| `moltenmetaltap-{side}` | `furnace-tap-{side}` — ⓘ spelled for **Stage B**, which only adds `type(iron\|slag)` |
| `tuyere-tuyere-{o}` · `twintubmpblower-twintubmpblower-{o}` | `furnace-tuyere-{o}` · `furnace-twintubblower-{o}` |
| `hopper-tall` at `furnaces/hopper-tall` | **left the family** → `hopper/tall`, + the N4 fix |

Paths `furnace/{type}`, shapes moved with them. Three released migration rows re-pointed; ⚠ the tuyere row
was **narrowed** from `("blastfurnace", …)` to `("blastfurnace-tuyere", …)` — the broad form would have
walked every live `iwex:furnace-*` and invented smex sources for parts that never shipped.

### ⚠⚠ A shared def helper silently renamed **another mod's** block

`BlockFurnaceCoreBase.Core(...)` is used by iwex's four cores **and by smex's hot blast furnace**. Making it
emit the shared `furnace` code turned `smex:blastfurnacecore` into `smex:furnace` — a cross-mod rename
nobody asked for, in a family whose own naming pass has not happened yet.

★ Caught only because smex's generated code table drifted. The helper now takes the code **explicitly**
(and a nullable `type`), so smex keeps its own and gains no group. The warning is at the site: *a shared
helper must never rename another mod's block.*

### ⚠ Two more the wave had been quietly dropping

* **`blockdesc-` keys are code-derived and nothing tested them.** `blockdesc-bunker-*` was left behind by
  family 6 — three locales of ore-bunker description dead in game, every test green, because
  `LangCoverage` only checks `block-` *name* keys. Now guarded by `LangCoverage.OrphanedDescriptions` +
  a test in all four suites. ★ The asymmetry is the point: a *missing* description is legal (blocks may
  have none), so only the **stale** direction is a defect.
* **`multiblockFacings` indexes the dash segment of the code**, not the variant group — so every part that
  gained the `furnace-` segment shifted its facing index by one. Silent if missed: the layout would check
  the wrong segment for a facing.

### ⓘ A test that fired exactly as its author predicted

`ChargePileTests.The_name_key_is_bare_because_the_pile_has_no_variant_group` carried the comment *"Add a
variant group here and the two tests disagree, which is the intended alarm."* Giving the pile
`type(chargepile)` set it off on the first run. Rewritten to pin what is now true and **why the key stays
exact**: one group with one state renders exactly one code, so there is no suffix for a wildcard to cover.

### Notes

* ⚠ **A chicken-and-egg on first build**: `IwexCodes` referenced generated members that could not be
  generated until it compiled. Broke the cycle by inlining the three literals, regenerating, then
  restoring the accessors.
* ★ `IwexCodes.Tuyere` and `MoltenMetalTap` are now the generated `.Any`. Under a shared code
  `Code + "*"` is `iwex:furnace*` — it would have matched **every** furnace part in a layout legend. The
  N7 test cannot catch that, so the warning lives on `FurnaceCode` itself.
* ⓘ `solidifiediron` / `solidifiedcastiron` deliberately untouched: Stage A merges them into
  `hearthmetal-{metal}`, a design change. Renaming here would migrate a released code twice.
* Discovery floors raised to ~95 % of the new counts.

## 2026-08-03 — ★★ N7: a base code may not prefix another. Found by asking, before writing `furnace`

Scoping the furnace family surfaced a rule the convention was missing, and a **live violation I had
introduced myself two families earlier**. **2445 tests** (was 2441 — four new guards), all three game
versions, zero skips. Nothing committed.

### The question that found it

The plan wants `iwex:furnace-tuyere-{o}`, which under the pipe grammar means base code **`furnace`** +
`type(tuyere)`. But the other furnace parts would be `furnace-chargedoor`, `furnace-tap`, … — and
`IwexCodes.Tuyere` is literally `FurnaceTuyere.Code + "*"`, fed straight into the **`Legend` of all three
shaft-furnace layouts**.

⚠⚠ So `iwex:furnace*` would have accepted **a charge door in a tuyere cell**. The structure completes
wrong; every code involved is real; every block resolves; nothing fails. That is a layout silently
building the wrong machine.

### N7, and the violation already in the tree

> **No base code may be a proper prefix of another at a `-` boundary.** Within a family, *either every
> member lives in the `type` variant under one shared code, or none does.*

Probed all four mods before deciding. lpex, hpex, smex: **clean**. iwex: **2 collisions, both mine** —
family 3 had given shaft and bevel the shared code `mpenergy` while `mpenergy-flywheel` and
`mpenergy-transmission` kept their own. A textbook half-and-half family, latent until someone wrote
`mpenergy.Code + "*"`.

★★ **The fix cost nothing at the surface.** Moving the flywheel's size into a `size` group and the
transmission's gearing into `kind` frees `type` to name the member — and **every rendered code came out
byte-identical** (`mpenergy-flywheel-normal-ns`, `mpenergy-transmission-x2-north`). No golden code, lang
key, recipe, layout or test string moved; the whole change is in how the def is *authored*. Two one-line
reads followed (`Variant["type"]` → `["size"]` / `["kind"]`) and two test fixtures.

Guarded permanently by `CodePrefixCollision` + `{Iwex,Lpex,Hpex,Smex}CodePrefixTests` — one per mod,
because a family never crosses a domain.

### ⚠ What this does to the `furnace` family — decided, not yet applied

N7 forces the choice: **all 13 furnace parts share one code `iwex:furnace`, with `type` naming the
member** (`blastcore`, `cupolacore`, `heatingcore`, `puddlingcore`, `chargedoor`, `puddlingchargedoor`,
`chargepile`, `heatinghearth`, `puddlinghearth`, `puddlingchimneycap`, `tap`, `tuyere`, `twintubblower`),
paths `furnace/{type}`. That is more than a rename — ten of them have no `type` group today and must gain
one, via the shared `Core(…)` and `Door(…)` helpers.

Two consequences worth stating before the pass:

* ⚠⚠ **`IwexCodes.Tuyere` and `IwexCodes.MoltenMetalTap` must stop being `Code + "*"`** and become the
  generated `.Any` (`iwex:furnace-tuyere-*`). Under a shared code, `Code + "*"` is *actively wrong* — and
  N7's test cannot catch that, because it checks code-vs-code, not wildcard misuse. This one is on the
  reader.
* `hopper-tall` **leaves** the family for `hopper/tall`: the hopper family spans iwex's `furnaces/` and
  smex's `blastfurnace/`, so it is the one place a genuine cross-mod family beats the folder rule.

## 2026-08-03 — Rename wave, families 7 and 9: `molten` and `pipe`

Both applied. **2441 tests, all three game versions, zero skips.** Nothing committed. Eight of nine
families are now converted; only `furnace` is left in iwex.

| Was | Now | Asset path |
|---|---|---|
| `iwex:moltencanal-{type}-{skin}-{o}` | `iwex:molten-canal-{type}-{skin}-{o}` | `molten/canal/{brick,cobblestone}/{type}` · `molten/canal/tap` |
| `iwex:moltenbarrel-{construction}` | `iwex:molten-barrel-{construction}` | `molten/barrel` |
| `{iwex,lpex,hpex}:pipe-…` **(code unchanged)** | — | `pipes/*` → `pipe/*`, all three mods at once |
| `lpex:steamcondenser` **(code unchanged)** | — | `pipes/steamcondenser` → `steamcondenser` |

★ `pipe` was **path-only** — the code was already right, only the plural folder was wrong. And the steam
condenser left the pipe folder because it is not a pipe: its code is `steamcondenser`, not `pipe`, and it
declares no `type`. Shape trees moved with the blocktypes in both cases.

### ⚠⚠ The molten sweep hit the exact failure I had written down, in the two places the guard could not see

The pre-sweep enumeration predicted three protected strings (`smex:moltencanal*`, the plural
`moltencanals-*` handbook keys, `iwex:moltenbarrel-info-*`) and the sweep guarded them by count. **It
missed two more, both migration *sources*, because neither is spelled `smex:` anywhere:**

* `new AssetLocation("smex", "moltenbarrel")` — the domain is a **separate argument**;
* `("moltencanal", "molten-canal")` — a bare **tuple** in `SmexToIwexMigration.Relocated`.

Both were rewritten to the *new* code. That is the worst failure mode in the wave: the migration stays
**internally consistent, matching nothing** — every released canal and barrel orphaned, and **no test
fails**, because the only thing that knows what an old world contains is `ReleasedCodes`, whose strings
*are* `smex:`-qualified and therefore survived. The two rewritten sources just stopped agreeing with it,
quietly.

★ Caught by reading every `AssetLocation(domain, code)` and every migration table by hand immediately after
the sweep, which is now the recorded procedure. **A migration source is a historical fact, not a current
code** — said so in `BarrelConstructionMigration`'s doc-comment and beside the `Relocated` table, so the
next sweep is warned at the site rather than in a doc.

### ⚠ A generated-table ordering trap, worth knowing

`EXLIB_WRITE_BLOCKCODES=1` regenerates `{Mod}Blocks.g.cs` **after** compiling, so the iwex suite passed
green while leaving the tree unbuildable — `IwexCodes` still named `IwexBlocks.MoltenCanalbrickStart`,
which the new path had renamed to `MoltenCanalBrickStart`. **A single-suite green run is not proof the
tree builds.** Only the next full run caught it. (The fix also improved the code: the tap now reads
`IwexBlocks.MoltenCanalTap.Code` instead of borrowing the brick-start member.)

### Notes

* ⓘ **Recipe-cost catalogue keys were preserved** (`["moltencanal-start-grid"]` etc.) while their `Match`
  selectors moved — same rule as family 1. Keys are persisted player config.
* ⓘ **`cell-filling-moltenbarrel` is a shape path, not a code**, and stayed.

## 2026-08-03 — `molten` scoped and its traps written down; not applied

Stopped **before** sweeping the molten family, deliberately. It is a different size from the six behind it —
13 source files, 22 test files across **four** mods, 19 goldens, 23 lang keys — and, unlike any family so
far, its codes **shipped** (as `smex:moltencanal-*`).

★★ **Enumerating every occurrence first was worth the pass on its own: three strings look exactly like the
block code and are not.** All three now sit in `naming.md` § *Traps*:

* `smex:moltencanal-*` / `smex:moltenbarrel` / `smex:moltencanalstart-ns` — the **released** codes, i.e. the
  migration *sources*. ⚠⚠ `smex:moltencanal` contains `moltencanal` as a plain substring, so a
  search-and-replace hits it, the migration source stops matching what shipped, **every released canal is
  orphaned — and nothing fails**, because the migration stays internally consistent. This is the single
  worst failure mode in the whole wave and it is invisible to the test suite.
* `moltencanals-title` / `-text` — handbook topic keys, **plural**.
* `iwex:moltenbarrel-info-*` — block-info UI strings.

**Decided and recorded** (so the sweep is mechanical): `moltencanal` → `molten-canal`,
`moltenbarrel` → `molten-barrel`; paths `molten/canal/{brick,cobblestone}/{type}`, `molten/canal/tap`,
`molten/barrel`. The released migration row is a one-string edit in `SmexToIwexMigration`
(`("moltencanal", "moltencanal")` → `("moltencanal", "molten-canal")`), and `BarrelConstructionMigration`'s
target moves with it.

### ★★ The rule that actually decided all six families, now stated

> **The family is the asset folder, singularised.**

That is what produced `casting-sandbed`, `ore-bunker`, `forming-rollingmill`, `mpenergy-shaft` — and it is
what makes `molten`, `furnace` and `pipe` mechanical instead of a fresh argument each. It is **not** a
material and **not** a class name; that distinction is what cost the one deviation from the plan's sketch.

Three small families in one pass. **2441 tests, all three game versions, zero skips.** Nothing committed.
No migrations — none of these ever shipped.

| Was | Now | Asset path |
|---|---|---|

| `iwex:rollingmillaxle-shaft-{o}` | `iwex:forming-millaxle-{o}` | `forming/millaxle` *(was the asset root)* |
| `iwex:sandcastingbed-{brick}-{sand}-{side}` | `iwex:casting-sandbed-…` | `casting/sandbed` |

★ **The last N3 stutter outside the furnace is gone.** `rollingmill-rollingmill` → `forming-rollingmill`,

★ **`rollingmillaxle` and `castmold` were the two blocks "N1-conformant by accident"** — sitting at the
asset root with no family folder, so code and path trivially agreed. Both now have a real family, which is

### Notes

* ⚠ **The sand-casting shapes deliberately did NOT move.** `BlockSandCastingBed` carries an explicit warning
  that `iwex:sandcasting-bed` is a **pre-rework shape that must not come back** (it lacks the ten per-slot
  elements, and selective-element matching drops an unknown name *silently* — an invisible hole, not an
  exception), and `SandBedLayoutTests` walks `casting/sandcastingbed` by path. Renaming that file to
  `casting/sandbed` would put it one character from the forbidden name for no gain. **Shapes are not block
  codes and N1 does not reach them.**
* ⓘ **`SandCastingCellBrickMigration` had its target re-pointed, not removed.** It migrates a *dev*-world
  code (`iwex:sandcastingcell`, which never shipped), so under the released-only rule it would not be
  written today — but it exists, it is tested, and keeping it working cost one line.
  ⚠ **A dev world holding the post-brick-migration `iwex:sandcastingcell-fire-north` is now orphaned** and
  gets no migrator, per the released-only ruling. Stated here rather than discovered in a playtest.

  this time the two casting *recipe goldens*. The goldens caught it both times.

## 2026-08-03 — Rename wave, family 3 of N: `mpenergy`, and N1 restated so it is testable

The drive line: shaft, bevel, flywheel, transmission. **2441 tests, all three game versions, zero skips.**
Nothing committed. No migration — mpenergy is post-`791b43b` and has never shipped.

| Was | Now | Asset path |
|---|---|---|
| `iwex:castironshaft-shaft-{o}` | `iwex:mpenergy-shaft-{o}` | `mpenergy/shaft` |
| `iwex:castironbevel-bevel-{o}` | `iwex:mpenergy-bevel-{o}` | `mpenergy/bevel` |
| `iwex:flywheel-{normal\|large}-{o}` | `iwex:mpenergy-flywheel-{normal\|large}-{o}` | `mpenergy/flywheel` |
| `iwex:transmission-{x2\|x4\|clutch}-{side}` | `iwex:mpenergy-transmission-…` | `mpenergy/transmission` |

★ **Two of the five N3 stutters are gone** (`castironshaft-shaft`, `castironbevel-bevel`). Three remain —
tuyere, twin-tub blower, rolling mill — and they land with their own families.

### ⚠ Deviated from the plan's sketch, deliberately

`2026-08-03-block-renames-and-codegen.md` proposed **`iwex:castiron-bevel`** / **`castiron-shaft`**. That
was written before the convention existed, and it does not survive N2: **`castiron` covers two of the four
blocks.** The flywheel and the transmission are cast iron too and are not named for it — so `castiron` is a
material modifier, not a family. The family the convention picks is the **network**, which is what `pipe`
and `moltencanal` already do. The plan now records the supersession rather than silently disagreeing.

### ★★ N1 had to be restated before this family could be spelled

The rule as first written said *"base code with `/` for `-` == asset path"* — and **the pipes already break
that**, because `iwex:pipe` sits at `pipes/straight`. The precise, testable form:

> the asset path with `/`→`-` equals **either** the base code, **or** the base code plus one state of its
> first variant group (when that group names the member).

⚠ **Which form a block takes is forced, not chosen.** `BlockNetworkNode` keys `AllowedOrientations` by
`Variant["type"]`, so a node cannot drop the group; giving it its own code *as well* would stutter. Sharing
the family code is the only spelling left. `mpenergy` demonstrates both forms in one family: shaft and
bevel are members of `mpenergy`, while `mpenergy-flywheel` owns its code because its `type(normal|large)`
is a **size**, not a member. Same rendered codes either way — so the choice is authoring economy, not
grammar.

### Notes

* ⚠ **The golden caught a half-finished rename.** My scripted patch replaced the recipe *outputs*
  (`transmission-x2-north`) but not the two ingredient *selectors* (`transmission-x2-*`) in the same file;
  the shafting recipe golden failed on exactly those two lines. This is the second family in a row where
  the golden caught the miss — the pattern is that `-*` wildcard forms hide behind a literal search for the
  concrete code.
* Two runtime lookups moved with the codes: `BlockCastIronShaft.cs:75` and `BlockCastIronBevel.cs:127`
  build each other's code with `CodeWithPath("…-" + orientation)`. ⓘ **`CodeLiterals` cannot see these** —
  the literal is a concatenated fragment, not a `domain:code`, so the checker's regex misses it. Worth
  widening.
* Six doc citations repointed (`flywheel-and-shafting.md` ×4, `engine-watt.md`, `nail-machine.md`). Shape
  paths under `mpenergy/` were already correct and did not move.

## 2026-08-03 — The convention, written down, and family 2 of N: `crafting`

`docs/design/mechanics/naming.md` now carries N1–N6, and `conventions.md` gained **R10** pointing at it —
the debt the `slag` entry below recorded. Then the design table, which is the smallest family left.
**2441 tests, all three game versions, zero skips.** Nothing committed.

| Was | Now | Asset path | Migration |
|---|---|---|---|
| `iwex:designtable` | `iwex:crafting-designtable` | `crafting/designtable` | ✗ never shipped |

The shape moved with it (`shapes/design-table.json` → `shapes/crafting/designtable.json`), which is the
first time the wave has touched art. ★ It is verified for free: `DefinitionAssets.MissingShapes` resolves
every mod-domain shape reference against the tree, so a shape left behind is a red build, not an invisible
block in game.

### What the write-up turned up that reading the code did not

* ★★ **The inventory is now data, not memory.** The generated tables print code and asset path on one
  line, so pairing them across all five mods is a ten-line script. **Every non-slag code in the suite
  breaks N1** except `exlib:structurefiller` — and four (`castmold`, `manualfluidpump`, `rollingmillaxle`,
  `solidified*`) are conformant only *because* they sit at the asset root with no family folder, so N2 will
  break their N1 conformance before fixing it. That is recorded on the page so a future reader does not
  mistake a `✔` for "nothing to do".
* ★ **`smex:hopperbell` / `hopperreinforced` and `iwex:hopper-tall` are one family spelled two ways** —
  found by applying N5 rather than by looking. The wave will have to reconcile them across a mod boundary.
* ★ **"The orientation group is always last" already holds everywhere** — no def in any of the five mods
  declares a variant group after `side`/`orientation`. Released ppex shows it was once broken both ways in
  the *same* mod (`pipe-straight-ns-iron` vs `pipe-outlet-black-d`). Free to pin as a test; logged as Open.
* ⓘ **N1's canal exception is stronger than "a material skin".** The two canal defs declare *different
  variant group names* — `brick(fire|black|…)` against `rock(from block/rockwithdeposit)` — so they cannot
  be one def, and an asset path must be unique per def. One code, two grammars, therefore two files.

### Notes

* ⓘ **Deliberately not renamed**: `InventoryClassName` (save-persisted, and a rename orphans stored
  inventories for no gain), the GUI/handbook lang keys (`designtable-title`, `handbook-designtable-*` —
  UI strings and a topic page, neither is a block code), and `recipes/grid/designtable.json` — which
  matches the precedent family 1 set by leaving `grid/slagpath.json` alone. **Recipe file names do not
  track block codes.**
* Three docs cited `assets/iwex/shapes/design-table.json` by path and were repointed.
* ⚠ Same discipline as family 1: goldens moved and patched by hand; only the scoped
  `EXLIB_WRITE_BLOCKCODES=1` was used, never `EXLIB_WRITE_GOLDENS`.

## 2026-08-03 — Rename wave, family 1 of N: `slag`, and a convention amendment it forced

First family of the code/asset-path rename, chosen because it is self-contained (no layouts, no shapes of
its own). **2441 tests, both lanes, zero skips.** Nothing committed.

| Was | Now | Asset path | Migration |
|---|---|---|---|
| `iwex:slag` (block) | `iwex:slag-block` | `slag/block` | ✅ released as `smex:slag` |
| `iwex:slagbricks` | `iwex:slag-bricks` | `slag/bricks` | ✗ never shipped |
| `iwex:slagbrickslab` | `iwex:slag-brickslab` | `slag/brickslab` | ✗ |
| `iwex:slagbrickstairs` | `iwex:slag-brickstairs` | `slag/brickstairs` | ✗ |
| `iwex:slagpath` | `iwex:slag-path` | `slag/path` | ✗ |
| `iwex:slagpathslab` | `iwex:slag-pathslab` | `slag/pathslab` | ✗ |
| `iwex:slagpathstairs` | `iwex:slag-pathstairs` | `slag/pathstairs` | ✗ |

★ One migration for seven renames — the released-code manifest is already paying for itself. And the
`smex:slag` row was a **one-string edit** in `SmexToIwexMigration` thanks to `CodeRelocation`, which is
exactly what that helper was built for.

### ⚠⚠ N6 — a path segment may only become a folder if it is not itself a block

I drew this family as `slag/path`, `slag/path/slab`, `slag/path/stairs` first, and it was wrong in a way
that would have shipped silently.

`ExRecipeCosts` applies **every** catalogue entry in sequence (`foreach (var entry in catalogue.Values)`),
not first-match. So `iwex:slag-path-*` matching the slab and stairs means whichever entry enumerates last
overwrites the others' costs — in dictionary order, which is not a contract.

★★ **Flat names were accidentally safe.** `iwex:slagpath-*` could not match `slagpathslab-free`, because
the character after `slagpath` is `s`, not `-`. Hierarchical names remove that accident. The fix is
structural, not a patched selector: `path` is itself a block, so it must not become a folder —
`slag/pathslab`, not `slag/path/slab`.

ⓘ Vanilla agrees, for what it is worth: `game:claybricks` and `game:brickslab` are name suffixes, not
folders.

Guarded by `CostSelectorOverlap` + `IwexCostSelectorTests`, mutation-proven: re-pointing
`slagpathslab-grid` at `iwex:slag-path-*` fails with both selectors named and the colliding codes listed.

### Notes

* ⚠ **The block/item ambiguity bit me mid-patch.** `iwex:slag` is *both* a block and an item; I scripted
  `"iwex:slag"` → `"iwex:slag-block"` across the block's golden and hit its `smeltedStack`, which
  references the **item**. Caught by the golden, fixed. The item keeps its code throughout — only the
  block moved — and `SmexToIwexItemMigration` is untouched.
* Goldens moved by hand and patched by hand. ⚠ `EXLIB_WRITE_GOLDENS` rewrites a whole domain and the tree
  carries ~110 uncommitted user blessings; the scoped `EXLIB_WRITE_BLOCKCODES` was used for the generated
  table only.
* Lang keys renamed in all three locales with each wildcard's shape preserved; `LangCoverage` confirms.
* ⓘ Textures stay at `block/slag/` — texture paths are not block codes, and the derivation flagged that
  alignment as optional. Keeping it out holds the diff to blocktypes.
* ✔ **Written up** (same day): `docs/design/mechanics/naming.md` carries N1–N6 with the verified
  code-vs-path inventory, and `conventions.md` gained **R10** pointing at it.

## 2026-08-03 — Stage 2 hardening: four invariants, and four live bugs they found

The rename programme's Stage 2 — turn each of the rename's silent failure modes into a red build. Every
check found something already broken. **2440 tests, both lanes, zero skips** (was 2426). Nothing committed.

### ⚠⚠ The flywheel could not be placed, and its own comment said otherwise

`ExDefinitions.OrientationMap` recorded a def **only** when `VariantStates("type").Length == 1` and
silently skipped it otherwise. `iwex:flywheel` declares `type(normal|large)` with `orientation(ns|we)` in
one def — so its `AllowedOrientations` came back **empty**, `ComputeValidOrientations` returned `[]` for
either size, and `TryPlaceBlock` refused the block outright.

★ `BlockFlywheel.cs:37` states *"AllowedOrientations is inherited from BlockNetworkNode, which derives it
from this block's own code-first defs (type → {ns, we}) - no hand-kept table."* It did not. There is no
exception on that path, only an empty list, so nothing contradicted the comment.

Fixed at the root: **every** type state a def declares is mapped, since they share that def's single
orientation group by construction. The old test was named `..._skips_defs_without_a_single_type_state`,
which conflated *zero* with *many* and covered only zero — the gap was in the name.

★★ The rename connection: the five stuttering codes (`iwex:tuyere-tuyere-*`) invite deleting the
redundant-looking single-state `type` group. That is exactly what makes a node unplaceable. The group is
load-bearing; the **code** is what moves. `NetworkNodeContract` now makes it unrepresentable.

### ⚠ A bare block code cannot resolve once the block gains a variant group

Same defect as a bare lang key, one layer down — and `iwex:moltenbarrel` had it in **four** places since
the barrel gained `construction(bolted|cast)`:

* `BlockMoltenCanalTap.cs:73` — the barrel silently dropped out of the tap's accepted contents (the call
  site is null-guarded, so nothing failed);
* `BlockEntityMoltenCanalTap.cs:172` — the parked barrel rendered as nothing;
* `:303` — `RemoveBarrel()` built `new ItemStack(null)`;
* `IwexRecipeConfig.cs:56` — the barrel's whole recipe-cost row matched nothing and was inert.

★ Fixing the last two properly meant a real gap: the tap stored `IsBarrel` as a **bool**, so the
construction variant was lost the moment a barrel was parked and could only ever be guessed back. It now
stores `BarrelCode`, persisted, falling back to **bolted** for older saves — the same choice
`BarrelConstructionMigration` already makes. ⓘ The recipe-cost *key* is unchanged, so a player's edited
`ex_recipes.json` is not orphaned.

⚠ **`CodeLiterals` had a false positive worth recording**: `.ShapeByTypePerOrientation("lpex:manualfluidpump", 0)`
names a *shape*, not a block, and shape paths use the identical `domain:path` syntax — one can equal a
base code exactly. Asset references are distinguished by the method they are passed to, not by the
literal, so the scan filters on the call rather than the string.

### Lang coverage: the gap was exactly what the Phase 1 ledger predicted

`heatingfurnacecore` and `puddlingfurnacecore` shipped with **no name key at all** (P1, recorded and
deferred), and smex's `block-blastfurnacecore` was **bare** while its `blockdesc-` sibling carried the
star — the F1 defect, still live. Names + descriptions written for the two iwex cores in en/ru/uk; the
smex one was a single character.

★ The earlier check was deliberately narrow ("no bare key on a variant-grouped block", iwex only) and
said in its own doc-comment that it could not assert full resolution because of those two blocks.
`LangCoverage` now asserts full resolution in all five mods, so the exemption is spent rather than
inherited.

### `structurefiller` — the literal that mattered was invisible to the drift test

`ExpandedLibModSystem.cs:54` is the assignment that **wins at runtime**, and it hand-typed the path. That
code is written into the world for every footprint cell of every mega-block in all five mods, so a drift
between it and the definition would orphan every multiblock in every save. Both it and
`StructureFillers.FillerCode`'s default now read `ExlibBlocks.Structurefiller.Code`.

★ Which is also why the rename plan should leave this block alone: a mod with a single block gains
nothing from a family segment, and this is the highest-blast-radius code in the suite.

### Notes

* Four new harness checkers, each usable per-mod: `NetworkNodeContract`, `LangCoverage`, `CodeLiterals`,
  and (from Stage 0) `DefinitionCodes`. All run the real definitions rather than restating them.
* ⓘ hpex, lpex and exlib passed lang coverage first time; hpex and smex passed the literal check.
* Discovery floors raised again to ~95 % of the new counts.

## 2026-08-03 — Migration contract: what shipped is now a fact, and B22 is dead

Stage 0 + Stage 1 of the rename programme (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-03-block-renames-and-codegen.md`),
which exist so the code/asset-path rename wave has a net under it. **2426 tests, both lanes, zero skips**
(was 2405). Nothing committed.

### ★★ The lever: only three mods have ever shipped

`dist/Releases/` holds **exlib, ppex and smex** and nothing else — there has never been a released `iwex`,
`lpex` or `hpex` build. So the migration surface is **45 blocktypes / 501 concrete codes**, and *every block
added after the smex→iwex and ppex→lpex splits carries no migration debt at all*. That is what bounds the
coming rename to a knowable set instead of every block in the suite.

`scripts/gen-released-codes.py` extracts that into `test/ExpandedLib.Testing/ReleasedCodes.cs` — the
derivation checked in beside its output, so the manifest is a fact rather than a memory.

### ⚠⚠ Three released blocktypes had no migration path, and the test that covered them was fiction

`SmexToIwexMigration` matched on `Code.FirstCodePart()` against a set of **smex** base codes while walking
the **iwex** registry — which stops matching the moment iwex renames the block. It had `blastfurnace` (the
tuyere, since renamed `tuyere`), `blastfurnacetap` (since `moltenmetaltap`), and no row at all for
`blastfurnacedoor` (now `chargedoor`). **9 released codes with no path to a live block.**

★★ And `SmexToIwexMigrationTests` was green throughout because it **fabricated `iwex:blastfurnace-tuyere-n`
and `iwex:blastfurnacetap-north`** — codes no definition has ever produced. The same tautology class as B24
and the Task 2.1 epsilon test: a fixture that invents the world it needs proves only that it can invent it.

### ⚠⚠ B22 reproduced by test, then killed

`HpexExtractionMigration` enumerated the whole hpex domain and emitted `lpex:<same path>` as a legacy
source. Once `RolledPipeDefinitions` added `hpex:pipe-*` at the paths lpex already used for **cast** pipes,
it began claiming **30 live `lpex:pipe-*` blocks** and silently rewriting every placed cast pipe into a
rolled one. Its only guard (`GetBlock(oldCode) != null`) *passes* for a live block.

★ The fix that matters is the general form, not the special case: **a migration's old code must be dead —
that is what makes it a migration.** `ReleasedCodeCoverageTests.No_migration_claims_a_code_that_is_still_alive`
now fails on any migrator that declares a live source, so this class cannot return.

### One root cause behind all three migrators

Every one derived its old codes by walking the live registry and assuming the path had never changed
(`ppex:{code.Path}`, `smex:{code.Path}`, `lpex:{code.Path}`). That holds exactly until a rename.

`CodeRelocation` (exlib) is the replacement rule: **identity is named literally and frozen; variants stay
derived from the live registry.** So a new orientation needs no migration edit, and a rename moves one
string per row. ⚠ Its suffix match requires a literal `-` after the base — which is the only thing keeping
a `slag` row from swallowing `slagpath`/`slagbricks`, and `pipe-passthrough` from swallowing
`pipe-passthroughbend`. Both are pinned by test.

Dropped as dead weight while rewriting: `hopperbell`/`hopperreinforced` (they **stayed** in smex) and the
slag-paving line (added after the split, never shipped). Rows for blocks that never escaped read like
coverage and are not.

### The migrator now chains

`BuildRemapTable` resolved each pair against the world *as it collected it*, so it could only ever honour
**one hop** — and a chain's intermediate code is by definition the one that no longer registers. Every
multi-hop history (`ppex → lpex → hpex`, `smex → iwex → renamed`) silently lost its oldest worlds.

Now three passes: collect declared pairs, collect purges, then walk each source to its terminal and resolve
*that*. The walk is `FollowChain` — pure, no world and no discovery, because it decides whether a save from
three renames ago survives and must be provable without standing up five mods.

⚠ **The hop-limit test caught an off-by-one in the guard I had just written**: it tested the limit before
asking whether a next hop existed, so a chain of exactly `MaxChainHops` reported an overflow though it
terminated cleanly. Pinning *both* sides of the limit is what found it — a one-sided test would have passed.

ⓘ The BE migration used for a chained hop is the **first** hop's. Every hop in the suite copies the tree
verbatim so it makes no difference today; a hop that ever needs to reshape state must be collapsed into a
direct pair rather than relied on mid-chain. Said at the call site.

### Notes

* `DefinitionCodes` (testing harness) expands real defs into the codes they register **with their variant
  maps**. ⚠ The variants are not decoration: `PipeMigration` gates on `block.Variant["type"]`, so a world of
  code-only blocks made it skip every pipe — my first run reported 93 orphans, of which 84 were my own
  fixture lying. Supplying variants collapsed it to the 9 real ones.
* The contract test lives in `HighPressureExpanded.Tests` because it is the **only** suite referencing all
  five mods, and a migration chain crosses mods by design — the gap a released code falls through is
  precisely the seam *between* two migrators.
* `smex:blastfurnacedoor` shipped with **no variant group** and iwex's charge door has a `side`, so every
  one comes back facing north. Stated in the migration rather than discovered in play.
* Discovery floors raised to ~95 % of the new counts.
* ⚠ WORKLOG convention drift: this file says "newest first", but the 2026-08-02 and 2026-08-03 entries were
  appended at the **bottom**. This entry follows the stated convention; the strays want moving.
