## 2026-08-01 — iwex layered-charge Phase 1: B16, D5b, and the cold-furnace safety net

Executed `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-01-iwex-layered-charge.md` Phase 1 under subagent-driven development
(4 implementers, 6 reviewers, ~30 mutations). **All five suites green: 671 + 875 + 179 + 202 + 42 = 1969,
zero skips** (iwex 857 → 875). Nothing committed; the ledger at
`.superpowers/sdd/2026-08-01-iwex-layered-charge/progress.md` is the durable record.

**B16 — fixed through the multiblock system, per the user's ruling**, not by resolving the anchor.
`BlockHopperTall` gained a `side` variant group + `HorizontalOrientable`, so the layouts can demand a
correctly-facing hopper (`iwex:hopper-tall-north` for the cold furnace, `-west` for the cupola) and the drip
rotates its candidate columns by the block's **own** variant. ⚠ Prerequisite the plan had not spotted: the
hopper had *no variant group at all*, so `layouts.txt`'s draft cupola legend `iwex:hopper-tall-west*` matched
nothing and would have failed `BlockNumber` validation.

**D5b** — the taps' hard-coded `Min(20,…) × 0.6f/0.8` became `TapDrainPerTick` (50), `TapIronStackFactor`,
`TapSlagStackFactor`. Effective iron rate 12 → 31 u/tick.

**New: `ColdBlastFurnaceScenes` + `ColdBlastFurnaceScenarioTests`** — 11 cases over the real 157-cell
footprint (never forcing `StructureComplete`). This is the net Phases 2–6 get rewritten against.

★★ **Two production bugs found, both by mutation testing rather than review-by-reading:**

1. **The relight loop was destroying player salvage geometrically.** A furnace that went out with a
   still-full, still-burning shaft re-lit on the next tick and re-ran `BurnOutCharge`, so coke decayed at
   **0.4ⁿ** — measured 0.20 → 0.00512 over 120 s. It bit the `standard` 20 % burden, the grade the design
   teaches first, because a stalled furnace never freezes a pool, so the structure survives and the tick keeps
   running — all while `bf-info-burnedout` told the player to come back for that salvage.
   Cause: `BlastmixPiles.ReleaseFromFurnace` reset the burn timer but left the pile `burning`. Fixed by
   extinguishing it. Safe for the shared core: the call graph is single-path
   `ReleaseFromFurnace ← BurnOutCharge ← ExtinguishResidue ← Extinguish()`, and `Extinguish()` sets
   `State = Idle` *before* the residue runs, so no path releases a subset of piles while meaning to stay lit.
2. **Four blocks would have rendered as raw lang keys in game.** Adding the hopper's variant group orphaned
   `block-hopper-tall`; `blastfurnacecore`, `cupolafurnacecore` and `twintubmpblower` were **already** broken
   the same way. VS has no dash-stripping fallback. All four wildcarded across en/ru/uk, and a lang-coverage
   test now fails if a variant block ships a bare name key.

⚠ **A subagent destroyed uncommitted work and it had to be restored by hand.** `EXLIB_WRITE_GOLDENS=1`
regenerates the **entire domain**, not the filtered subset; an implementer noticed the overreach and
"reverted" 16 tracked goldens to HEAD — which were in fact the user's own legitimate 2026-07-30 blessings, not
stale files. Restored on the user's ruling; verified source-correct (e.g. `slagpath` moving from
`game:block/stone/gravel/phyllite` to `iwex:block/slag/slag`). Every later dispatch carried an explicit ban on
git writes and on running the golden sweep. **Scoping that flag to the test filter would remove the footgun.**

★ Tests that looked right and were not, caught by fault injection: four new orientation tests passed with B16
reintroduced (`Columns` is closed under 90° rotation, so an unrotated search visits the same cells — fixed by
planting a decoy at the cell the unrotated order reaches first); the tap test set `TapDrainPerTick` to the
shipped default so it proved nothing; and the scenario suite pinned the tap rate at **no** value — with the
rate set back to 20, all nine original cases still passed.

⚠ **Carried, not fixed** (detail and rulings in the ledger): `heatingfurnacecore`/`puddlingfurnacecore` have no
name lang key at all; the float wart (`Ceiling(50 × 0.6f)` = 31, so the shipped ratio is really 0.62/0.80 —
`Math.Round` or integer ratio before Phase 5.2, since `double` only works here by luck of representation); doc
rot including an **invalidated decision** at `machines/coke-oven.md:92-116`, which chose `hopper-tall` + a lid
over a trapdoor *because* the hopper had no orientation to get wrong.

★ **C12 downgraded.** The puddling and heating hearths still have zero behavioural tests while inheriting the
whole shared core — but both currently **cannot complete their structure at all** (B8 and its heating-furnace
analogue), so `OnProductionTick` never runs for either. Largest net gap for Phase 4; not urgent now.

⚠ **Phase 4.3 of the plan is not achievable as written.** It claims Task 1.3's suite "still passes with the
timers gone", but the rig reflects on `DisruptionMixFloor`, `ExtinguishThresholdDefault` and
`BlastMixRequiredToFire` — all of which 4.3 deletes. Phases 2, 4 and 6 will each rewrite parts of the rig.
Standing rule adopted: **any Phase-1 case edited during Phases 2–6 must be re-proved by the same mutation its
task review used, recorded in the ledger** — otherwise a rewritten case gets made green rather than re-proved.

## 2026-08-01 — Plan: the three playtest systems (docs only, no code)

`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-01-iwex-playtest-three-systems.md`. Surveyed the design table, blast furnace,
cupola and sand casting against source rather than against STATE.md, which predates Stage 0 + 1.

★ **The finding is that all three targets already ship.** Design table has block, BE, GUI, grid recipe, draft
logic and two test files; sand casting has bed + cell + 4 patterns + draftable diagrams and six test files; both
furnaces are buildable and run. What is left is **two known-open items and one coverage gap**: B16 (the hopper's
drip columns are world offsets, so a rotated cupola charges outside itself), D5b (the taps clamp at a hard-coded
20 × 0.6/0.8, i.e. 12 and 16 u/s, contradicting the one-number rule the casting bed already follows), and the
cold blast furnace having no end-to-end scenario test while the hot furnace and the cupola both do.

⚠ **First draft split the layered-charge rework into a later plan; the user reversed that** — the point of the
playtest is to test the *new* model, not the one it replaces. Rewritten as
`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-01-iwex-layered-charge.md` (the split version deleted, not left to rot).

Six phases. **Phase 1 is the safety net and is independently shippable**: the hopper gains a `side` variant and
`HorizontalOrientable` so the layout can demand a correctly-facing hopper (B16 forced through the multiblock
system, per the user, rather than resolved from the anchor); the tap's hard-coded `Min(20,…)×0.6/0.8` becomes
three config keys at a reasonable 50 u/tick; and `ColdBlastFurnaceScenarioTests` lands with nine cases —
written **before** the rework precisely so Phases 2–6 have something to be rewritten against. Phases 2–6 are the
charge pile + column segments, descent + band-order charging, the counter-current furnace, the crucible, and
the burdenmaker.

★ **A blocker in the B16 approach, found while writing it:** `BlockHopperTall` declares **no variant group at
all** — it is a single non-oriented block with `StructureAngle => 0`. So `layouts.txt`'s cupola legend
`iwex:hopper-tall-west*` matches nothing and would fail `BlockNumber` validation. Forcing orientation through
the layout therefore means giving the hopper the variant group first, which the plan does.

⚠ **Phases 2–6 are at task fidelity, not step fidelity, deliberately.** Their steps consume interfaces Phase 2
creates, so writing step-level code now would mean inventing signatures — which is what the no-placeholder rule
exists to prevent, not license. Each phase carries an explicit expansion gate naming the files to read first.

★ **The crucible-size worry is withdrawn.** The user pointed out the furnace is refilled *while firing*, so a
campaign is not bounded by one shaft-full and a 19 200 u hearth just means longer between casts — which is what
real furnaces did. `layered-charge.md` § Sizing corrected.

## 2026-08-08 - comment sweep, formatting policy, script consolidation, docs split

Large uncommitted change set. Nothing is committed; the working tree holds all of it.

**Backup:** `f:/repos/_vsexpanded-backup-2026-08-08/` holds `docs/`, `scripts/`, `.vscode/`,
`CONTRIBUTING.md`, `.editorconfig` and the workflow scripts. `docs/design/**` was untracked until this
session, so that backup is the only copy of anything lost there.

### Done and verified

- **Comments.** 703 files rewritten to a stated standard, then a second pass tightened 70 files with
  over-long blocks. 30 039 -> 22 113 comment lines. em-dash / `<b>` / marker glyphs now 0 (were
  31 / 851 / 8). Doc blocks over 12 lines: 116 -> 1. Verified comments-only: 682 files were
  byte-identical after stripping comments.
- **Formatting.** Braces moved to same-line (K&R), 2-space indent. `.editorconfig` added.
  `exmod format` runs CSharpier (wraps to `printWidth` 80) then `dotnet format` (moves braces up).
  The pair is idempotent - verified over three passes. `csharpier check` cannot be the CI gate: it
  exits 1 on the finished style, so `format -Check` compares against git instead.
- **Scripts.** 16 files -> 7. One implementation in `scripts/exmod.ps1` (runs on all platforms under
  pwsh 7); `scripts/exmod.sh` is a launcher that finds or bootstraps pwsh. The old `.ps1`/`.sh` pairs
  had drifted - the same VS Code task provisioned `1.22` on Windows and `1.22.0` on Linux. Python
  generators moved to `scripts/tools/`. Rewired: `src/Directory.Build.props`, both CI workflows,
  `.vscode/tasks.json` (472 -> 390 lines, Linux duplicates removed).
- **Docs.** `docs/design/` is design only now. Moved out: `STATE.md`, `NEXT.md`, `iwex-bringup.md`
  -> `docs/internal/plans/`; `layouts-workbench.md` -> `docs/internal/workbench/layouts.md`; code style ->
  `CONTRIBUTING.md`. Deleted the `diagram-crafting.md` redirect tombstone. 219 links rewritten;
  broken links 174 -> 1 (`docs/design/machines/puddling-furnace.md` -> `../items/fettle.md`, a page
  that was never written, pre-dating this work). `docs/design/.gitignore` removed, so the tree is now
  visible to git but **still untracked** - it needs `git add`.
- **Guard.** `test/ExpandedLib.Tests/Invariants/CommentStyleGuards.cs`, 6 tests: em-dash, HTML
  emphasis, marker glyphs, filler openers, doc block over 16 lines, over 3 `<para>`. Only mechanically
  unambiguous rules - word tests on "we" or "used to" produce false positives, since `we` is the
  west-east orientation token.
- **Tests.** 2921 green on the 1.21 lane (2915 + 6 new guards). The 1.22 lane reports 170 failures,
  all one cause and none of them ours: see the IPlayer note below.

### In flight when the session ended

Design-doc tightening across 109 pages under `docs/design/`. Progress at last check: bold markers
19 248 -> 4 732, em-dash lines 5 365 -> 2 157, total lines 34 397 -> 33 522. Agents edit in place, so
whatever finished is already on disk.

- Run ID `wf_08084cf8-a06`, script preserved at `_vsexpanded-backup-2026-08-08/workflow-scripts/docpass.js`.
- To finish the remainder, re-run that script against the pages that still carry heavy `**` markup.
- Afterwards: re-run the link check and `exmod test 1.21`.

### Vintage Story 1.22.6

`IPlayer.IsInInteractionRangeOf(BlockPos, float)` is `internal abstract`, marked
`[Obsolete("This signature will change in 1.23. Avoid it for now.")]`. `internal` on an interface
member does not hide it from implementers - the vtable slot still exists and no external assembly may
fill it, so `IPlayer` cannot be mocked or implemented at all. .NET 10 validates at type creation and
throws; .NET 8 does not, and 1.21 declares neither overload, which is why only the 1.22 lane is red.
The obsolete note covers the signature, not the accessibility: if 1.23 keeps it `internal abstract`,
this stays broken. Ask upstream for a default interface implementation (keeps it internal, fills the
slot) or plain `public` with the attribute.

### 2026-08-08 addendum - after the session reset

- **Design docs finished.** All 109 pages. Bold markers 19 248 -> 2 906, em-dash lines 5 365 -> 640,
  marker emoji 0. Total lines only 34 397 -> 33 348: the pages are dominated by tables, numbers and
  gotchas, so density changed far more than length, which is the intended outcome. Broken links: 1,
  the pre-existing `../items/fettle.md` from `machines/puddling-furnace.md` (that page was never written).
- **1.22 lane unblocked.** `scripts/tools/patch-api.cs` (a .NET 10 file-based app, Mono.Cecil) flips
  `IPlayer.IsInInteractionRangeOf(BlockPos, Single)` from internal to public in the provisioned
  `.game/<slug>/VintagestoryAPI.dll`. `exmod provision game` calls it on every path, including the
  already-provisioned fast path - which is the one CI hits when `.game` comes from cache. Idempotent;
  a no-op once the member is public, so it removes itself when upstream changes it.
  Verified: 2921 green on **both** lanes (was 2921 on 1.21 and 2751 on 1.22).
- Writing a `TestPlayer : IPlayer` was proven impossible, not merely awkward: the compiler emits
  `CS0535` (must implement) and `CS0122` (inaccessible) for the same member in one build, and the
  member carries `CheckAccessOnOverride` so Reflection.Emit is blocked too. Subclassing the concrete
  `ServerPlayer` also fails - its constructor needs a real `ServerMain` and `Entity` is a sealed
  override, so the one member the tests need cannot be intercepted.

## 2026-08-09 - furnace and machine rebalance (smex 0.9.7, ppex 0.6.7)

Built `docs/internal/plans/furnace-and-machine-rebalance.md` end to end. That plan is now a record of what was
decided, not a to-do. All 12 test targets green, 1130 tests per version.

### Furnace

Melting is a rate, not a gate. `meltSpeed = HeatFactor(hearthTemp) x airFactor`, where `airFactor` is
the share of the requested blast that actually arrived; the tuyere draw scales with the same factor,
so a hotter hearth melts faster and breathes harder in step. `BfNaturalMaxTemp` moved 1420 -> 1540, so
cold blast clears the 1482 melting point on its own and a regenerator is an improvement rather than a
requirement - the ceiling and the heating rate both interpolate over `blastTemp / BfBlastTempReference`.

Deleted: the 300 s soak, the 1200 s fuel clock, and every path that turned blast mix into slag. An
unblown lit pile now calls vanilla's own `BlockEntityCoalPile.Extinguish()` and stays blast mix. A full
reservoir and a blocked flue stall the melt instead of counting toward extinguishing.

The readout carries the melt multiplier, the ceiling next to the temperature, air drawn against air
requested, and a reservoir-full line, because with nothing extinguishing a stalled furnace is otherwise
silent.

### Converter

Ported the shape of iwex's remelt without its carbon model: a computed bath temperature
(`base + pressure gain - radiation - cold scrap`) replaces the flat 1800 `HoldTemperature`, and the blow
is gated on staying over `BessemerRefineTemperature`. Scrap has **no cap** - it is cold mass on that
balance, so the ceiling is emergent and blast pressure buys it back. Charging matches against
`BessemerScrapCodes`, a comma-separated config string, not a hardcoded code.

### Three arithmetic errors in the plan, corrected here

1. `ThroughputScale = 3f` existed on both the air blower and the engine fluid pump; the plan's
   per-engine figures ignored it. Folded into the defaults (`AirBlowerOutputPerSecond` 48 -> 300,
   `PumpWaterPerSecond` 16.67 -> 100) and the constants deleted, so a configured rate is now the
   realised rate. Same fold applied to the tap's hidden `0.6f`/`0.8` factors.
2. `ConsumeForMelting` paid the full cycle yield for a part charge. At 102 iron per cycle that is a
   duplication route - drip-feed mix, collect whole batches. Yield is now proportional to mix consumed.
3. The scrap loop itself: `patches/vanilla/metalbit.json` deleted in the same change as the yield
   raise, never after it. `MoltenUnitsPerBit` is now the single key pricing chisel recovery, furnace
   nuggets and converter scrap.

### Bugs found while sweeping for hardcoded literals

- A barrel parked under a canal tap was handed `cooldownSpeed: 300f`, **12.5x faster** than the base
  rate, while the comment beside it claimed the barrel "cools at a fixed slow rate by design". Now
  uses `BarrelCooldownCoefficient` like the standalone barrel.
- The barrel chisel was the only caller overriding `unitsPerBit` (10 against 5), silently halving what
  a player recovered.
- A cowper never cooled when idle - its tick has three branches and no `else` - so a charged stove was
  a permanent asset. It now bleeds toward ambient on every tick, and its air-side loss scales with the
  flow drawn, so an overdriven furnace drains a stove several times faster than a baseline one.
- The hopper bell mirrored vanilla's pile cap as a literal `16`; it reads `pileBe.MaxStackSize` now.

### Mold pedestal could never be chiselled (player-reported)

The block hierarchy and the block-entity hierarchy are not parallel: `BlockMoltenCanalMoldPedestal`
derives from `BlockMoltenCanalTap`, but `BlockEntityMoltenCanalMoldPedestal` derives straight from
`BlockEntityMoltenCanal`. Chaining to `base.OnBlockInteractStart` therefore landed on
`if (... is not BlockEntityMoltenCanalTap) return false` and every click did nothing, while the
interaction hint - which reads the entity - kept advertising it. Endpoints now call a shared
`TryChiselClear` directly.

The click cannot be driven headlessly (`EntityPlayer.LeftHandItemSlot` has a real getter that walks the
player's inventory), so the new tests cover the `IChiselableMolten` target on every endpoint plus the
hierarchy premise as an assertion.

### Player-reported follow-ups, same session

**Submachine readouts.** `BlockEntityEngineSubmachine` had no `GetBlockInfo` at all, so the air
blower, fluid pump and MP generator showed nothing. The base now handles "no engine" and "idle" once
and delegates to an abstract `OutputInfo(power)`. The generator's line reports shaft speed against
the engine's cap and appends a labouring note when the shaft is loaded past what the engine holds -
which is the first thing that has ever made `IsMpOverstressed` reach a player, closing the carried
regression.

**Scrap moved to the vessel hatch.** Charging scrap was on the control lever panel; it now happens on
the `(0,1,1)` hatch cell, the same filler that owns chisel-out, and advertises the configured scrap
items as its hint icons.

**Furnace projection ignored the door facing.** `BaseAngleRad` used `-1f` as its "not yet derived"
sentinel and was latched on first read. Placement adds a half turn to the door's facing, so the
derived angle is routinely negative or past a full revolution - and a negative value read as the
sentinel, so the angle was recomputed from the wrong source, or from no source at all and silently
latched to zero (north). The angle is now re-derived from the door behavior on every call and
normalised to `[0, 2pi)`, so it also tracks a door that is rotated after placement rather than
staying where the furnace was first built. Covered by `BlastFurnaceRotationTests`.

**Refractory tiers, across the furnace.** The door carried no variant group at all; the tuyere and
the molten metal tap had one for orientation but none for brick. All three drew their surround from
the tier3 texture whatever the furnace was built from. Each now has the same `refractory` group as the
cowper and smoke stack intakes, their grid recipes bind `{tier}` to the brick already named in the
ingredient, and `BlastFurnaceTierMigration` remaps existing placements to tier3.

Two traps in that: adding a group ahead of the orientation moves the code, so the tuyere's
`shapebytype` selectors needed `*-tuyere-*-s` rather than `*-tuyere-s` - the old pattern silently
matches nothing and the block renders shapeless. The taps were safe because their selectors key on the
trailing side. Both blocks' `blockNumbers` entries in the multiblock were already wildcarded; checked
rather than assumed.

`ExBlockNames.Decorate` already knew how to append a refractory tier, but only the pipe family and the
cowper intake called it, so every other tier read as the same block in the inventory. The door, tuyere,
tap, heat sink and smoke stack intake now do too. The heat sink was also drawing the `bricks` texture,
which is not a full face - switched to `front1` like the rest.

The converter keeps its fixed tier - it is the one structure that demands a specific brick - but it
was drawn in tier3 while its construction stages ask for 84 tier2 bricks. The block now overrides the
shape's texture to tier2, so it looks like what it is built from. The handbook already said tier 2.

Two defects that shipped in that pass, both mine, both reported immediately:

- **The tuyere vanished from the creative inventory.** Its `creativeinventory` selector was
  `*-tuyere-s`, the same anchoring the shape selectors used, and the new tier moved the code out from
  under it. `Every_block_variant_resolves_a_shape` in `ShippedJsonAssetTests` now walks every shipped
  blocktype's variant combinations against its `shapebytype` patterns; mutation-checked by putting the
  old selector back, which fails it.
- **Names read "(Refractory Tier 2, Refractory Tier 2)".** `BlockTuyere` derives from `BlockPipe` and
  `BlockSmokeStackIntake` from `BlockPipePassthrough`, both under `BlockNetworkNode`, which already
  decorates - so the override I added decorated a second time. Which bases decorate is not visible at
  the override site, so besides removing the two overrides, `AppendQualifier` now skips a qualifier
  already present.

### Nuggets as furnace feed, and the burden rename

**The exchange rate came out of the repo, not out of a hat.** Vanilla has no smelt route for
`crushed-iron` at all - the mod adds one itself in `patches/vanilla/crushed.json`, at the same
`smeltedRatio: 20` to an iron bloom that a nugget has. And `patches/compat/em/nugget-crushing.json`
already forces iron-group nugget crushing to 1:1. So crushing is a *step*, never a yield multiplier,
and pricing a nugget at parity would have made the pulverizer pointless. The two figures that settle
it were both already written down:

- vanilla smelts **20 nuggets** into one bloom, i.e. 5 units of iron each;
- the furnace pays **8.5 units per crushed ore** (12 -> 102).

So a batch costs 12 crushed ore *or* 20 nuggets, crushing pays back 1.67x, and a nugget charged raw
is worth what a bloomery already gives you for it. 20 is also exactly one bloom's worth, which is
what makes the number explicable rather than tuned.

The counting is the fuel side's trick reused: `BurdenValue` scales by BOTH configured amounts, so
crushed ore is worth 20 ore units, a nugget 12, and a batch 240 - exact in integers whatever the two
are set to. `TakeUnits` is now shared by carbon and ore; both spend the denser feed first. Fuel and
ore rates moved out of `BlockEntityHopperBell` into `BurdenValue` because the tooltip needs them too.

**The tooltip is a Harmony postfix on `Item.GetHeldItemInfo`, not a `CollectibleBehavior`** - the
handoff note had that wrong. Accepted feed includes crushed ores contributed by EM and
IndustrialStory, which live in *their* assets; no JSON patch of ours can reach them. One hook covers
every feed item, and vanilla's `ItemNugget` calls the base implementation, so nuggets are covered
without touching `behaviorsByType`.

**blastmix -> burden.** "Burden" is the actual term for a blast furnace's charge of ore, fuel and
flux, and it is what ru/uk already said (шихта) - so the rename brings English into line with the
translations rather than the other way round. Swept across identifiers, the item code, asset
filenames, lang keys, handbook prose (all three languages) and the HTML sources.

Three things the rename needed beyond a find-and-replace:

- `BurdenRenameMigration : IItemCodeMigration` converts existing `smex:blastmix` stacks. Coal piles
  are `IBlockEntityContainer`, so piles standing in a furnace are covered by the chunk sweep.
- The persisted tree keys changed, so `burdenMagazine` falls back to `blastMixMagazine` and
  `burdenBurnTimer` to `blastmixBurnTimer`. Without it a stocked magazine and a burning pile's timer
  would silently reset.
- The config keys renamed with the properties; a value saved under an old name is dropped and takes
  its default. Noted in the 0.9.7 migration comment rather than given a rename table - 0.9.7 already
  resets the whole rebalanced set.

The vanilla `coalpile.json` texture key is keyed on the *content item code*, so it had to move from
`/textures/blastmix` to `/textures/burden` or every pile of burden would have drawn untextured.

+20 tests (`BurdenOreTests`), mutation-checked by stubbing `IsIronNugget` to false - 5 fail. 12/12
targets green at 171/589/160/297.

### Playtest round - blower curve, ignition air, and two defects under them

**The twin-tub blower is nonlinear now, and that was the user's call.** They measured 183.8 L/s
geared and ~40 L/s ungeared and said the geared build should be the default. Those two numbers do
not admit a re-rating: output was strictly proportional to shaft speed, so the spread is whatever
the gear train is, and "3x less ungeared" cannot be had by moving a constant. A saturating hyperbola
can, and it is physically the right shape - a bellows driven hard cannot refill between strokes:

    output = MpBlowerMaxLitres * speed / (speed + MpBlowerHalfOutputSpeed)

**⛔ The measurement that motivated it was taken through a bug.** `BEBehaviorMPFillerPort.Speed`
returned `Math.Abs(Network.Speed)` with no `GearedRatio` - vanilla's `BEBehaviorMPConsumer.TrueSpeed`
applies it, this repo's own `BlockEntityConverterControl` applies it, and the port's `AngleRad`
already did. The network's speed is held in the frame of whichever node seeded it, so a machine
behind a gear train read the drive's speed or its own depending on chunk load order. Fixed in exlib;
it affects the blower, the engine water pump and the MP generator alike.

With that fixed the gear ratio is deterministic: **5.5**, read out of `BEBehaviorMPLargeGear3m.ratio`
in the shipped assembly rather than inferred. Re-solving for 20 L/s ungeared (speed 0.29) and 60 L/s
geared (0.29 x 5.5 = 1.6) gives `MpBlowerMaxLitres` **110** and `MpBlowerHalfOutputSpeed` **1.3** -
ratio 3.01. Worth re-measuring in game, since the old reading came through the broken path.

**The furnace now breathes while being lit.** Air was drawn only once `State != Idle`, which needs
the *whole* hearth burning - so the lighting phase was free. Air draw is now gated on the hearth
being alight, and an unblown one goes out on `BfUnblownIgnitionSeconds` (10 s).

**Blast pressure became a requirement.** `!receivingBlast` is now a disruption, so losing the blast
puts a lit furnace out on the 30 s grace. This reverses the rebalance ruling that an unblown furnace
stalls forever; the grace is what keeps a cowper swap legal, and there is now a test on each side of
that. `No_blast_at_all_stops_the_melt_without_ending_the_campaign` was the old ruling written down,
and was rewritten rather than deleted.

**A second defect, found by the nugget workflow:** a batch mixing crushed ore and nuggets over-spent
up to 8 ore units, because 12 does not divide 20 and the last nugget cannot be split. `TakeUnits` now
returns the overshoot and both feeds bank it against the next batch. ⛔ The first test I wrote for
this did not discriminate - 3 crushed spread over 3 batches happens to divide evenly. The mutation
check caught it; the real case needs *one* crushed ore per batch, re-stocked between ticks.

**On the nugget tooltip: 5.1 was not a bug, and the user changed the design anyway.** Verified end
to end, including decompiling vanilla's `ItemNugget.GetHeldItemInfo` - it prints "5 units of iron"
from `smeltedRatio: 20`, so our line sat one row from a nearly identical number, which is what read
as wrong. Both realised rates matched their tooltips exactly.

The ruling: **a nugget is worth exactly what a piece of crushed ore is.** `HopperNuggetRequired` 20
-> **12**, so 12 of either feed makes a batch of 144 ore units and both price at 8.5 units of iron.
I flagged that this makes the pulverizer optional on the furnace route - crushing is 1:1 by the
mod's own patch, and both items stack to 128, so there is no yield or hauling advantage left - and
the user confirmed. Bloomery parity and the 1.67x crushing payback are both retired.

⛔ Two tests had the old asymmetry baked in: `A_nugget_is_worth_less_than_a_piece_of_crushed_ore`
(now inverted) and the short-feed theory, whose "one nugget short" rows were only short at the old
rate. And the mixed-feed banking test went **vacuous** at parity - equal rates never leave a
remainder - so it now drives a retuned config where they do not divide, which is the case the
banking exists for. Re-checked by mutation.

### Playtest round 2 - air demand, exhaust, and the readout

**Nuggets are worth what crushed ore is.** `HopperNuggetRequired` 20 -> 12, so 12 of either makes a
batch of 144 ore units and both price at 8.5. User's call, made after I flagged that crushing becomes
optional on this route - it is 1:1 by the mod's own patch and both items stack to 128, so no yield or
hauling edge is left. The mixed-feed banking test went VACUOUS at parity (equal rates never leave a
remainder), so it now drives a retuned config where they do not divide.

**Air demand is a ramp with a rated top.** `AirDemandFactor` climbs from `BfAirDemandIgnition` (0.25)
at ignition to 1.0 at the melting point, then `max(1, HeatFactor)` above it. Two rounds of getting
this wrong are worth recording:

1. First I made it flat `max(1, heatFactor)` everywhere. The user corrected me - a lower demand while
   firing was deliberate and good; it just never visibly rose.
2. Then I ramped it to the melt model's own floor, which tops out at 20 L/s. Wrong again: a melting
   hearth wants every tuyere's RATED volume, so the top of the ramp is 40, not 20.

⛔ The root cause of the whole confusion: `HeatFactor` is clamped at its floor for everything below
~1525 C - the entire firing phase - so reading it directly for demand gave a figure that genuinely
never moved. Any "make it dynamic" request against a clamped function needs the clamp removed, not
the display changed.

**⛔⛔ The furnace vented DOUBLE what it breathed.** `exhaustVolume` was computed from the total air
drawn and then pushed into EACH gas outlet, so a two-outlet furnace made 160 L/s of flue gas against
a 96 L/s stack. No correctly sized smoke stack could ever have kept up. The total is now shared
across the outlets. Peak is 80 against 96, pinned two ways: a config-arithmetic invariant and an
end-to-end run at the melt ceiling.

**⛔⛔ A readout field that never reached the client.** `_blastPressure` was added without a
`ToTreeAttributes` entry. `GetBlockInfo` runs CLIENT-side off the synced tree, so it read 0.00 atm on
a furnace that was visibly melting - indistinguishable from a dead blast main. Every other readout
field was synced; this one was not. `Every_field_the_readout_prints_reaches_the_client` now sets a
distinct value into all ten and round-trips the tree.

**A regression I shipped and removed inside one session.** I tried to show "supplied of needed" using
`PipeNetwork.State.Volume`, which is the gas STORED in the main - 780 L on a long run at 2 atm -
rendered through `ExMeasure.FlowRate` as "780 L/s". The user caught it in game. There is no honest
per-second number that shows headroom: the furnace draws only what it asks for and the blower is
pressure-capped, so realised throughput always equals demand. Headroom lives in the PRESSURE line.
Field, sync, per-tick network lookups and dedupe set all removed - no dead state left.

⛔ Note for the next readout change: a volume formatted as a rate passes every test in the suite. The
tests pin that fields sync and that the model is right; nothing pins that a quantity is printed in
its own units, and nothing will until units leave `float`.

**Final block info**, confirmed correct in game: `Temp:` with no ceiling (it is dynamic), then
`Blast input: {drawn} of {needed}` and `Blast pressure: {at tuyeres} (min {gate})` as separate lines.
The converter got the same pressure line - its 2.5 atm gate is the hardest requirement in the mod.

**ru/uk prose.** All 10 smex handbook articles audited; 8 accepted first pass, 2 rejected for real
regressions (a dropped "not necessarily" hedge, a pronoun pointing at the mold rather than the metal,
`по <hk>` where the file says `через`), then both accepted on retry.

### Waterwheel drives everything at the wrong speed after a reload

Player report: since the water wheel arrived, everything on its network turns slower after a world
reload, and breaking and replacing any axle restores it until the next load.

**`BEBehaviorMPWaterWheel.CheckWater` overwrites the wheel's own `GearedRatio` with a hardcoded 1.**
A network's `Speed` is one figure held in the frame of whichever node seeded the network; each node's
`GearedRatio` converts it to that node's own shaft speed. The wheel's water check calls
`SetPropagationDirection(new MechPowerPath(..., 1, Pos, false))` whenever the sensed flow direction
changes, and that assigns the ratio along with the direction (`BEBehaviorMPWaterWheel.cs:217`, the
only runtime call in vanilla that hardcodes a ratio outside network creation - the windmill has no
equivalent, which is why this arrived with the water wheel).

The sensed direction lives in a `float dir` field that is never written to the tree, so after every
load it starts at 0 and the first check always counts as a change. Consequences line up with the
report exactly: reload-only, because `dir` is only ever 0 at load; cured by replacing an axle,
because `RebuildNetwork` re-derives every ratio and `dir` is non-zero by then; and uniform across the
network, because one node's ratio being wrong moves where the whole network settles.

It only bites when the wheel is not the node that seeded the network - otherwise its true ratio is 1
and the overwrite is a no-op. **Our machines are seeds**: every MP behaviour we ship sets
`OutFacingForNetworkDiscovery`, so it discovers its own network at load, unlike vanilla's
`BEBehaviorMPConsumer`. Whichever block entity initialises first anchors the frame, so the corruption
is a load-order race. The error factor is the gearing between the wheel and the anchor: 5.5 across a
large gear, nothing at all with no gearing in the chain.

Fixed in `ppex/Patches/WaterWheelGearedRatioPatch.cs` - a prefix/postfix pair around `CheckWater`
that holds `GearedRatio` steady. The direction change is wanted; only the ratio is restored. Guarded
`#if GAME_GE_1_22` and skipped via `Prepare()` when the method cannot be resolved, so a rename in a
later version is a skipped patch rather than a Harmony exception during mod load.

**`BEBehaviorMpPumpDrive.DriveSpeed` read `Network.Speed` raw** - the same defect already fixed in
`BEBehaviorMPFillerPort`, still present in the MP fluid pump, so a geared pump's stroke rate depended
on which node seeded the network and therefore changed between loads. Now geared through
`GearedRatio`, with `Drive_speed_is_geared_into_this_shafts_own_frame` pinning it (mutation-checked).

### Converter: scrap was free, and the readout said otherwise

Player report: the fill status counted only the molten charge though scrap shares the volume; the
block info was bloated; and 620 units of scrap showed a ~600 C heat cost - did that mean the bath
would drop 600 C?

**It meant nothing at all.** `BessemerColdScrapLossCoefficient` appeared in exactly two places, both
of them block-info strings. `ProcessTemperature` was `static` and read pressure alone:

    return BessemerBaseTemperature + overGate * BessemerPressureTempGain - BessemerRadiationLoss;

So scrap was free steel at any pressure, with no cap - while the config's own doc comment described
the trade in detail ("scrap is pure cold mass on the heat balance, and the point where it drags the
bath under BessemerRefineTemperature is the limit") and the readout quoted the player a figure the
simulation ignored. Now an instance method with the scrap term in it.

**Rebalanced against two anchors the player set**: about 15% of the vessel on the minimum blast,
rising to 40% at the top of what a plant can actually blow. The blast bonus is now a **saturating
curve** rather than a flat 100 C per atm, so the allowance tops out at a holdable pressure instead of
climbing until it would want a line no pipe survives - the same shape the twin-tub blower's output
uses.

    bath = 1850 + 580*x/(x + 0.7) - 50 - 0.8*scrap,  x = atm over the 2.5 gate, floor 1500

The ceiling anchor is not arbitrary: a **Cornish engine breaks above 8 atm** inlet and the air blower
delivers `inlet * SteamEngineEfficiency (0.75)`, so **6 atm is the hardest blast a plant can hold**,
and that is exactly where 40% lands. Between the anchors: a full stack of 128 bits is 640 units, 27%
of capacity, and needs about 3 atm. Half a vessel of scrap is unreachable at any pressure - the
asymptote sits near 46%.

**Air and blow speed now stop at the same pressure.** `ConversionSpeed` reduces to
`clamp(0.5 + (P - 2.5), 0.5, 2.0)` - it hit its ceiling at **4 atm**, so once the heat curve reached
out to 6, every atmosphere above 4 bought scrap tolerance for **no extra air and no slower blow**.
`BessemerPressureReference` 1.5 -> 3.5 puts both ceilings at 6 atm. The band now reads 12 L/s at the
gate, 27 at 4 atm, 48 at 6 - so the hardest blast costs four times the air of the softest, which is
what pays for the tolerance. Mid-tier is the cost: a 4 atm plant blows at 1.14x where it used to get
the full 2.0x. `BessemerSpeedMin` is the softener if that reads too harsh in play.

**Scrap yield loss removed.** `BessemerScrapSteelYield` (0.97) is gone and scrap returns unit for
unit; material loss on a remelt belongs to the ironworks mod's design, not this one. The handbook
already said "it comes out as steel with the rest", which is now simply true.

`BessemerPressureTempGain` is replaced by `BessemerPressureTempGainMax` + `...Half`; all four
converter keys are in the 0.9.7 migration reset list, since they were never live figures before. The
handbook's "every atmosphere over the gate buys back another 100 C" sentence is rewritten in all
three languages.

**Scrap charging took the whole hotbar, and forgot what it took.** `TryChargeScrap` gated on the
held stack but then called `ExInventory.TakeHotbar(byPlayer, IsScrap, roomBits)`, which sweeps every
matching slot - so charging iron swallowed the player's steel bits too, and there was no way to
charge one kind at a time. It now takes from the active slot alone.

`_scrapUnits` is replaced by a **per-item-code ledger**, so a vessel broken or chiselled mid-charge
returns iron bits as iron and steel bits as steel instead of one lump of whatever the molten charge
was. Keyed by code rather than two fields, so `BessemerScrapCodes` stays the thing that decides what
counts as scrap. Serialised as a sub-tree; a save written before this reads its old `scrapUnits` as
iron, which is what it must have been - steel scrap only became chargeable in the same change.

⛔ Found on the way: `OnConverterBroken` dropped unmelted scrap only `if (_content != null)`, so
**scrap charged into an empty vessel was destroyed on break** - and charging before the iron is the
order the design intends. The new drop path has no such condition.

**⛔⛔ The blast pressure line read 0 atm with 2.6 atm standing at the intake** - the same defect the
furnace had one round earlier, in the machine that copied its readout. `AppendStructureState` called
`BlastPressure()` live, and that walks the pipe network, which exists only on the server;
`GetBlockInfo` runs client-side. Now sampled in the production tick into `_blastPressure` and synced,
alongside `_airDrawn` / `_airDemand` for the throughput line the panel had no equivalent of.
`Every_field_the_readout_prints_reaches_the_client` is now on the converter too - the furnace has had
it since the same bug there, and the converter shipped without it.

The blow-rate line is gone, replaced by `Blast input: {drawn} of {needed}`: the draw is the rate times
a constant, so the two lines were one fact printed twice, and the air figure is the one that tells a
player whether their blower keeps up.

`ReflectionHelpers.Invoke` now finds static methods too (null receiver) - a rule expressed as a
static helper is still that type's rule, and a test should not have to make it an instance method to
reach it.

**Readout merged to one charge line**, and the fill figure now counts scrap and molten metal
together, since they share the vessel:

    Charge: 60/100, 1450 °C - 40 iron + 20 scrap (-6 °C)

Three forms - molten only, molten + scrap, and scrap-only (scrap is charged into an empty vessel
before the iron, so it can be all there is). Duplication removed with it: the status line no longer
repeats the fill figure ("Filling", not "Filling… 40/100"), the blow line no longer repeats the bath
temperature (`HoldTemperature` keeps it equal to the charge's), the scrap status no longer repeats
the amount, and the too-cold status now carries only the threshold. Five lines down to three.

### Cowper stoves discharged at main length, not at their rated rate

Player report: stoves discharge abnormally fast; suspected the passive heat loss.

Not the passive loss - measured, that is 0.73 C/s on a full 1240 C stove against the 1.46 C/s the
discharge itself is rated for. The fault was the discharge term:

    float flowShare = _intakeVolume > 0f ? airVol / _intakeVolume : 1f;

`airVol` comes from `passthrough.Volume`, which is the gas **standing in the whole cold run**, not a
per-tick flow. A modest 8-pipe main at 2 atm holds ~480 L against a 24 L intake, so `flowShare` ran
about 20 and the core lost ~37 C/s: 1240 C to 600 C in half a minute rather than seven minutes. The
discharge rate was set by how long the player's blast main was, which is backwards.

Now scaled by what the stove can actually draw, `min(passthroughVol, _intakeVolume)`, so the share
sits in (0, 1] - a trickle still costs proportionally less, which was the point of the term.

Same units confusion as the `780 L/s` readout last round: a stored volume used as a rate. Third
instance this release. Nothing in the type system separates them while both are `float`.

⛔ **Left alone, needs a balance decision.** The delivery side has the same conflation and it is
load-bearing: the stove produces `airVol` (up to the whole standing volume) into the hot main but
consumes only `_intakeVolume` from the cold one, so it creates gas, bounded only by the hot main's
ceiling. That is what lets one 24 L/tick stove satisfy a furnace drawing 80 L/s at melting. Making
the mass balance honest caps hot-blast throughput at the stove's intake and would starve the furnace
unless the intake or the stove count changes - a rebalance, not a bug fix.

### Engines sprayed water at a correctly plumbed outlet

Player report: water particles at the engine's water-out face even with a pipe connected.

`OutputCondensate` spilled on any `TryProduceLiquid` returning false, and that call returns false for
**brim-full** as well as for unplumbed. A closed water loop holds its main full - a pressurised main
is full by definition - so a correctly closed loop sprayed continuously. The behaviour contradicted
the comment above it, which already said the spill was for condensate with "nowhere to go".

Now `OutletSpills` decides: spray only when the face is unplumbed, or the line there carries a medium
water cannot join (a real plumbing mistake, worth showing). A full water line backs up quietly and
the condensate is lost.

Worth noting how it survived: no fixture wired anything to the water-outlet face, so the whole outlet
half of the water loop had no cover at all. `A_running_engine_sends_its_condensate_into_a_plumbed_outlet`
now pins the positive path - it passed unchanged, which is what ruled out the connector lookup and
left the full-line case as the only explanation.

**ppex uk handbook prose.** All 5 articles rewritten and accepted. Mechanically re-checked before
applying: numbers against en, tag counts, `handbooksearch://` targets unchanged, no en/em dashes, no
`по <hk>`. ru is still untouched.

### Still open

- Air blower measured 72 L/s on a Watt against the 90 the rate implies, i.e. `AvailablePower` was
  about 0.24 rather than 0.3. Most likely steam-limited rather than a rate error; worth re-measuring
  with the boiler holding nominal pressure.

### Release-readiness sweep, 0.9.7

A five-lens audit of the release turned up one ship-stopper and a list of smaller things. The
stopper: `smex 0.9.7` declared `ppex: 0.6.6` while ppex shipped 0.6.7, and Vintage Story treats a
modinfo dependency as a **minimum, not a pin** - so the pair would pass the game's check and then
fail to load. Fixed by the author.

**⛔ Text that documented behaviour the code does not have** - three of these, all player-facing:
the handbook sent players to the *control block* to charge scrap when only the vessel's upper hatch
takes it; it promised a five-minute blow when the blast now sets the pace (ten minutes at the gate,
two and a half at 6 atm - a consequence of moving `BessemerPressureReference` to 3.5 and not
re-reading the article); and the Engines article recommended a Cornish boiler straight onto a Watt
engine, which bursts it, since the boiler holds 5 atm and the engine wears above 4.

**⛔ A temperature DELTA rendered through the absolute converter.** `ExMeasure.Temperature` applies
`c * 9/5 + 32`; the converter's scrap heat cost is a number of degrees lost, not a temperature, so
imperial players read every penalty 32 °F too high. New `ExMeasure.TemperatureDelta` scales without
the offset. Anything that formats a *difference* in degrees needs it.

**A pipe carrying its whole load reported "Empty"** (from a player comment on the mod DB, still
open against the published build). The throughput line was gated on the medium label, and a run
drained as fast as it is fed holds nothing and loses that label - so the pipes working hardest were
the ones that looked dead. A furnace tuyere in balance with its blower was the reported case.

Also swept: the hot-blast ceiling was stated as 1740 in one paragraph and 1700 in another;
`_exhaustVented` reads as dead code but is the observation point for the one-stack-clears-one-furnace
invariant, so it is documented rather than removed; `_processTemp`/`_convSpeed` are no longer synced
now that nothing prints them; the README no longer calls `docs/*/handbook/*.html` "sources", since
they have drifted from the shipped lang text and would regress it if re-synced; draw.io's `.$*.bkp`
lock files are ignored.

⛔ Two lessons for the next release. **Moving a balance constant invalidates prose**, and nothing
checks that - the blow-time and scrap-tolerance sentences were wrong within minutes of the config
change. And **an audit agent will write into the working tree**: one left a scratch probe test in
`test/Integration.Tests/Scenarios/`, which would have been easy to commit.
